# Decisions

Why things were decided, so they aren't reversed by accident. Newest first.

## 2026-11-06: A homing teleport goes to a target and strikes it; one-off homing picks are random (owner's decision: a flicker strike)

- **Before:** a homing teleport bent its way toward a target in a cone and blinked its card's distance (so a farther target was not reached), measured from the staff tip it was cast from rather than the body it moves, so its landing strike often missed (`homing · teleport` did 0 damage and `on hit` chains never fired). It avoided the target it landed at last (`_landed_at`, `Homing.avoid`). Its landing hit's payload was cast by the point.
- **Now:** a homing teleport picks a target within its homing reach and blinks to it, however far, landing with the body's centre at the target's edge (`LAND_CLEARANCE` short), measured from the body it moves (`CarrierMove`: its anchor), so its landing strike reaches it; with nothing in reach it teleports in place (owner, 2026-11-06: a blink of 0, so the cast, its landing strike and its payloads still happen where it stands). A teleport without homing is unchanged. Its landing hit's payload (and a dash's pass hits') is cast by what it moved (`Carrier.hit_caster`, `payload_cast_by`), so `on hit · homing · teleport · on hit · homing · teleport · homing · teleport` hops from enemy to enemy, each blink cast by the wizard's body where it landed.
- **One-off homing picks are "a random enemy in range" (owner: "no special rules about hitting the same enemy twice etc"):** `Spells.pick_target` picks any enemy within the homing reach, any way round, at random, rolled afresh for each pick (`_picks`); it may pick the same one again. A teleport's target and a rain's spot use it. `_landed_at`, `Homing.avoid`, `Homing.aim`, `Homing.target_radius` and `homing_point`'s `avoid` are gone.
- **Unchanged:** a flying shot's steering (`home_velocity`, the manager's homing) keeps its target and its "not just hit" rule, so it does not wobble; a beam's legs keep their target while the beam lasts (a beam bends every step, so it is steering too); a dash steers as it goes.

## 2026-11-06: A row hits as the launch that made it (owner: "can we fix this more elegantly?")

- **Before:** a held beam's later ticks, a swing's or slam's later sweep, a dash's pass, a shot's zaps and a teleport's landing hit in their carrier's own steps, when no caster is set, so a pull (or a swap's zap) on those hits moved the wizard even when a decoy, or a shot, had cast them. A chain jump from a shot's hit was registered with whatever caster was current when the hit came, the wizard's own, so a decoy's or a payload's jump returned to, and pulled, the wizard.
- **Now:** everything a launch makes remembers who cast it. A row keeps the caster and seq it was launched with (the beam, swing and slam already did; a dash's pass now does), and each step of it runs between `CarrierKit.cast_as(caster, seq)` and `cast_done()`: one pair, at each carrier's step loop (and a beam's zap, a shot's zap, a move's landing), on one stack in `SpellRuntime` (`_cast_as`, `_cast_done`; scratch, no allocation once grown). The four places that swapped the caster by hand (`_launch_as`, `_penta`, `_run_pending`, a waiting fire in `_fire`) use the same pair.
- **A chain jump belongs to the shot it jumped from (owner, same day):** the handling of a hit (`Spells._on_hit`) runs between the same pair, as that shot's caster (`shot_caster`; a ray's hit as the caster already casting it), so the jump shots it makes are cast by what cast that shot: they return to it, and their hits pull it. A jump of a ray waits in the jump queue with that caster (`_jq_caster`, `_jq_caster_seq`) and is cast by it, or by the point once a shot that cast it has ended (`_live_caster`). A payload the hit fires is still cast as `payload_cast_by` says (that shot while it flies on, else the point): it passes its own caster. No combo in the catalogue changes: every chain in it is the wizard's own cast, whose jumps were and are the wizard's.
- **Unchanged:** a payload a row's hit fires is still cast by the point where it hit (`Carrier.payload_cast_by`); only what reads the caster on the hit itself (pull, a swap's zap) changed.

## 2026-11-06: A decoy is a regular actor; a body that fires a payload casts it (owner's decision)

- **Before:** a decoy guarded itself with a guard of its own (`CarrierDecoy.guard_decoy`, every step, Entities' `_shield`/`_deflect` and `DECOY_GUARD`): a shield stopped shots within 16 px while its charges lasted, a deflect turned them for its whole life. Cards a payload read with no carrier after them always landed on a point, so a shield or a deflect in any payload was wasted.
- **Now, the decoy:** a regular actor. Its shield is a Combat barrier on its body for its life, through the caster's own path (`CasterMoves.shield_caster` with the decoy's actor id, in `CarrierDecoy._body_made` once Entities has put it in the world; a decoy placed again is a new actor in Combat, so no barrier carries over). Its deflect turns the shots round it once, as it appears, as a caster's deflect does (`CarrierKit.turn_shots_round`, shared with the caster and the teleport's landing). Its barrier's blocks set off `on block` (`Spells._on_shield_blocked` fires in the card caster's name). The decoy is no longer `GUARDS`, so a shield beside a deflect is not wasted on it.
- **Now, the wider rule:** leftover payload cards land on whatever fired the payload: a body (a decoy's; the wizard's for a dash's timer, `on hurt` or end, an `on cast`, a barrier's block), a live shot (a bolt's timer, a piercing bolt's hit), or a point. The compiler knows from the carriers' yes/no answers (`Carrier.payload_cast_by`, new flags `CASTS_FROM_SHOT`, `CASTS_FROM_BODY`) and lands them on a third fallback carrier, `bearer`, where a body or a shot may cast the payload, else on `point`; a card is wasted only when nothing that may cast it could use it. On a body a shield is a barrier and a deflect turns the shots round it once; on a shot a shield adds charges and a deflect makes it deflect; the strike is round it either way. Its actor stays the wizard. Recoil is now wasted on a point (`CASTERLESS`): nothing there to push.
- **Guard cards strike nothing (owner's choice A):** a fallback's strike hit the body its cards landed on, so a lone Shield or Deflect cost the wizard 4 health a press, and `timer · decoy · shield` killed its own decoy in 2 s. A fallback now strikes only when a card on it uses the strike (an infusion, or a card that needs more than shots to meet: `CarrierFallback.uses_strike`); a lone infusion still strikes its caster (self-harm stays on).
- **The body that fires a payload casts all of it (owner, same day; this replaces "only the left-over cards land on the body"):** a payload fired from a body (a decoy's own, or the wizard's for a dash's timer, `on hurt` or end, a barrier's block) is cast by that body the way a staff's own cast is cast by the wizard's. A dash, teleport, pull or swap in a decoy's payload moves the decoy, not the wizard (rules R4c's "a move cast by a point moves the wizard" still holds for a true point: a rain's spot, a familiar's orb, a shot that ended); an orbit or familiar circles it and follows it, a returning shot comes back to it, a beam, swing or slam is held by it (along its aim), recoil pushes it. An orbit in a dash's timer circles the wizard, not a spot on the dash's path. The actor stays the wizard: credit, budgets, colours, tag.
- **One caster value:** `SpellRuntime._caster` is the actor's own body (CASTER_BODY), another actor's body (`body_caster(id)`, CASTER_BODIES - id), a live shot's slot (with `_caster_seq`), or a point; the kit's `caster()` is the one value every carrier asks (`SpellRuntime.body_of`, `caster_body`, `caster_shot`, `caster_place` read it), and `CasterMoves.moved_by` is the one rule for what a move moves. A payload's caster keeps its build as a shot in the air does; only the actor's own cast (CASTER_BODY) is dropped by a rebuild. Shots cast by another body (a decoy's orbit) are a point to the projectile manager that Spells moves with that body every step (`_follow_body`), so the manager and its native kernel need no new caster. A decoy's Mover takes its owner's card's move when the event names it (`Mover._owns`); its answer counts as the card's end point (`CasterMoves.asked`).

## 2026-11-06: Penta's ring turns (owner's decision)

- **Before:** each cast turned the ring to a new random angle, so casts in a row jumped, and a held ring stood still.
- **Now (owner: "Instead of Penta rotating in steps. It should rotate continuously if eg. Held"; "Both"):** the ring turns at `SpellLimits.PENTA_TURN` (π a second). Casts in a row stand where the card's last ring has turned to (`SpellRuntime._penta_rings`; the first from `penta_start(tag)`), and each turns the other way from the one before (owner, later the same day: "I don't think mirror should reverse turning. Penta could simply swap rotation direction every cast instead"), so a held button sweeps back and forth; a beam copy (`CarrierBeam`, `_bm_ring`) turns with its ring while it lasts, still aimed in at the centre. Nothing else needed a rule: a still shot stays where it was placed and a flying shot flies off, as they do from any caster.
- **Not:** the ring does not follow its centre ("Don't let it follow the centre"). Mirror's second cast skipped Penta (five ring copies and one plain copy backwards); now a firing and its mirror both go through `SpellRuntime._cast_once`, so a mirror is the same cast again with its aim reversed and nothing else (owner: "Fix #1"), and mirror reverses no turning. A Penta's mirror is a second ring, and as the next cast it turns the other way, from the rule above, not from mirror.

## 2026-11-06: A payload spares the body that fired it until clear of it; runes arm (owner's decision)

- **Before:** a payload's shots could not hit the body whose hit fired them for `SOURCE_EXCLUSION` (0.1 s). A slow shot was still on that body when the time ran out, so `on hit · bolt · slow · nova` put all eight shots on the imp the bolt hit (the owner: it "can possibly be abused"). A fast shot born at the contact point could also cross the body and hit it (both in docs/BACKLOG.md, now gone).
- **Now (owner: "I really like that rule! Fix it"):** no timer. `ProjectileManager.exclude(slot, node)` keeps the shot off that body until a hit test finds it clear of it where its step starts; from then on it hits that body like any other (a `return`, a bounce, the body stepping back into its way). The manager's `_hit_test` and the C++ `ShotKernel::scan` do the same (`_skip_on`), checked by `test_native_kernel`. A payload that starts off the body is clear at once, so yesterday's rule (`SpellRuntime._launch`, `touches_target`) is now part of this one; it stays in `_launch` for the carriers that are not shots (a beam never moves off its source, so one born on it never hits it).
- **Runes arm (owner: "they should have an arm time and only explode after eg. 1s (and that should hurt the enemy it spawned on)"):** a rune hits nothing for `SpellLimits.RUNE_ARM` (1 s), as a local stop (`freeze_shot`: its life, hits and cards wait, so it still lives its 8 s once armed), then goes off on whatever is on it. It is the exception to the rule above: `CarrierRune.launch` sets no exclusion, so a rune cast by a hit goes off on that body once armed. Every rune arms, a cast one too: a foe that crosses a fresh rune in its first second is not hit.
- **Tests:** `test_projectiles_ends.gd` (held while on it, a slow shot crawls out, hit when they meet again, a shot starting off it hits it), `test_slow.gd` (a slowed on-hit nova does not shotgun its imp), `test_rune.gd` (arming, and a rune cast on a hit goes off on that imp). The trigger tests that counted a still probe's hits on its own source count the probes now; `fire_trap` and `overcharged_fire_rune` run a second longer for the arm time.

## 2026-11-05: Penta always rings its caster; homing no longer moves the ring (owner's decision)

- **Before:** with homing on the same carrier, Penta's ring stood round the target the homing picked, and homing also steered each copy. That gave homing a second job only Penta had: a rule between two cards, the kind the portal dropped the same day ("to not have custom rules").
- **Now (owner: "Drop it, Penta always rings the caster"):** `SpellRuntime._penta` reads no other card. The ring stands round its caster: the wizard's body, or the point or shot that casts a payload. Homing steers each copy after it leaves the ring, as on any carrier.
- **A payload that starts outside the body that fired it can hit it (owner, same day: option 1, "with the rule \"a payload that starts outside the body that fired it can hit it\"").** A payload was kept off the body whose hit fired it: a shot for `SOURCE_EXCLUSION` (0.1 s), a beam always, so a ring of payload beams round a hit never touched the imp it was cast round. Now `SpellRuntime._launch` keeps it off only when it starts touching that body (`ProjectileManager.touches_target`: its own radius plus `SpellLimits.SOURCE_TOUCH`, 8 px of slack for a bigger shot that hit). An ordinary payload starts where the shot that hit was, so it still spares that body (`test_the_held_spell_cannot_hit_the_target_that_fired_it`); a Penta copy on its ring hits it like anything else. A general rule, not a Penta one.
- **Surrounding a target:** cast Penta as a payload: `on hit · bolt · penta · fire · beam` rings where the bolt hit, and `on hit · bolt · hold · penta · beam` keeps that ring of beams on while the staff is held (a held payload holds, M2-rules R1f). The combos `penta_beam_cook` and `penta_frost_novas` now do it that way; `test_penta.gd` checks both, the held ring, and that homing leaves the ring round the caster.

## 2026-11-06: Online play may slow the game, and netcode can be allowed (owner)

- **Slowing the game is allowed** (owner: "Slowing the game is an option if we have to!"). Owner decision 4 of online-ready (60 ticks a second, fixed) stands for normal play, but online a session may run slower when a machine cannot keep up: fewer ticks a second, or the game's speed lowered. A last resort, after the session rules (N11) are capped at the weakest machine's.
- **Netcode can be allowed** (owner: "netcode can easily be allowed"). The netcode phase's exception to hard rule 4 is a formality, not an open question. The rule (AGENTS.md) and its check (`ci/smbot/rules.py`, owner-only) change when netcode work starts; until then networking stays banned.
- **Asked in the same talk, still open:** whether online play is lockstep (the plan) or host-authoritative with Godot's own multiplayer (the host simulates, clients draw; lighter for weak machines and the web, more bandwidth), and whether to pause the lockstep-only parts (N6, N7, N8, N10) for a netcode spike first.

## 2026-11-06: One clock: the Tick (online-ready N3a, owner session)

- **N3 is three PRs:** N3a the Tick (this), N3b a run starts from a fresh game's state, N3c the `sim` service and chance by hash. N3 as one PR touched five large files' resets, every seeded outcome and the clock at once; each part is checked by its own test.
- **The Tick** (`core/sim/tick.gd`, autoload `Tick`, before every other physics step) is the only driver: the input frames, the services (projectiles, Spells, Combat, Entities), the swarm, every actor's body by id (`Actor.tick_step`), then the run's rules and timers (Flow, Coop's `step_rules`, the horde's `step_timers`). **The order is the one the scene tree gave, not the plan's sketch** (Flow and Coop first, actors before Spells): the game plays as before (the wave balance numbers are the same to the decimal), and changing it would move every cast and hit by a step for no gain. Only the bodies changed: by actor id, not by when they joined the tree.
- **No callbacks:** the areas' `_physics_process` and Flow's, Coop's and the horde's `_process` timers are gone (A2); drawing stays in `_process`. **The flag is the switch:** the tick steps what has its physics processing (timers: processing) on and can process, so pauses work as before and the tests that step a body by hand (`set_physics_process(false)`) did not change.
- **The delta** is the physics step's, `Tick.DT` (`test_tick.gd`), except while Feel's stops scale the engine's time: N4 counts them in ticks, so the delta is not forced here (forcing it would end hit-stop's slow motion until N4).
- **The horde read the camera (found by N2's replay once Coop's timers ran on the tick):** its spawn ring was the eased camera's view, and the camera eases on real time, so a horde run played differently at another frame rate; N2's test hid it by holding Coop's `_process`. Now Coop works out the fitted view on the tick from where the wizards stand and sends it (`view_fitted`, new event); the horde spawns outside that and reads no camera (`test_the_spawns_never_read_the_camera` fails on the old code). In play: enemies appear outside the view the camera is heading to rather than the one it is easing through, a few pixels apart while the party moves.
- **Cost:** none measured. The headless storm, two runs each: a physics step's scripts 15.53 and 15.56 ms against 15.61 and 15.68 ms before.
- **`test_wave_balance.gd`:** its timers no longer follow drawn frames, the cause docs/BACKLOG.md named; three runs at once gave the same clear before and after (15.8 s for one player), so the flake did not show on this machine either way.

## 2026-11-06: The same game on every platform, a first look (online-ready N2b, owner session)

- **How it was measured.** A scripted sandbox fight (the storm, bolts, dashes, a staff rebuilt mid-run) and a horde run of a bot, 600 ticks each, recorded and played back on the real game in the test exports, every area's state hash printed at every tick (`tests/platforms/replay_hashes_runner.gd`, on `--replay-hashes`; `tools/platform_hashes.py run` exports, runs and compares). Every platform plays the same game: a fixed run seed (`Frames.record` takes one) and the web's caps (1,000 shots, the horde's web alive cap; per-machine settings until N11). Linux headless; Windows through WSL's interop, from a copy under %TEMP%, in a new **Windows Test** preset (the Windows preset with tests/); the web in headless Chromium (`smbot.websmoke`, SwiftShader). Not Wine and not Playwright, as the plan guessed: neither is installed, and the owner wants no downloads. About 2 minutes a run on Linux, 4 on Windows, 7 on the web.
- **What the module covered.** The web ran the Emscripten-built native kernels; Windows ran the GDScript twins (no MinGW-w64 here to build the DLL), so it was compared with a Linux run of the GDScript too. Linux native against Linux GDScript: the same at all 600 ticks of both scenes (after the fixes below). The MinGW DLL is first compared in N8's CI.
- **The inputs were the same everywhere:** each replay's bytes had the same md5 on all four runs, and each platform's replay played back to its own recording at every tick.
- **Where the platforms part: only in the engine's trig, one float step apart.**
  - Windows, sandbox tick 0 and horde tick 89: `Spells._walk_turn`, `aim.angle_to(walk)` (atan2; core/spells/spells.gd), -0.067111656 against Linux's -0.06711166.
  - Web, sandbox tick 1: the atom's spread, `Vector2.from_angle(angle) * speed` (core/spells/carriers/atom.gd), one velocity 28.682417 against 28.682415, then the shots made from it.
  - Web, horde tick 117: the aim a frame carries, `Vector2.from_angle` of the 16-bit angle (core/sim/input_frame.gd), 0.33083495 against 0.33083493, then the wizard's aim and the cast.
  - So nothing parted inside the engine's own code before trig did, the plan's main unknown, and N7 (`DMath`) takes exactly these calls out. What hides behind the first parting (the body's `move_and_slide`, A4, above all) is unseen until N7 lands: N8 runs this again then.
- **What the first runs found in N1's and N2's tools,** fixed with tests that fail on the old code:
  - **The engine sorts StringNames by address, not text.** `StateWalk` walked a dictionary's keys "sorted", so the web walked Spells' carriers in another order and the hashes parted with the game the same. `StateWalk.sorted_keys` sorts StringName keys by text. The simulation's own sorts are safe (`horde_curve.gd` already sorts its ids by text).
  - **The native kernels write the manager's arrays in place, past copy-on-write.** A copy taken with `duplicate` shares the buffer, so the desync report's kept leaf showed the next step's shots (N2's fix of the same bug held only for the GDScript). `Desync._copy` slices a packed array into a buffer of its own. **For N10:** a snapshot copies the manager's arrays the same way.
  - **The native switch and the hit test's scratch were state.** `native` (the projectile manager's and the swarm's) gives the same game either way, and `_target_mark`, `_target_entry`, `_cand_target`, `_cand_along`, `_cand_point` and `_cand_found` are scratch only the native pass sizes ahead: all DERIVED. Before that a native and a GDScript run parted at tick 0, then 1, then 499.
- **The tool is kept** for N8 (its CI job) and for whoever changes the kernels or the maths: `tools/platform_hashes.py run [--platforms linux,windows,web] [--ticks N] [--scenes sandbox,horde,wave]`, and `compare LOG...` for saved logs. When a platform parts it runs both again up to that tick, keeping its leaves, and names the members.
## 2026-11-05: Every shot bursts out and slows; the Drag card is gone (owner's request)

- **Why:** the owner liked the motion Drag gave a carrier ("it kinda makes them look like fireworks") and wanted it as every shot's default, in one place, so every carrier now and later moves that way.
- **Where:** `SpellLimits.SHOT_SPEED_START`, `SHOT_SPEED_END`, `SHOT_SPEED_EASE` are the starting values of `CompiledSpell.speed_start`, `speed_end`, `speed_ease`, so every compiled spell's flying shots take the curve through the path Drag already used (`Spells._apply_travel`, `ProjectileManager.set_speed_curve`, the GDScript pass and the kernel's, unchanged). A card's speed numbers still multiply on top; a carrier that does not fly has no speed to curve, as before. The Drag card's folder is deleted.
- **The numbers: 2.2, 0.2, 1.5 (Drag was 2.5, 0, 1.5).** On every shot rather than one card, a slightly softer burst, and an end that keeps a fifth of the speed, so a shot's last moments drift out like a spark instead of hanging dead still in the air (and an imp at the edge of its range is still reached, not crept up on). The three keep the shot's range exactly speed x life (start + (end - start) x ease / (ease + 1) = 1, as Drag's did), so no carrier's reach or balance changes; only when it gets there.
- **The curve is read at the middle of each step** (`_pass_curve` and the kernel's, which mirror each other): the life is counted down before the pass, so reading the speed for the step's end left a front-loaded curve a step's change short, about 2% of a 1 s shot's reach and 4% of a nova's. Read at the middle it goes as far as speed x life to within a pixel; `test_projectiles_kit.gd` holds it to that.
- **What moved with it:** a payload's shots cannot hit the target that fired the trigger for `SOURCE_EXCLUSION` (0.1 s); slow shots used to be still inside that target when it ran out and hit it anyway (an `on hit` nova struck its own imp with half its ring). Bursting shots are out of it by then, so such a payload now hits the bodies around its source, which is what the exclusion is for. Combos placed by a shot's old timing were moved (linger's two to where a shot is at half its life now, growth's orb, the hexed fire burst, deflect's return); the beam's on-hit nova now checks the imp beside the line.
- **A sticky shot sticks on its line of flight** where it comes closest to the target's centre (a head-on bolt: the centre), the same spot whichever moment of a step it met the target in (`_register_hit`). It stuck at the point of that step's path nearest the centre, anywhere from the centre to the rim, so a planted nova did 14 or 29 to its imp by the distance flown (before this change as well). Tests: `test_projectiles_kit.gd` (the spot, six step phases) and `test_sticky.gd` (the same damage at eight distances).
- **Tests:** `tests/core/test_shot_speed_curve.gd` takes over Drag's (sooner strike, same reach, four players at once) and checks a card's numbers multiply the default.

## 2026-11-05: A card is one folder (owner's request)

- **Why:** adding the Drag card touched about 25 files, and taking a card out meant finding its lore file, its combos, its place in hand-written test lists and, worse, renumbering: a modifier stored its behaviour as the enum's number, so removing a behaviour would quietly give every later card another one.
- **The card's folder holds what is the card's:** its `.tres` with the builder line in it (`SpellComponent.description`; the 67 files of `content/story/lore/components/` moved in as they were, same rules, a missing one still only warns), icon, `assets.json` and `combos/` (each combo moved to the folder of the card it was written for: the combo test's control card, else its name's prefix, else its rarest card). Its test is in the folder too (`test_<id>.gd`; the owner's follow-up the same day): `ci/build/build.sh` and `tools/ptest.sh` run every `test_*.gd` under `content/` as well as `tests/`, and the shipped presets leave `content/*/test_*.gd*` out (`test_export_presets.gd` checks it). The 42 single-card tests moved, then the group tests were split (the owner, the same day): `test_forms`, `test_modifiers` and `test_triggers` became the first fourteen cards' own tests on a shared rig (`tests/support/card_rig.gd`), their cards-together tests `tests/core/test_cards_together.gd`, `test_homing_forms` moved to the Homing card, the cue each card's moments play and a wall's stones moved from `test_carrier_looks` to the cards, and `test_elements` reads every element from the registry. What stays in `tests/content/spells/` holds every card to a rule over the registry (validator, every card in its own folder with its own icon, combos, looks, elements, carrier cues). Adding a card is adding its folder; taking one out is deleting the folder. Both were tried on this change (a made-up Surge card added, Drag deleted): the whole suite passed.
- **Behaviours by name, one row each.** A card says `behaviour_id = &"return"`; the enum stays as core's index (fast arrays, 250 uses), and what the rules know about a behaviour (needs, stacking, travel only) is one row of `ModifierBase.RULES`, replacing `NEEDS` and the compiler's `MULTIPLIED` and `TRAVEL_ONLY`. A new behaviour is still engine work: the enum value, its row, and the code that runs it (the manager's pass and its C++ twin, or the runtime). Kept in code, not data: what a behaviour does to a shot (`Spells._travel_shots`), since that is engine code next to the setters it calls.
- **A static var on `ModifierBase` leaked a script at exit** in every test (Godot keeps a script with a static var alive in a reference cycle); the name lookup reads the enum's own dictionary instead.
- **Motion as numbers where it can be.** Drag was a behaviour with its own pass; it is now three modifier numbers any card can set (`speed_start`, `speed_end`, `speed_ease`: speed over life, `ProjectileManager.set_speed_curve`, one pass in GDScript and the kernel). A card that speeds up, slows evenly or bursts out is a new `.tres`. The other motions (return, homing, spiral, linger) stay behaviours: each needs code no number can say.
- **Tests find the cards** in the registry: the landing test casts a card its sets do not with a staff of its own made from its kind; the looks test lists the shareable look kinds, not the cards; a combo whose point is one card says so in its file (`"needs"`), replacing the combo test's `CONTROLS`.
- **Description checks live in `SpellValidator`** (`check_descriptions`), using the story validator's line rules: only `core/spells` may read a card's fields (`test_spell_field_reads.gd`).

## 2026-11-05: Input frames and replays (online-ready N2, owner session)

- **One input path.** The simulation takes input only from `InputFrame`s (`core/sim/input_frame.gd`): a seat's move (a signed byte per axis), aim (a 16-bit angle or keep), staff buttons held and pressed, captured, and the tick's commands. `PlayerController` builds its intent from `Frames.frame_of(seat)`; the device layer moved in front of the frame (`core/input/local_input.gd`: the stick's aim hold, the mouse turned into a direction from the hand, press counts, captured). Bots fill frames (`FrameSource`) instead of pressing virtual buttons, and think at the tick's start.
- **The press buffer stays in the simulation,** not in front of the frame as the plan said: it lasts until a cast leaves, which only the simulation knows. The frame carries the fresh press; `PlayerController` remembers it for `INPUT_BUFFER`, as before.
- **The tick's start is the `Frames` autoload's physics step** (`process_physics_priority` -1000, the smallest honest hook until N3's Tick takes it over). It runs while the tree is paused too, since the horde's level-up break takes its picks then.
- **Commands at the next tick's start.** The HUD's requests the simulation hears (the builder's three, the horde's pick and ready, the sandbox page's action and spawn, reward pick, door vote) are commands: the bus hands them to `Frames` (`Events.command_sink`), which puts them into the seat's next frame and applies them then (`Events.apply_command`); the simulation hears them through `Events.listen`, never the signal. The HUD did not change. A frame speaks for its own seat (the seat is not on the wire). The requests that start a run (play, sandbox, horde) are not commands: a replay's header says how its run started. `bag_set_requested` is the horde's, inside the simulation, and stays a plain event. Tests on the game's bus wait with `commands_applied()`.
- **Joins and leaves** are in the frame (JOIN, LEAVE) so a replay knows the tick, but still happen as the device joins: Flow and Coop read the joined seats when a run starts, and online joining is N10's snapshot. A replay played joins its seats with virtual devices.
- **The strict decoders:** sizes and ranges checked, an id is ASCII letters, digits and `_` only (so never a path), nothing left over, no `bytes_to_var`; a bad frame or replay is rejected whole and says why.
- **Replays** (`core/sim/replay.gd`): the build (version and commit), the content (a hash of every content id; a changed number inside a file is not seen, which the build covers), the run's seed, its start and seats, and each tick's frames with whether the game stood paused (a pause-menu pause in the recording is skipped through with its commands; a paused tick plays while the game stands paused, as the horde's break). Only `user://replays/`. While one records or plays, `SimSeed` deals the seeds from the replay's seed, and the horde director and the swarm take a new one at each run's start (A11 for replays; tests that seed them are unchanged).
- **What the replays found,** each fixed or listed: Flow's `spells` and `save`, Coop's `_downs` (REFS) and camera target (VIEW), the swarm's kernel and its rows by instance id, the AI board's attack targets by instance id (DERIVED until N5), the swing's and slam's struck lists (REFS). Two N1 bugs: a kept tick's packed arrays were held by reference (the report showed later values), and every float in a report was an error line (`%.17g`). The walker no longer stops on a REFS array of arrays or an area with no script.
- **What a replay does not hold yet (N3, N4, N5):** the services' clocks run from the game's start and their counters carry from run to run (`sim_time`, `_step_index`, `_next_seq`, Spells' clock, Combat's steps, the AI board's clock), so a replay plays the same only from a fresh game the same number of ticks after its start; N3 starts the one clock with the run. Flow's, Coop's and the horde's `_process` timers (A2) and Feel's real-time stops (A1) run on drawn frames. Shots keep homing targets and skips by instance id (`_home_target`, `_skip_id`): a run with homing shots parts where they find targets (N5; the file is in the projectile lane).
- **A bot changes what it reads.** The bot's brain asks the projectile manager for threats and targets, which fills caches counted as state (the threat grid, the target table) and changes the next step; a replay without the bot parts at tick 0. So a bot's run replays with the bots watching again: a source set while a replay plays is asked each tick and its frame dropped. A person's input reads nothing of the game but where the hand is, and a scripted player's run replays as it is. The caches are the projectile manager's (its lane, or N3's written order).
- **The test** (`tests/core/test_replay_game.gd`): a scripted player in the sandbox (the storm, a dash, bolts, a staff rebuilt mid-run by a command), two fighter bots in a wave and a bot in a horde run, each recorded on the real autoloads and played back from a fresh game (`tests/support/fresh_game.gd`, every autoload made again), the hash agreeing in every area at all 240 ticks. It freezes Feel's clock and steps Flow's, Coop's revive and the horde's `_process` timers once a tick, as N3 will. About 75 s.
- **Open for the owner:** whether the game should keep the last run's replay by default (for bug reports), and where (the intake task that attaches one).

## 2026-11-05: Shot scale H1 dropped, H2: the hit budget priced by measurement and no lost damage (owner session)

- **H1 dropped (the owner's pick, after measuring it):** a hand-back from the native hit pass costs about 3-4 us (1.8 us the call itself, the rest reading the target arrays): about 12-15 ms of the 211 ms step of crowd_all at 100,000 shots, under 1 ms in a budgeted step. A run of hits handed back in the same order needs the kernel to scan ahead and undo whenever a hit handler changes a target or a later shot (and the hit memory's free list). H3b's hit pass hands back only walls and ends, which takes the crossings away anyway.
- **The budget lost damage:** a hit was priced at 40 units, a target examined at 1, so a budget of 6,000 went on about 150 hits, and in a crowd most shots went untested that step; an untested shot skipped what it passed, and one that met a wall past the budget ended untested. In the benchmark's window the crowd at the desktop cap dealt 47% of its damage, the storm at 30,000 shots half.
- **Priced by measurement:** a unit is one target examined by the native hit pass (about 0.13 us), a hit 400 units (about 50 us of listeners; H0, owner's laptop). `WORK_PER_STEP` = 150,000, the smallest round budget at which no shot waits more than one step in a full crowd at the desktop cap (`test_hit_budget.gd`; at 120,000 thousands wait twice). A fixed number in the code, never measured at run time (online-ready T1). The GDScript fallback's scan costs more a unit; it is only slower.
- **No lost damage:** a deferred shot is marked `F_DEFERRED` and keeps where its untested path starts (`_untested`); its next test sweeps all of it, and a deferred shot that ends is tested first whatever the budget. Not covered: a shot ended or blinked by a hit handler, or removed by a cap drop or a revoked tag, while it waits (its untested path goes untested; a blink would otherwise sweep across the gap).
- **With moving targets** a deferred shot sweeps its old path against where the targets are now, so which body it hits may differ (totals over whole flights within 0.01%). With still targets a budget changes no hit at all.
- **The cost moves to the caps:** the hits the budget dropped now happen, so a full crowd at the desktop cap costs about 13 ms a manager step on the owner's laptop (6 ms before). Since no damage is lost the budget only spreads a burst of hits over a step or two; the caps (H7) and cheaper hits (H3, H3b) bound the cost.
- **Recorded again:** `test_projectiles_step_exact.gd` (a budget of its own, 50,000, about the 125 hits a step its flood had) and `test_projectiles_targets.gd` (80,000, spent in 49 of its 90 steps; a new check that it is spent in some steps).

## 2026-11-05: Hits' motes shared by staff, whole sprays or none (owner session)

- **The owner's ask:** after the tails (#567, #568), a lone cast's impacts should stay readable beside a crowd's too: a Fireball hitting a wall in a Storm threw nothing, because the Storm's hits came first in the frame and took every hit row (`Motes.MOMENT_ROWS` kept, first come for the rest).
- **Shared by staff, as the tails:** every hit is one spray (a shot's shed on a body or a wall, the wall's chips, its cue's `often` sparks, its damage's `hit` sparks; Feel frames them with `Spectacle.begin_hit` and `end_hit`, and the damage follows the impact's roll with `hold_hit`), in its caster's staff's group (`Spectacle.hit_group`, the projectile manager's `group_of`; statuses' motes a group of their own, hits of no one's another). `gameplay/feel/hit_shares.gd` shares the frame's hit rows (`Motes.hit_rows()`: the frame's past the tails' and the moments') with `fair_level`.
- **Variant (b), the owner's pick:** a thinned staff's hits are a fair random subset that spray whole (each hit rolls its staff's chance once), not every hit a little: a storm keeps the look of sprays, only fewer.
- **Planned, not queued:** each staff's chance is planned at the step from the frame before (its hits times the rows a spray of it took), as the tails are, so a hit is written when it happens (no queue, no delay, nothing allocated) and a skipped hit costs no card lookup. A staff new since the plan sprays whole; beside a thinned staff, a staff not thinned may also take `Motes.FRESH_ROWS` (32) of the moments' rows, so a lone cast's first hit is never cut by a crowd's (with no crowd, hits leave the moments all of theirs, as before). Holding a quarter of the hit rows back for new staffs instead left a lone Storm a third fewer motes; this way it keeps about as many (116 a frame against 128 before, the rolls' spread).
- **The budget table:** the frame's rows by class (tails, moments, hits, settles, births) are one table at `Motes.FRAME_ROWS`, saying what each class keeps and which ones are first come.
- **Cost:** about 0.7 us a hit of bookkeeping (300 wall impacts a frame: about +0.2 ms on desktop headless, noisy runs; `test_hit_shares.gd`'s setup timed). Not measured on the GPU web build yet.
- **Tests:** `test_hit_shares.gd`: a lone staff beside a crowd, a thinned staff never past its share, staffs with room never thinned, the groups, a Fireball's wall impact whole every frame in a Storm of 300 hits (0 motes without the sharing), the Storm's impacts whole or none, and a body hit's damage spray following its impact's roll.

## 2026-11-05: The flash shader made as fast as before flash kinds (owner session)

- **Measured first** (the web storm on the owner's GPU, `webstorm.sh --seconds=10`, main before #569 against #569, three pairs, one left out for a loaded machine): frame time avg 6.0 against 6.5-7.0 ms, p95 15.3-15.8 against 17.3-19.4 ms. The game was the same (5,947 hits each run), and the shader compiled and ran on Chrome's ANGLE/Direct3D. The owner: "build the fix and optimize it more if you can".
- **Fewer texels:** a curve's search stops at the first segment not started (they start in order), and a curve the kind does not give is read as its default without a texel: a third head texel holds the mask of the curves it gives (`FlashTable.given_of`, `HEADS` 3; the shader's defaults at each `curve()` call are `FlashTable.DEFAULTS`, a test holds them). Each quad reads only the curves it draws: the glow its glow and the ring and column it lights round, the shape its parts and shades.
- **Smaller quads:** the shape and the glow were each drawn over the flash's whole square (24 x zoom across); now each covers only what it can colour, with a margin: the shape its parts' reach (a ring + 1, the dots + 2, the rays + 1) and its column, the glow its two falloffs' reach (`falloff` is 0 past it) round its ring or column, and no glow quad at all when its glow is 0. The fragment works out an angle or a hash only for a part the flash has.
- **The same pixels, but for ties:** the comparison render (old shader against new, 4,752 cases of the ported kinds and 2,592 of F4's, as #569's) gave every pixel the same for flashes along an axis, and 139 pixels in all differ for flashes turned 0.6 rad: one edge pixel at a time, whose centre lies on a grid line to within a float's last bit, which a quad of other corners interpolates the other way. Not a change of shape. The probe was not committed this time (#569's first F2 commit has it).
- **Measured after** (the same storm, three alternated pairs): before 6.17/15.6, 6.03/15.0 ms (and 7.39/20.4 on a loaded run), after 6.10/15.1, 6.01/14.5, 6.32/15.9 ms: the same within the noise, so flash kinds cost nothing measurable in the storm.

## 2026-11-05: Four new drawings for flashes (flash kinds F4, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner's "Add them" to the plan's question 4 (a star, rays, a dotted ring, a spiral).
- **Four parts, each a few lines of the flash shader's fragment stage with no loop**, drawn in the shape quad with the old ones (disc, ring, dots, spiral, rays, star, then column and cross on top): a star of `star_points` points (its reach narrowing between the points as |cos(n theta / 2)|^6, white-hot `first` in its inner 30%), `ray_count` one-pixel rays from `rays_inner` out to `rays` (white-hot within 2 px of their inner end), a ring of `dot_count` dots at `dots` (a pixel, a plus from zoom 2), and a spiral of `spiral_arms` arms out to `spiral`, turned `spiral_turn` turns, coils `spiral_gap` px a zoom step apart (closed form: the pixel's coil is near a whole one). Each size is a curve like the others; the counts are whole numbers in a second head texel of the kind's row (`FlashTable.HEADS` 2, `TEXELS` 170) and travel packed in two varyings (points + 16 rays + 512 arms; dots + 128 for a plus), so at most 12 points, 31 rays, 64 dots, 4 arms (`FlashKind.problems`).
- **The spiral's gap scales with the zoom:** in pixels it packed two arms into rings at zoom 2 (the first sheet); a zoom step's pixels keep its coils as many at every size, as every other size does.
- **Six example kinds, not yet named by any cue:** `star`, `star_six`, `rays_out`, `rays_in`, `dot_ring`, `spiral`, drawn by `tests/screenshots/shot_flash_drawings.gd` (plain, fire, frost, lightning; a third and two thirds through their lives). The owner picks which, if any, a cue shows (a parry's star, a level-up's rays).
- **The port stays exact:** the new parts are off unless a kind gives them (`test_flashes` checks every ported kind draws none), so F2's pixel comparison still holds.

## 2026-11-05: Flash kinds are data (flash kinds F2 and F3, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner's go for [flash-kinds.md](milestones/flash-kinds.md): flash looks as data, ported exactly first ("yes"), then tuned.
- **A `FlashKind` per file** (`core/base/flash_kind.gd`, `content/flashes/`, 14 kinds): its `draw`, seconds, step, `room_light`, `on_floor`, and for a `shape` its curves over its life: a cross (`arm`), a dotted disc, a fraying ring (round, diamond or flat; its salt), a column (width, top, foot, `column_length`, dotted), its glow (`glow`, round its ring, round its middle) and its two shades. A curve is up to 4 segments, each from `at`, `from`/`to` in pixels plus `from_z`/`to_z` in zoom steps, a way (`linear`, `out`, `in`, `hold`), an `over` (its way may end after the next segment cuts it: the rise's ring eases over the whole life and stops at 0.7) or `off`. Every curve of the old vertex stage fit: piecewise, at most 4 pieces, no new math.
- **The shader reads a table** (`gameplay/feel/flash_table.gd`: a row a kind, 121 texels RGBAF, two a segment), its fragment stage untouched; the vertex stage's per-kind branches are one `curve()` with a fixed loop of 4 (small for ANGLE and Direct3D).
- **The kind travels in the transform:** the custom data is half precision in the compatibility renderer (probed: 0.90123 read back 0.90098), too coarse for 256 kinds beside an age; the 2D transform's two unused floats reach `MODEL_MATRIX[2].xy` at full precision (probed: 77 and 201 exactly). Feel writes the table row in the first and the flash's room light in the second; spell light reads the light there, so `SpellLight.FLASH_LIGHT`, `BLAST_LIGHT`, `kind_lights`, the shader's `kind_light[16]` and the unlit sign of the age code are gone. A table row's age code sits beside 15 (`FLASH_TABLE_CODE`), where half floats are finest.
- **F3 folded in:** the shell, the fireball and the arc are kind files too (`draw` names their shader), so `CUE_FLASHES`, `CueBase.FLASHES`, `LENGTH_FLASHES`, the KIND_* codes of the flares and every flash's `*_SECONDS` constant are gone, and the tests changed once. Whether a cue's flashes exist is `CueBase.kind_problems(kinds)` (the content test), as a motes part's kind is the registry's. The `poof` is its own kind: the `POOF` look bit is gone, so **look bit 22 is free** for a card's look layer.
- **Tests and API:** `show_flash`, `flashes_of`, `flash_info`, `flash_dress` take a kind's id; `status_info()`, `flash_kind(id)`. Test registries that lend the game's cues lend its flash kinds too.
- **Ported exactly, checked twice:** `tests/content/test_flashes.gd` holds every curve of the 11 ported kinds to the old branches written in GDScript (97 ages, zooms 1-4; it stays); and a one-off render (`tests/screenshots/probe_port.gd` with the old shader, in this PR's first commit and deleted in its second) drew 4,752 cases (11 kinds x 18 ages x zooms 1, 2, 4 x two aims x a hat ramp and the enemy ramp x plain and fire) with the old shader and the new one in the compatibility renderer (llvmpipe): every pixel the same.

## 2026-11-05: Effects are flashes (flash kinds F1, owner session)

- **The owner's question:** "Would it make sense to consolidate effects and motes?" No: a mote is a particle that flies by itself after one row write; a flash is one shaped glow whose shader draws it whole, follows a body, or lasts a telegraph's wait. What were particles in the flash shader are already motes (S3, S6). The gap is that a new flash look is code, where a mote kind, a mark kind and a cue are files: the plan is [flash-kinds.md](milestones/flash-kinds.md) (F2 to F4). The owner: rename "Yes", "Effect slots" "Then keep it", port exactly then tune "yes", new drawings "Add them".
- **The rename:** Feel's effects are flashes, the word the newer code and docs already used (`CuePlan.Flash`, the cues README, the shader's header): the cue part key and type `effect` are `flash` (the 60 cue files), `show_flash`, `flashes_of`, `flash_info`, `live_flashes`, `flash_dress`, `flash_mesh`, `_flash_*`, `FLASH_*`, `CUE_FLASHES`, `CueBase.FLASHES`, `SpellLight.FLASH_LIGHT`, `light_flashes`, `shot_flash.gdshader`, the sheet `shot_flashes.gd`. "Effect" is left to status effects and what a card does.
- **The hit flash** (a sprite turning white when hit) owned "flash" before: it is `HIT_FLASH_SHADER`, `_hit_flash_material` and `FeelPreset.hit_flash_seconds` now, and the slot of a hit's spray is `_hit_slot`. The horde's `SwarmLook` keeps its own row arrays (`_flash_left`, `FLAG_FLASH`): they are its hit flash, inside its own file.
- **Kept:** the "Effect slots" setting, its saved key, `EffectSlots` and `effect_slots` (the owner's pick: the slots hold flashes, arcs and statuses). No look changed; the suite passes unchanged but for the names.

## 2026-11-05: Tails and particles shared by staff, not by shot (owner session)

- **The owner's case:** "when casting eg. the Storm wand, it keeps creating (splitting) new shots. So If i fire the fireball wand while the storm is still going, im not getting a tail on the fireball. But the storm is already densely packed, so I cant even see individual tails there, so they are kinda wasted." The tails' births (`tail_births_cap` a frame) and the cards' particles (`tailless_particle_cap`) thinned every shot alike, so a Storm of thousands of shots took all but a thousandth of each, and a Fireball's share was a thousandth.
- **Shared by group:** every shot is in a group, its caster's staff (`group_of`: the spell tag's staff; a shot of no spell, its caster's first), kept as it spawns and ends (`_join_group`, `_leave_group`). A frame's budget is shared out fairly among the groups that ask (`fair_level`, water-filling): a group that asks for less than an even share gets all it asks for, the rest share what is left alike, and within a group every shot thins alike as before. A Fireball beside a Storm keeps its whole tail and its embers; two Storms thin alike.
- **A staff, not a cast:** a held Storm is many casts; by cast it would still take many shares against the Fireball's one. A payload's shots carry their staff in their tag, so a split or a trigger stays in its staff's group. Enemies' staffs are groups like any other.
- **The tails:** planned before the drawing (`_plan_groups`): a group's ask is its last frame's, scaled by its shots now against then (a Storm that split since asks for that much more); a group new since the last frame asks for nothing yet, so a new cast's tail is whole from its first frame. The level shares `TAIL_FRESH` (1/8) less than the most, the thinned groups stop at what they were given together (`tail_thin_most`), and the groups given their whole tail stop only at the most: a frame never lays more than `tail_births_cap`, a new cast always finds room, and a Fireball drawn after a Storm that laid past its share (slot order) is not cut for it. `ShotKernel.draw_shots` does the same to the bit (the group arrays are compared by `test_native_kernel.gd`).
- **The particles:** each group's share of its ask (`_group_share`, after the drawing), and the births rolled per group over its drawn shots (`_by_group`, filled in slot order by the drawing), so the work still follows the births. A plain shot asks for one particle (its twinkle; `particles_of` gives it none). The shaders' global `particle_share` (runes and orbits) is unchanged.
- **Tests:** `test_tail_births.gd`: a Storm of one staff and a lone Fireball of another (whole tail, the Storm thinned, the frame within the most; on the Storm's staff the Fireball thins with it), the same for embers beside a crowd over the pool, the fair level, a shot's group.

## 2026-11-05: A dash's streaks are a cue's stream, not the projectile manager's (owner session)

- **The owner's question:** "Do we actually need 'special code' for dashes?" After the trail removal, the manager kept a per-caster table (`follow_caster`, `_dash_*`, sampled every step) only to give off a dash's speed streaks and card particles: looks in the simulation, and state N1's hash had to cover.
- **Now:** a new cue part, `stream` (`CueBase.STREAM_KEYS`; `gameplay/feel/streams.gd`, the spectacle's `streams`): motes given off from the cue's caster while it moves, following its anchor (`Projectiles.get_anchor`), back along the way it goes (the cue's way until it has moved), from the cue until the carrier it shows ends (`carrier_ended` of its caster and tag; `Spectacle.end_streams`), the anchor is gone, or its `seconds` (2) run out. `kind` `dash_path` (the `streak` kind) or `spell` (its cards); `rate`, `side`, `ahead`, `feet_layers`, `below`. `dash_start` has two: the streaks (120 a second, 13 px across, 4 along, as `DASH_STREAKS` 2 a moment were) and the cards (60 tries a second, 10 px across, embers and flecks from its feet). The births go out with Feel's own (`births.gd`), so they take the spell's cards by tag as before.
- **One rule:** a dash's card particles are kept at a shot's rate for their layer (`SPARK_RATES`); embers and flecks were 0.5 (`DASH_FEET_RATE`) and are 0.45 and 0.32 now. The pool's share no longer thins them (Feel's births stop at the frame's most).
- **Gone from the manager:** `follow_caster`, `is_dashing`, the `_dash_*` table, `_sample_dashes`, `_give_off_dash_sparks`, `DASH_*`, and H0's `PROF_DASHES` part (the step's parts end with the passes after the hits). `SPARK_STREAK` stays: the birth kind the stream writes.

## 2026-11-05: The tail shader's tail removed (spell spectacle 2, S5 step 4, the cleanup; owner session)

- **What went, since tails are motes:** every shot's trail row and its sampling (30 a second), the trail texture and its upload, the cooling trails left where shots ended, the tail meshes and their switching by the pool's share (`TAILS_*`), `tails_enabled`, the tails-on spark pool (`PARTICLE_CAP`, `particle_cap`, `trail_share`, `trail_demand`, `TRAIL_COST`, `drawn_trails`), and in `shot_trail.gdshader` the path search, the tail pixels, the pieces' lights and lightning's jagged line (478 lines to 185). The same in `ShotKernel` (`sample_shots`, the loose rows in the drawing); `test_native_kernel.gd` still compares the two to the bit.
- **What stays:** a shot is drawn as its core and the core's light, told the way it flies in its instance (`_write_head`: no path, -1, then the way where a path's numbers were, as tail-less shots were already); the births' pool is `tailless_particle_cap`. A dash (`follow_caster`) keeps its contract (its streaks and cards along the way its caster goes) on a small per-caster table in place of a trail row, sampled every `DASH_EVERY` (1/30 s) as the rows were. A blink and a new orbit now start the shot's tail where it lands (`_tail_from`), as they started its trail.
- **Then the batches:** lightning's and the dashes' batches (`BATCH_*_JAG`, `BATCH_DASH`, `BATCH_ENEMY_DASH`) only differed by their mesh, and the dashes' were always empty: folded into the plain ones in a PR of its own (10 batches to 5, one mesh, `LOOK_LIGHTNING_BIT` and the mesh shapes gone; the native kernel's batch rule the same). Lightning's shots now draw in slot order with the others of their batch, not above them.
- **Recorded again:** `test_projectiles_step_exact.gd`'s recording, whose state lines held each shot's trail: every step's events, hits and live shots are the same as before (checked line by line), only the digests changed.

## 2026-11-05: Tails are motes, each shot's in one of three kinds (spell spectacle 2, S5 step 4, owner session)

- **The owner's pick:** "I'll pick motes. They look awesome as tails!" and "keep all three and make the shots randomly pick one". A shot's tail births are `SPARK_TAIL` + 0 to 2 (`SPARK_TAIL` is 60 now, `TAIL_KINDS` 3, so the codes fit the motes' pair key below `PAIR_LAYERS`), the code picked once from the shot's sequence (`tail_code`, `tail_hash(seq, -1)`; the native kernel's twin, compared to the bit by `test_native_kernel.gd`); the motes lay code n in the nth tail kind by id (A `tail`, B `tail_streak`, C `tail_long`).
- **Gone:** the "Tails" and "Tail motes" dev switches (`ShotTails`, `TailMotes`, `shot_tails_changed`, `tail_motes_changed`, their pause rows); `tails_enabled` is off. The tail shader's tail is dead code in the game now and goes in its own PR (its tests turn it on until then).
- **Cost:** the same CPU for A, B and C (the sandbox storm with the tails off, each twice in turn: 0.54-0.67 ms of the motes' a 60th of a second, 1.8-2.1 us a mote, the spread between two runs of one pick as wide as between picks); a mix lives longer on average than B alone (C's motes up to 0.55 s), so about a third more tail motes drawn, GPU only.

## 2026-11-05: Tails as motes (spell spectacle 2, S5 step 4, owner session)

- **The owner's answer to "do shots need tails?":** "I don't really like the look without tails. BUT I'm seeing a pretty substantial performance increase without them ... for the atom, when holding down the cast button, the motes seem to spawn at fixed equal distances along its path ... If only the motes could look more like a tail, then I would love them and the fact that we only have one system." So the tail is laid as motes; the tail shader's tail stays the "Tails: On" look until the owner picks.
- **Why the lattice:** each frame a shot's card births were rolled in the first `fraction` of its last step only (`_prev.lerp(_pos, randf * fraction)`), so the rest of each step never had any, and a held stream (same place, same speed, a shot a frame) put them at the same distances from the staff. Now they are rolled anywhere on the path drawn since the last frame (the frame's moments back from the drawn point). A test (`test_tail_births.gd`) fails on the old rule.
- **A tail by distance, not by moment:** `_lay_tail` (in the drawing, after the shot's own instance) lays a birth of `SPARK_TAIL` about every `TAIL_SPACING` (2) px of the path from where the shot was drawn last frame, each gap a random 0.5-1.5 of it from `tail_hash(sequence, count)` and the first from a phase of its own, so a fast shot's tail is as whole as a slow one's and no two shots line up. It is in `ShotKernel.draw_shots` too, to the bit (`test_native_kernel.gd` compares the births, the per-shot state and the demand with the tails off, past the cap too; a changed die in the C++ fails it). The dice are an integer hash, not the manager's RNG, so the C++ needs no random state; every product stays below 2^63 so GDScript's ints and the C++'s agree.
- **The budget:** `tail_births_cap` a frame (TAIL_BIRTHS 256 at the default births' pool, scaled by the "Tailless motes" slider, at most half of SPARK_BIRTHS_MAX); the frame's ask (`tail_demand`, births at TAIL_SPACING) over it widens the next frame's `tail_spacing`, so in a storm every tail thins and none is cut whole; the spectacle gives the motes that many rows more a frame while tails are laid, so a tail never takes a moment's rows. No interaction is limited: only how many motes draw a tail.
- **One system:** a tail mote is a mote like any other (one writer, `Motes.give_off` and the native `MoteKernel`, unchanged: a tail birth is one more (tag, kind) pair). Its kind is data: `MoteKind.tail` (a kind of no card, surface or dash), one in use at a time (`use_tail_kind`). Three to play with on the "Tail motes" dev switch (`TailMotes`, Off/A/B/C by id): A `tail` single pixels like the shader's, B `tail_streak` 3-px streaks along the way that join into a line (the default: in the sheet it reads most as one tail, in a held stream and on a single bolt), C `tail_long` twice the life, drifting up and swaying. They start one shade below white-hot (white-hot ones laid by a held stream of atoms, one a frame on one line, read as a solid white bar) and glow little. A quarter of an infused shot's tail is in its caster's colours (a white core), as the shader's was.
- **Measured once on the GPU build** (the owner's rule: `webstorm.sh --seconds=10`, the default storm, cap 1,000, 2 rounds of main tails on / main tails off / branch tails off, alternated, all six passed): frame average main tails on 29.4 / 30.9 ms, main tails off 29.8 / 30.2, branch tails off 31.8 / 31.8 (p95 79.8 / 82.2, 81.7 / 81.1, 83.5 / 86.9); a physics step's scripts 11.6 / 12.1, 11.5 / 11.6, 11.9 / 12.1 ms; the motes' CPU 0.47 / 0.48, 0.56 / 0.57, 0.88 / 0.88 ms a frame for 77, 136 and 330 motes a frame (the branch's births 214-216 a frame: the tails at their cap of 256, thinned); the manager's `update_draw` 0.37, 0.23, 0.24 ms (laying the tails in the native drawing costs next to nothing). So in this storm the tails cost about 0.3 ms of the motes' CPU and 1.5-2 ms of the web frame against no tails (about 5%; two pairs, noisy), where the tail shader cost this CPU-bound web storm nothing measurable (its cost is the GPU's). The GPU side the owner felt (no tail meshes) is kept: a tail mote is drawn only while it lives, a quad each. The lever if it is too much: TAIL_BIRTHS (128 halves the storm's tail births).
- **Open:** a bigger shot's tail was thicker in the shader (`zoom - 1` pixels); a tail mote is the same for every shot. Lightning's jagged line is not drawn with the tails off (S5) and its tail is a plain one. The motes' ring (8,192 on the web) holds a storm's tails too, so the longest-lived motes are overwritten sooner in one.

## 2026-11-05: A burst a row, again after S6: still not merged (spell spectacle 2, S4, owner session)

- **Why again:** S6 moved the flashes' sparks, the swing's, beam's and statuses' particles and the hits', ends' and deaths' puffs into cue bursts and births, so more of the storm's motes come in bursts, where a row a burst should pay most. The owner kept the branch open for this (PR #546).
- **The merge:** the branch took main 7a9d254d. The native trail births (`MoteKernel`, #548) now write the burst-row format: each birth a one-mote row of SINGLES, its kind's trail numbers packed (`_trail_packs`), its settle worked out by the shared hash (`_land`, `mote_hash` in C++), byte for byte as the GDScript (`test_native_motes.gd`, which now compares the rings' next rows and rows written). A burst with a `line` (S6 item 9) writes a row a mote, each from its own point of the line, since a row holds one ground point. Main's new mote tests read what was written through `mark()` and `motes_since()`. Suite 229 green; the mote sheets (motes, sources, flash motes, staff swap, statuses, swing trails) look as main's.
- **Measured once on the GPU build** (the owner's rule: `webstorm.sh --seconds=10`, the default storm, cap 1,000, three pairs by turns against main 7a9d254d, all six passed): frame average main 29.9 / 29.6 / 31.8 ms against the branch 30.8 / 30.3 / 29.7 (p95 80.8 / 80.8 / 84.0 against 82.6 / 82.3 / 82.6); a physics step's scripts 11.8-12.2 ms on both; the motes' CPU 0.47-0.51 ms a frame against 0.50-0.52, but the branch writes 122-125 motes a frame against 77-81 (4.1 us a mote against 6.6), since fewer bursts are cut by the frame's rows (48 against 56 a frame); the rest of the frame (renderer and the wait for the GPU) 7.8-8.3 ms against 7.7-8.1, draw calls 166 against 159 (the three rings' chunks).
- **What the web run is bound by:** the CPU. About 1.6 physics steps a frame of about 12 ms of scripts, plus 3 ms of process scripts, is two thirds of the 30 ms frame; the motes are half a millisecond of it on both sides. So the branch's saving per mote turns into more motes at the same cost, not a shorter frame.
- **So: not merged; #546 closed (the owner, 2026-11-05).** On the owner's measure it changes nothing a player would feel: no shorter frame, and the extra motes are motes the frame's rows cut on main. It costs three rings, 12-bit packed numbers and a hash mirrored in GDScript, the shader and C++, and on the desktop's Intel UHD it drew the storm's bursts 0.5-0.65 ms slower (2026-11-04, not re-measured: the owner measures on the web build now). If the motes' count becomes the limit (S5's tailless pools want more births in busy fights), the branch keeps the build and this merge, and FRAME_ROWS is the cheaper lever to try first.

## 2026-11-05: Every moment a cue: swing, beam and status particles as motes (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 10, 11, owner session)

- **Feel's own drawings give off births, in the trails' format** (item 10): a swing's sparks and card particles and a beam's splash and card particles were quads inside `shot_swing.gdshader` and `shot_beam.gdshader`. They are births now (`gameplay/feel/births.gd`, the spectacle's `births`: SPARK_BIRTH_STRIDE floats at the SPARK_* offsets, the projectile manager's own), given off by `Spectacle.step` after the trails' through `Motes.give_off`. So there is no new writer: the native MoteKernel writes them where it is built (no C++ change, so nothing to mirror), and a birth's tag gives its spell's cards' motes, as a trail's does (`CarrierBeam.beam_tag` is new; `swing_tag` was there). The shaders keep what is a shape: the swing's line and its light (two quads, 223 to 133 lines), the beam's pieces and their light (no splash, splash light or particle quads and no path search in the vertex stage, 360 to 217 lines).
- **Where and how many, from what the shaders drew:** a swing's sparks are given off as its tip passes each stretch of the arc that hits (`swing_turned` between two frames), flung outward from the line, a quarter in the infusion's ramp as the line's pixels; its card particles (4, 2 more a card kind, at most 12, as the shader's) back the way the tip came. A beam: `BEAM_SPLASH_RATE` (36) sparks a second thrown back off where it lands while more than `BEAM_SPLASH_FADE` (0.3) whole (the shader's 6 sparks of about a sixth of a second each, hidden below 0.3), and each card particle `BEAM_PARTICLE_RATE` (3.25) times a second from a random point along it, out to a side. A beam shown whole (`form_shown`) gives off the same.
- **16 sparks a sweep, not the shader's 28:** a spark mote glows and starts white-hot, and 28 of them thickened the swing's line into a band (`swing_trails` sheet, before and after); 16 read as the one-pixel sparks did.
- **A status is its element card** (item 11): `FeelPreset.status_looks` (a colour and a look kind a status) is gone; a status is shown as the element whose `status_id` it is (found once in the registry, through `ElementBase.status_look()`, as a form's looks through `look_cues()`: gameplay reads no card field): a glow in its `tint` that follows the body and pulses (`shot_status.gdshader` keeps only that, 48 to 31 lines, no per-pixel loop of 8 particles), and its card's motes (the kinds that name it, else its look layer's, as a shot's) from a slow emitter round the body (`Spectacle.status_motes`, `status_mote_rate` 8 a second, drifting a few pixels out of an oval; one of many a frame). So a status's colour is its element's: hex is void's pink, not the old purple, burn fire's, shock storm's.
- **Freeze is chill at full depth:** no element puts freeze on (combat makes it of three chills), so `FeelPreset.deep_statuses` names the status it deepens from (`freeze: chill`, frost's) and it is shown at full depth: its glow `status_deep_white` (0.75) of the way to white and `status_deep_motes` (2) times the motes. A frozen body is near-white and thick with flecks, a chilled one teal and sparse (`statuses` sheet). The map is the preset's (a look of combat's rule), not a field on the card: combat, not the card, knows that three chills freeze. `status_order` (which status shows when there are several) stays the preset's.
- **Measured on the GPU build** (the owner's rule and limit: one A/B, three pairs by turns, `webstorm.sh --seconds=10 --cards=hold,split,split,split,fire,beam`, a held fire beam split into 27; the storm's usual build has no beam or swing, so `--cards` is new, base main 13105179 with it): about 30 births a frame on the branch. Frame avg / p95 15.84 / 25.3 -> 16.06 / 31.2 ms, 16.27 / 25.6 -> 16.32 / 30.5, 15.68 / 25.4 -> 16.40 / 26.3; process scripts 2.47-2.62 -> 3.04-3.22 ms a frame (the births written by Feel and given off, the motes' own CPU 0.11-0.12 -> 0.32-0.34 ms of it); the rest of the frame (renderer and GPU wait) 9.82 / 10.36 / 9.96 -> 9.46 / 10.07 / 9.78 ms. So 27 beams' particles cost about 0.6 ms more of wasm scripts and about 0.3 ms less of the rest: about 0.3-0.7 ms more a frame in this extreme build; a fight's few beams and swings give off a few births a frame. No further rounds.

## 2026-11-05: Every moment a cue: a flash's sparks are its cue's motes, a torch lit once (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 3 and 12, owner session)

- **The flash shader draws a flash's shape and its light, nothing else** (item 3): `effect_spark` (a burst's sparks back against the shot, a cast's along the aim, a rune's all round, a poof's, a quake's rubble and dust, the motes up a rising column and out from a falling one) and the card particles are gone from `shot_effect.gdshader`, with its `shot_particle` include and its dead dash branch (no cue has shown `KIND_DASH` since S5; Feel's `KIND_DASH` and its sizes went too, the arcs keep theirs as `ARC_LONGEST`). The effect mesh is six quads (its light, a poof's four clouds, its flash) instead of 34, and the fragment stage has four small modes. Every effect is now one way: the look is its cue's parts, as the owner asked ("every moment a cue").
- **The sparks are motes parts of the cues that show the flashes**, judged by their own looks (`tests/screenshots/shot_flash_motes.gd`, before and after): `spark` back against the shot from a strike (`impact`, `impact_ring`, `expire`, `deflect`, `slam`, `shield_block`; all round from `parry` and `gem_pickup`, whose flashes face a fixed way), along the aim from a cast (`cast*`), a ring of sparks out to the rune's ring from `rune_burst` with its cards' motes, `glimmer` rising up a column pulled up (`vanish`, `dismiss`, `trade`, `revive`) and sparks along the floor from a column struck down (`appear`, `rainfall`, `conjure`, `trade`). The many-a-frame ones (a strike's, a shot's end, a cast's, a gem's) are `often`, so they leave the moments' rows alone. A quake's rubble and dust were already its cue's `surface` motes (S3). A cue that names the `poof` effect (none does since item 9) throws its own sparks.
- **A strike's card particles are its shot's shed**, not a part of `impact`: `Spectacle.shed_hit` and `shed_on_wall` already throw the shot's cards' motes back along its way (P1b), so a part would have thrown them twice. The cues whose flashes showed cards and have no shed (`rune_burst`, `telegraph`, the columns) got a `spell` motes part.
- **`bare` on a motes part** (in the caster's ramp, without the cards or the cue's tint, as a bare flash): a cast's sparks stay in the seat's colours, as the cast flash's are. It takes no `color` or `body` beside it and no `spell` kind (`CueBase` names those). In a cue with a colour of its own a bare part keeps to the ramp, as a bare flash does.
- **Measured once on the GPU build** (the owner's rule: the Web Test storm in Chrome on the real GPU, `webstorm.sh --seconds=10`, cap 1,000, three pairs against main 13105179 taken in turn; all six passed, so the smaller shader compiled in Chrome): frame time avg main 29.3, 31.9, 30.5 ms against 30.3, 28.2, 29.8 ms here; a physics step's scripts 11.7, 12.0, 12.2 against 12.0, 11.8, 11.8 ms; the motes' CPU 0.45 to 0.49 against 0.44 to 0.47 ms a frame; motes written 76 against 77 a frame (the frame's rows were full either way: 37 to 56 bursts cut short a frame on both). No difference above the noise: the web storm is the wasm CPU's, and the motes' rows were already the limit, so the extra sparks are cut where the frame has no rows left. What the shader lost (28 quads an effect through the vertex stage, the particle routine in its fragment stage) is GPU time this storm cannot show.
- **The torch is lit once** (item 12): its painted glow (`Glow` and `Core`, additive sprites) is in the group `painted_light` (`LightSpot.PAINTED`), and spell light hides that group while it is in the tree (the torches that come later too, as they are added) and shows it again when the setting turns it off. Data on the scene, no script in the room (rooms carry none). No "mote spot" embers: an emitter in a room's data would be a new system for a look nobody has asked for; the owner can ask for it.

## 2026-11-04: Online co-op later; the simulation becomes deterministic first (owner)

- **Asked:** the owner: "Real online play is wanted. I hereby grant permission to change the design, such that it is possible to add. So I think we should follow your suggestion and tighten determinism. Make a plan."
- **Done:** docs/DESIGN.md says online co-op comes later and adds "One game, the same everywhere" (same inputs, same game, on every platform; logic on the tick; nobody waits online). The plan is [milestones/online-ready.md](milestones/online-ready.md) (phase N, N1–N10), waiting for approval.
- **Why lockstep (inputs only), not sending state:** up to about 3,000 shots, 200 actors and a 500+ swarm are far too much state to send every tick; an input frame per seat is a few bytes. Lockstep needs a bit-identical simulation, which is what phase N builds.
- **Why host-ordered lockstep (later):** in plain lockstep everyone waits for the slowest machine, against pillar 2; with the host fixing each tick's frames and repeating a late seat's last frame, only the slow player suffers.
- **Revised the same day (owner: yes to the feasibility review):** a first cross-platform measurement (N2b) moves ahead of the costly parts, since whether Godot's own builds agree across Linux, Windows and the web is the plan's main unknown; settings the simulation reads, starting with the per-platform shot cap, become session settings (N11); the netcode sketch states its limits (good under about 80 ms ping, the slowest machine sets the pace, the same build everywhere).
- **The architecture, so development does not get harder (owner: "It should NOT become increasingly difficult to develop the game"):** state is declared once per script (`STATE` / `DERIVED`) and hashed and saved by one generic walker, with a test that fails on any undeclared member; one input path for devices, bots, replays and the network; time and chance only from a `SimContext`, chance as a stateless hash; the simulation/view line by folder, enforced; a per-member desync report; every card, enemy and room replayed by itself (N12). Not done: no rewrite into an entity-component system, no fixed-point maths.
- **Reviewed for cost (owner: "Will this impact performance? Will the code be worse off and harder to maintain?"):** every member is state unless listed in `DERIVED` (a forgotten cache is harmless, so most new members cost nothing); `step(delta)` keeps its signature and time and chance come from a `sim` service, with no new parameter; the bus holds HUD commands to the next tick, so the HUD is unchanged; hashing never runs in a normal build's tick; our own body movement (N6) and `DMath` (N7) only where N2b shows the platforms part. The cost table is in the plan ("What it costs").
- **N6 and N7 in C++ from the start (owner: "I think n6 and n7 should just be c++ from the beginning"):** our body movement (`BodyKernel`) and deterministic maths (`DMath`, `dmath.h`) are built in `native/` whatever N2b finds, each with a GDScript twin as every kernel has (revised the same day, owner: "So then I can't open it in Godot anymore?"): without twins the simulation would need the module, and a project opened in a plain Godot editor could not start a run. The twins are small (about 150 and 60 lines), are the fallback, and are compared with the C++ to the bit, so a game without the module is the same game.
- **Approved, with the four proposals (owner: "Go with your proposals on all four decisions"):** phase N runs after the owner lane's current work, beside D3 and C2 without holding them up, and ends before M3; hit-stop online is game-wide, counted in ticks; online the pause menu stops nothing (DESIGN.md rule 6); 60 ticks a second, fixed. The tasks are in NEXT.md's owner lane.
- **Checked against shot scale and the code again (owner: "check again that everything is covered and is compatible"):** three gaps found and added: the horde's and the swarm's generators are unseeded in a real run (A11, to N3), the level-up break and pause pause the scene tree rather than the simulation (A12, N4 stops the tick instead), and the projectile manager draws inside a simulation script (A13, a `VIEW` list, N9). Shot scale fits on five rules (T1-T5), written into both plans; the one change to it: H2's hit budget is fixed work sized once, never measured at run time.
- **Shot scale approved, in one order with phase N (owner: "Yes"):** shot-scale.md is approved with rules T1-T5; its tasks and phase N's are in NEXT.md's owner lane in the combined order, with a projectiles lane (the projectile manager and the shot kernel, one PR at a time). N7 splits into N7a (C++, before H5) and N7b (GDScript).
- **Not changed:** hard rule 4 still bans networking. The netcode is a later phase with its own owner exception, kept to one owner-only folder like `native/`.
## 2026-11-04: A burst a row: measured, not merged (spell spectacle 2, S4, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle 2: a burst a row, an experiment (S4)" ([spell-spectacle-2.md](milestones/spell-spectacle-2.md)): one row a burst instead of one a mote, merged only if the storm's CPU drops and the GPU time on llvmpipe and the Intel UHD does not grow more than that. **It does not pass:** the CPU drops, the Intel UHD pays more than that. Built on the draft branch `owner/burst-a-row` so the figures can be read again.
- **How it was built:** a row holds a burst: its ground point, birth and a 24-bit seed in full floats, its way and half spread, slowest and fastest speed, speeds up and lives as 12-bit pairs packed into three more full floats (the transform's 2 and 6 arrive as `MODEL_MATRIX[2].xy`, checked on llvmpipe), and its source, kind, count and height in the colour. The vertex shader works out mote q of the row from an integer hash of seed and q (`mote_hash`, the same on the CPU with multipliers below 2^31), so the CPU writes no per-mote numbers; a settling mote's resting point is worked out on the CPU from the same hash (`_land`), only for those. A kind's own numbers are packed once (`_kind_packs`, `_trail_packs`), so a shed or a birth packs nothing. A quad past a row's count still runs the vertex stage, so there are three rings by a row's quads (1, 4 and 16; a burst of fewer than 3 motes takes single rows, of fewer than 16 rows of 4), each cut into chunks of 1,024 quads. The settles' serials became one space per ring (`Marks.forget_settles_before(serial, space)`): with one serial for every ring, a storm forgot most chips' specks before they landed.
- **The storm's bursts** (counted on main): a frame writes 156 motes in about 22 one-mote bursts (trail births), 4 of 3, 5 of 6, 4 of 10 and 1 of 32. Most motes come in bursts of 6 or more, so rows can save; but the CPU goes mostly to the calls (working out a shed's kinds, its source, the burst's set-up), not the rows.
- **CPU** (`mote_write.gd`, a call): main 1.6-2.0 us for a burst of 1, 2.6-3.3 of 3, 4.0-5.0 of 6, 5.9-7.2 of 10, 16-20 of 32, 47-61 of 100; burst rows 2.4-2.6 for 1, 2.2-2.9 for 3 to 32, 4.8-5.0 for 100. A shot's shed of 3 motes 5.3-6.6 us against 4.8. **The storm** (`sandbox_storm.gd -- --motes=32768 --fixed-fps 60`, main and the branch by turns under the benchmark lock, the quiet rounds): main 156 motes a frame for 0.47-0.55 ms; burst rows at 128 rows a frame (`--mote-rows`, new) 178 motes for 0.32-0.37 ms, at 256 rows 368 motes for 0.40-0.52 ms. So at the same motes a frame the CPU saves about 0.15-0.2 ms, or at the same CPU a storm throws 2.4 times the motes.
- **GPU** (`trail_gpu.gd -- --motes`, with a new scene `storm mix` that throws the storm's bursts every frame into the desktop ring, so a row's folded quads are paid as in a fight; two rounds each, by turns): **Intel UHD (d3d12)**: the storm mix 2.36 ms on main, 3.01 ms with three rings (4.46 ms with one ring of 16-quad rows and 4x coarser chunks, the first build); the full ring 3.21-3.28 against 3.54 ms (a live mote's vertex now unpacks its row and hashes twice). llvmpipe: the storm mix 20-30 ms on main, 16-17 ms on the branch; the full ring 44-71 against 42-51 ms (its noise is larger than the difference). The vertices a frame in the storm mix: 102,400 on main, 131,072 with three rings, 221,184 with one.
- **Again on main with S5 merged** (6d01a9e7, the branch merged with it, two Intel rounds and three storm rounds by turns): the Intel UHD's storm mix 2.36 ms on main against 2.88 ms (126,976 vertices), the full ring 3.14 against 3.41 ms; the storm's motes 0.49-0.54 ms for 156 motes on main against 0.31-0.38 ms for 178 at 128 rows (368 at 256 rows for 0.41-0.59 ms).
