- **So:** on the Intel UHD the storm's motes cost about 0.5-0.65 ms more GPU for about 0.15-0.2 ms less CPU, and the gate says no. FRAME_ROWS and the hand-written rows stay as they are on main. What would change it: a GPU where vertices are cheap (llvmpipe here, likely the NVIDIA, which d3d12 does not time); or most motes coming in large bursts (cues with hundreds of motes, S6), where a row of 16 saves 15 writes. A dead body's crumble (a mote a pixel, up to 160) stays a row a mote either way.
- **The look:** the same kinds, numbers and flights, the randoms from the hash instead of the CPU's generator, the speeds in quarter px/s and the lives in 4,095ths of LIFE_MAX (`shot_motes.gd`, `shot_mote_sources.gd` side by side with main). With a row budget, a sheet's later bursts are no longer cut, so cues show a few more chips.

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue: the staff swap (spell spectacle 2, S6 item 9, owner session)

- **Following is the cue's, for every cue on a body:** `play_cue` takes a `follow` node (`CueContext.follow`), and a flash of such a cue is shown following it at its offset from the body, as a status already was. Motes, marks and lights stay where the cue played: motes fly free, a mark lies on the floor, and a light that slid with a body would not read as a moment. Possession's rings follow their body too (`_cue_on_body`), by the same rule.
- **Along the whole staff, by a line any cue may have** (the owner on #554: "The whole staff could change potentially. So it needs to cover the whole staff, right?"): a cue's line is its way for its `size` pixels from its point; parts say where on it they play (`along`, 0 to 1) and a motes part may be thrown from all of it (`line`). The swap is played at the staff's lower end (`Actor.butt_position`: `StaffBase.butt` behind the hand, 10 px, where every head's art ends; the owner: the staff's body changes too, not only the head) along the aim to its tip, not as a swap special case: a beam's end, a wall or a sword's edge can use the same keys. `Motes.burst` takes the line (each mote from a point of it at random); the native births never pass one, so `MoteKernel` still mirrors `burst` to the bit with none. The line is the aim's, not StaffLook's 16 drawn columns: within half a column of the drawn staff. The old cloud's spot (4 px from the hand along the aim) is only the screenshot sheet's, to show it as it was.
- **A new look** (the owner, 2026-11-04: "The current poof is not that good looking. You can do better this time."): in the house style (soft glow, tiny white-hot cores, cooling tails, as the carriers have), covering the staff from grip to tip. Live: `staff_swap`, tuned with the owner (2026-11-04: half as long, no flat white over the shaft, separate motes with dark gaps, only a few white-hot cores, a small flare at the head on the swap, never hiding the face, a dimmer light): the shaft comes apart into six of the cards' motes from all along it and three sit on it as the new one forms (a motes part's new `life` key, 0.35 and 0.3 of the kinds' lives: the bright part about 0.1 s), a ring closes on the head in 0.063 s (exactly StaffLook's `SWAP_AT` of `POOF_TIME`) with two sparks there, a dim light (0.25) over the staff's middle for 0.12 s. Picked over `staff_swap_b` (a ring closing on the whole staff, glimmers and the cards' motes all along it: softer, it reads as a shimmer more than a change) and `staff_swap_c` (a ring going out from the staff's middle and sparks all along it: the clearest at 1x, but its ring is UI-like). The old cloud stays as the effect `poof` for any cue that wants it. The owner picks from `shot_staff_swap.gd`'s filmstrips (two staff angles). The face is drawn on the staff: the motes over it are few and short, so it never hides.
- **No delay key on parts:** a part that waited for the swap moment would need a queue of played cues; the closing ring's seconds time the look to the swap instead.
- **The colour of the staff, through core:** Feel asks `Spells.current_tag(actor, staff)` and `profile(tag)` (core, not another area) for the first spell's tint and look, and passes the tag on, so a motes part of kind `spell` throws that staff's cards' kinds.

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue, part three: cast, impact and hit looks (spell spectacle 2, S6 item 2, owner session)

- **A carrier names four cues, not two looks.** The plan said `cast_look` and `impact_look` become one cue id each; the code had four moments, not two: the flash on the staff when a seat casts (`spell_cast`), where the carrier shows itself (`form_shown`, the old `cast_look` `cue`), where each of its shots strikes (`projectile_impact`) and where a carrier with no shot ends (`carrier_ended`, the old `impact_look` `cue`). So `FormBase` has `cast_cue` (`cast` by default), `shown_cue`, `impact_cue` (`impact` by default) and `end_cue`, read by Feel through `FormBase.look_cues()` (Feel reads no spell field; `test_spell_field_reads`). The dictionaries and their checks (`CAST_LOOK_KEYS`, `IMPACT_LOOK_KEYS`) are gone; `test_carrier_looks` checks every cue a card names is a file.
- **The cast and impact cues are files**: `cast` and `cast_<card>` where a card's flash was its own (atom, swing, bolt and slam, beam, wall, orbit, rune, nova), `cast_gather` (a ring gathering in: rain, swap, teleport) and `cast_ring` (one flashing out: dash), each the flash `bare` in the seat's hat ramp and a 1.5 px kick back (`FeelPreset.cast_kick_pixels` is gone); `impact` (a zoom 1 burst: every flare was zoom 1 once rounded) and `impact_ring` (with a ring in the shot's colour without its cards: a new flash key `no_cards`), and `rune_burst` gains the rune's burst, so Feel's `form == &"rune"` branch and `wall_spark_size` are gone. The seconds and zooms are the old ones exactly; `form_looks` differs by 361 pixels in the nova's cast (the draw order of its flash and ring).
- **An impact cue plays on a body too.** The old `cue` of an impact look played only where the shot ended on no body (the rune's alone everywhere); the flare played on both. One rule now: a shot's `impact_cue` plays wherever it strikes, with the shot's tag (`impact_tag`, so its cards' motes and marks are its spell's). A slam's strike stays its `end_cue`.
- **A seat's cast is in its hat ramp**: `play_cue` takes a `ramp` (after `weight` and `body_color`), for a moment of a seat or of a hit body rather than of the caster's spell. A `back` or `aim` kick needs a direction: a cast with no aim kicks nowhere, as before.
- **A hit is a cue** (the plan's "S3's own special case"): `hit` (the spray and 10 `spark` motes along the way, 15 px a step) or `hit_puff` (a puff and 14 sparks all round, a ring), `size` the zoom steps times 12 px; `show_effect` no longer throws sparks for a spray or a puff, and `Feel._flash_motes`, `Spectacle.flash_motes` and the preset's `spray_mote_*` and `puff_mote_*` are gone. A flash alone throws no sparks, so the cues that drew a spray or a puff name theirs as a motes part (appear, rainfall, conjure, dismiss, the ten reactions, both deaths). New motes keys: `often` (a part of many a frame: it stops once only the moments' rows are left, as a shed; a `share` part is `often` too, one mechanism) and `body` (the body's own colour, as a flash's `body`). The moments' sparks are not `often`: a death's are thrown even when a crumble took the frame's rows, so a death shows more sparks in a storm than before (`deaths` sheet; the owner may want `often` there).
- **Cues are read once for playing** (`cue_plan.gd`): the hit and impact path plays a cue on every hit, and reading every key of every part from its Dictionary made a cue cost 9-22 us headless against 0.7 us for the effect it shows. Feel keeps a plan a cue: each part's player looked up once and the part prepared once by its type's `prepare` (`add_cue_part`'s third argument: a flash into `CuePlan.Flash`, a motes part into `Spectacle.MotesPart`); a cue with no weight skips the holds, shake and sound; `match` on another script's constants became `if` chains (about 0.7 us a flash); the pool's `kind_index` is a dictionary (it scanned every kind); an `often` part returns at once on a closed frame. Headless, a hit 10.8 -> 18 us, an impact 2.2 -> 5.1 us, a cast 2.8 -> 7.2 us (main against the branch by turns; casts are a few a second).
- **Measured on the GPU build** (the owner's rule: the Web Test storm in Chrome on the real GPU, `webstorm.sh --seconds=10`, cap 1,000, 5,947 hits on the dummies a run; main and the branch by turns; the box is shared and noisy, so runs whose motes CPU shows load, 0.58-1.19 ms against 0.46-0.51, are left out). Before the plan above (three pairs, two clean): a physics step's scripts 11.4-11.6 ms on main, 12.0-12.3 on the branch, the frame 29.1-29.3 against 29.7-30.6 ms. After it (four pairs, six clean runs): a step's scripts 11.29-11.53 ms on main (three runs) against 11.68-11.87 on the branch (three), the frame 28.3-29.9 against 30.2-31.7 ms avg, p95 68-70 against 74-77 ms. So the data-driven hit and impact cost about 0.3 ms a frame of wasm scripts in this storm (about 3%), and the frame's average about 1 ms within the noise. Further measurement waits for the owner.
- **The impact cue plays on every strike** (the owner, 2026-11-04: bodies too, the general rule; no wall-only gate, not even as data), and the cue path was made cheaper instead. Where the time went first (headless, best of seven, a hit's 21 us): the `spark` part's throw (writing its motes, about 9 us, as on main), `play_cue`'s own work about 2.4 us (its context and plan), the spray's flash about 1.5 us over the effect it shows, the motes part about 1 us over its throw. So: a flash keeps its reach in steps of its zoom and is played with no null test (a part its type could not prepare is left out of the plan), its particles test apart from the card and size gates; a motes part of one named kind keeps that kind's index, surface flag and own count for its pool and throws it directly (no kinds array, no kind reads); `play_cue` skips the unknown parts' loop when there are none. Nothing is allocated on a play (the plans, the contexts and a card's cues are kept), and a part that would do nothing returns before building its arguments (an `often` part on a closed frame, a flash with particles off).
- **The one web A/B after that** (the owner's limit: three pairs, `webstorm.sh --seconds=10` against main 79089c4c, by turns; the GPU host was faster than in the runs above, every frame about half as long): a step's scripts 10.32 / 8.48 / 10.00 ms on main against 9.39 / - / 9.43 ms on the branch (its second run never printed STORM OK in time and is left out), the frame 15.7 / 11.0 / 14.4 against 12.3 / - / 13.5 ms avg, p95 56 / 28 / 41 against 43 / - / 42 ms. In both whole pairs the branch was not slower; the cost the earlier rounds showed (about 0.3 ms of a step) is within this noise now. Two pairs is little: a later storm may still show a small cost.
- **Left as it was:** a rune's light still comes from its flash's kind (`SpellLight.EFFECT_LIGHT`): the `light` part (item 4) overrides that only where content says how a moment lights, and a rune's light is unchanged by this item.

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue, part two: a shot's end, the death puff, colours as data (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 5, 6, 14, owner session)

- **A shot's end is the cue `expire`** (item 5): its small flare is an `effect` part (`burst`, zoom 1, 0.2 s: the old `POP_ZOOM` and `POP_SECONDS`) and its motes a `motes` part with a `share` (1: the old `expire_mote_share`). A `share` makes a motes part a shed, not a moment's burst: each kind its own count times the share, split between the kinds, at its own speed and spread (what `Spectacle.shed_end` threw, now gone), as one of many a frame, so it leaves the moments' rows (`Motes.MOMENT_ROWS`) to the moments and is skipped, before its kinds are looked up, once only those are left. It takes no `count`, `reach`, `radius` or `ring` (`CueBase` says so). Which shots pop stays code: a flying or orbiting body, not one that ended on a wall (its impact showed) nor a still one (a rune goes off its own way).

- **Measured on the GPU build** (the owner's rule, 2026-11-04: the Web Test storm in Chrome on the real GPU, `webstorm.sh --seconds=10`, cap 1,000, 92,880 shots made a run, so many thousands of ends), main c6a99662 against this branch, alternated pairs, frame avg / p95: 30.05 / 76.4 -> 29.46 / 68.4 ms, 30.61 / 71.5 -> 28.44 / 71.0, 29.17 / 72.9 -> 29.91 / 73.3; a physics step's scripts 11.5-11.7 ms on both, the motes' CPU 0.49-0.51 ms on both. No change past the noise: a shot's end as a cue costs what the special case did. (One more branch run, 71 ms with every part doubled, the motes' own CPU included, was the machine and was run again.)
- **A death's puff is a part of `enemy_death` and `boss_death`** (item 6): a `puff` flash (zoom 2, the old `death_particle_size` 1.6 rounded; `particles`, so the stress benchmark's particles-off run leaves it out) with the new flash key `body`: in the own colour of the body the cue is of, in no one's ramp and without the cards (as the old puff, whose core picked the nearest palette ramp). `play_cue` takes that colour (`body_color`, kept on `CueContext.body`); a cue with none draws it plain white. `_upgrade_hit` is gone, and with it "one slot": the killing blow's spray no longer turns into the death's puff, so a kill shows the blow's spray where the shot struck and the cue's puff at the body (two effects where there was one; an effect slot is cheap, and the cue is the one place a death's look lives). The blow's sound still gives way to the death's (its flash still showing at that spot). `FeelPreset.death_particle_size` is gone (the punchy preset's 1.8 also rounded to 2).

- **Colours as data** (item 14): an enemy's own colour is its data, `EnemyBase.hit_color` (ember, the old `FeelPreset.enemy_hit_color`, by default; `DEFAULT_HIT_COLOR` also for a body with no enemy data: a swarmer, a stand-in). Not "an enemy's ramp" as the plan said: every enemy shares one ramp (`RAMP_ENEMY`, Blight ochre, its shots' colours), and the decision of 2026-10-28 ("No enemy special case in Feel") keeps a hit on an enemy's body in its own colours, not its spell's; a colour per enemy keeps every hit looking as now and lets a new enemy bleed its own. The hit's spray reads it at the hit (`Feel._hit_color_of`: a type check and a field), the death passes it as the cue's `body_color`. The horde gems' look is `gameplay/horde/gem_look.tres` (`GemLook`: core, glow and edge colours, size, breathing), beside the horde's curve, not under `content/`: there is one gem look, not a kind of content the registry lists.

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue, part two: new moments and a light part (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 13, 4, owner session)

- **New moments are sent by their areas as `cue_shown`** (item 13), not heard by Feel from the areas' own events: flow `wave_start`, horde `level_up` and `gem_pickup`, player `revive`, combat `shield_block` and `pull`. Feel learns no new event and no area knows Feel; a moment with no cue file shows nothing. Each is played as the actor it is of (the wizard's, the shielded actor's, the puller's), so `bare` parts are in its ramp.
- **The throttle is the cue's** (`CueBase.every`: the least seconds between two plays for one actor, parts and weight left out whole), not code in the horde: one rule for any moment that comes by the hundred, tuned in the file. The horde sends at most one `gem_pickup` a wizard a step (`HordeGems.picked_by`), and the cue says 0.08 s. Reactions keep their own repeat rule (their parts still play; only the weight is throttled).
- **"A magnet pull" is a body newly caught** by a pull (combat's `pull`, which only the magnet card asks for): a moment per body, not per magnet shot per step. "Newly" is not pulled in the last `PULL_GAP_STEPS` (6) steps, since a magnet beyond `MAX_PULLS_PER_STEP` pulls every other step; `pull`'s `every` 0.1 s keeps a magnet in a crowd to a few rings a second.
- **A level-up shows behind the pause:** the level-up's break pauses the tree at once, so its cue stands frozen under the picks until the break ends. Left so for the owner to judge (the look could instead play on `horde_break_changed(false)`).
- **A `light` part** (item 4): strength, reach or radius, seconds and a colour chosen as a flash's, played by the spectacle into a batch of the spell light's own (64 lights, rows written each frame only while one lasts: they fade, so they cannot be handed over like the effects' rows). **"The part overrides the default" is per cue:** a cue with a light part lights by its light parts alone, and the flashes it shows are coded unlit in their rows, by the sign of the age beside the kind (the free half of the code's range), so no row is written twice and no flag needs a new channel; the effect shaders read the age either side (`coded_age` takes its size). A cue without one lights as before, by its flashes' kinds (`EFFECT_LIGHT`). The heal (44 px green), the level-up (64 px gold, 1.2) and the revive (48 px, its ramp) light by their parts; a rune's burst is still Feel's impact effect, so its light waits for item 2's impact cues.
- **Starter looks only**, from existing parts (rings, columns, bursts, glimmer motes), and sound ids with no `.ogg` yet (silent): the owner tunes them (`shot_moment_cues.gd`).

## 2026-11-04: Every moment a cue, part one: reactions, possession, arcs (spell spectacle 2, S6 items 1, 7, 8, owner session)

- **Reactions are cues** (item 1): one file a reaction, `content/cues/reaction_<id>.tres` (eight rows' ids and lightning's `discharge`), and a plain white `reaction` for an id with none. Not a `ReactionRow.cue`: a reaction has two rows (one each way), and the rows are combat's data, while how it looks is Feel's; a name by convention keeps both apart and needs no core change. Ten `FeelPreset` fields went (`reaction_colors`, `reaction_power`, `reaction_seconds`, `reaction_shake`, `reaction_stop_seconds`, `reaction_burst_size`, `reaction_ring_size`, `reaction_sound_prefix`, `reaction_mote_count`, `reaction_mote_reach`) and so did `Spectacle.reaction_motes`.
- **A cue's own `color` and `power`** (CueBase): the plan put a `color` on each part; a cue-level colour is one line a file instead of three, and it is also what Feel tints the reaction's name with (the name stays code). A part's own `color`, `plain` or `bare` still wins. `power` is on the cue (not a `play_cue` argument), so a reaction's strength is data next to its colour, and the rule of what it scales is written once (`play_cue` for holds, shake and punch; the part players for sizes, counts, reach and a mark's radius): never seconds, nor an arc's length (the distance between two points). A fixed `zoom` may now be a fraction, rounded after the power, so every reaction's puff and ring keep their old zoom exactly (2.2 and 2.9 for the old `reaction_burst_size` 2.2 and ring 0.9 x 2 x 1.6); the sheets `shot_reactions` before and after are the same to the pixel.
- **The repeat throttle stays code**: `play_cue` takes `weight` (false: the parts only), so a reaction repeated within 0.3 s draws again without a second hold, shake or sound, as before.
- **Motes parts** gained `color` (an exact colour, like a flash's) and `radius` (fixed pixels for a cue with no size; a reaction's ring flies 22 px times its power).
- **Possession** (item 7): `possess_start` and `possess_end`, played on the possessed actor's drawn body with it as the cue's target (so a cue could hold it, `hold_target`); the rings are `bare` (the driver's ramp, no cards). The `shot_possess` sheet is the same to the pixel.
- **Arcs** (item 8): the `arc` effect part was already there (S5's lightning dash). A discharge plays `discharge_arc` (its colour is the cue's; `FeelPreset.arc_seconds` is gone). A zap is sent as `form_shown` with the form `zap`, which is the zap card's id but no carrier: Feel's card lookup now gives a modifier that shows itself its own `cue` (`ModifierBase.cue`, the field `blast` already uses) as its cast look, so `zap.tres` names `zap` and Feel has no `form == &"zap"` branch. Not a new `cue_shown` from core/spells: three senders and the tests that count zaps by `form_shown` would change for the same result. A spark into the air (22 px or less) burned out at 0.6 of a zap's time; that is two arc parts gated by size (`if_size_over`, `if_size_upto`: new effect keys any cue may use), not a number in code. The `shot_arc_looks` sheet now plays the cue; its plain row takes the ramp of an actor that is not open (the jags differ run to run anyway: the arc shader rolls them on the clock).

## 2026-11-04: The motes' trail births in native code (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner, after the "Tailless motes" dev slider (2048/4096/8192, #547): "Build the native code now." The births were the motes' part that grows with the pool (S5: at 3,000 shots the births' CPU 1.8 ms at a pool of 2,048 and 2.9 ms at 4,096).
- **Done:** `MoteKernel` (`native/src/mote_kernel.cpp`) writes a frame's births in one call, as `Motes.give_off` and its one-mote `burst` do, to the bit (`test_native_motes.gd`: two pools side by side, every chunk's rows, counters, random state and settles compared after each frame; a mutation in the settles' point and one in the angle's rounding each fail it). A spell's kinds come from a script (`card_kinds.gd`), so the kernel first names each birth's (tag, kind) pair (`key_births`, a frame's births come from few spells) and GDScript looks each pair up once; the marks of the settling motes come back in a list for `Marks.settle`. The chunks the frame's rows can reach are made first in GDScript (`_ready_chunks`; the kernel makes no nodes). Cues, hits and deaths still write through GDScript `burst` (tens of motes in a call, a few calls a frame).
- **Measured** (headless, 400 births, the best of 40): GDScript 948 us (2.37 us a birth), native 107 us (0.27 us). The GPU cost of the motes is unchanged: at a pool above 2,048 the GPU may now be the limit (llvmpipe at 3,000 shots: 31 ms at 2,048, 39 at 4,096).
- **Owner's file:** a new kernel class is one line in `ci/smbot/rules.py` (`NATIVE_CLASSES`); the build profile needed nothing new.

## 2026-11-04: Do shots need tails? The ceiling, the motes with the tails' budget, a Tails switch, and the dash as cues and motes (spell spectacle 2, S5, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle 2: do shots need tails? (S5)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle-2.md), steps 1-3 and 5; step 4 (what the game keeps) is the owner's pick after playing with the switch.
- **The switch:** `ProjectileManager.set_tails_enabled` (`tails_enabled`; the event `shot_tails_changed`, the pause menu's Settings row "Tails: On/Off", saved as `shot_tails`, default on: `ShotTails` in core/settings). Off: a flying shot opens no trail row, so no path is sampled (`_sample_trails`, the native `sample_shots`) and no `trail_data` is uploaded; the rows in use and the cooling ones are given back at once (a dash keeps its row: its births need its path). A shot is drawn with the head-only mesh (`TAILS_NONE`: the core and its light, 2 quads; the trail meshes are now `trail_mesh(tails, shape)` with `TAILS_NONE/THIN/FULL`), and a shot with no row carries the way it flies in its instance where the row's numbers were (`_write_trail`, the native kernel to the bit; this also turns the core of a shot that found no free row, which was always drawn flying right). Lightning's jagged line goes with the tails (it is drawn along the path). Its births are rolled with no row.
- **The ceiling** (`tests/benchmark/trail_gpu.gd -- --trail-motes [--no-tails] [--tailless-cap=N]`, llvmpipe on WSLg, runs under the shared GPU lock and taken in turn, medians; a first pair under heavy load from other sessions was 3-8x slower and is left out). With the births held at today's pool (`--tailless-cap=1024`, the same motes either way):

  | shots | GPU on | GPU off | vertices on / off | `update_draw` CPU on / off |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 60 | 13.5-17.8 ms | 5.8 ms | 65,892 / 360 | 0.28-0.34 / 0.24 ms |
  | 400 | 17.7-20.0 ms | 10.4 ms | 24,060 / 2,400 | 0.86-0.92 / 0.46 ms |
  | 3,000 | 57-69 ms | 27 ms | 174,288 / 18,000 | 2.0-2.5 / 0.78 ms |

  In this scene the spark pool already leaves the tails no share from 400 shots on (`trail_share` 0: no tail pixels, only the newest pieces' lights), so in busy fights the tails are mostly gone today; what still costs is the head-light, piece-light and jag slots of 6,272 trail instances (the cooling ones too) and the path upload. The headless storm (cap 6,000, native, two pairs): a frame 8.29-8.46 -> 7.05-7.10 ms, a physics step's scripts 10.0 -> 9.3 ms (the sampling), process scripts 2.1 -> 1.3 ms (the upload), `update_draw` 0.33 -> 0.16 ms, static memory 143 -> 117 MiB.
- **The motes with the tails' budget (step 2):** with the tails off the births' pool is `tailless_particle_cap` (`TAILLESS_PARTICLE_CAP`) instead of `particle_cap`. Measured at 1,024 / 2,048 / 4,096: births a frame 74 / 145 / 176 at 400 shots and 69 / 145 / 287 at 3,000; GPU 10.4 / 13.5 / 15.8 ms at 400 and 27 / 31 / 39 ms at 3,000 (the motes' chunks: 9 / 18 / 30 shown); the births' CPU (`update_draw` plus throwing them) at 3,000 1.2 / 1.8 / 2.9 ms, against 2.4-2.9 ms with tails on. **2,048 it is:** twice today's births in a busy fight (a fight of thousands keeps more of the look of a fight of tens, the owner's wish), still about half the GPU of tails on and less CPU; 4,096 overruns `Motes.FRAME_ROWS` (256) and costs as much CPU as the tails did. The storm with tails off throws 29 births a frame for 12.5 (the motes' CPU 0.53 -> 0.78 ms a 60th of a second), well inside the frame it saved. GDScript writes the births; the native module writing them is not needed at these counts.
- **The dash (step 5):** its moments are the dash card's cues: `dash_start` (its `cast_look` `cue`, at `form_shown`: a few `dust` motes at its feet, `below` 8, in the room's colours; for lightning an `arc` along its way as far as it goes, `if_layer` 5) and `dash_land` (its `impact_look` `cue`, at `carrier_ended` from `CasterMoves.end`: a ring in its caster's ramp without its cards, `bare`; its element's flare, `if_cards`; its cards' motes; and its cards' floor mark, a mark part with `cards` and no `mark`, so frost leaves frost and a plain dash nothing). Feel's dash state (`_dash_from`, `_dash_core`, `_dash_look`), its `caster_move_finished` listener and `DASH_SECONDS` are gone. The arc is drawn at the start along the dash's way (the cue knows where it leaves, not where it will land), where the old one was drawn at the landing from a start kept per budget (the enemies shared one, so one enemy's arc could start at another's dash). Carriers now set the runtime's `cue_tag` round `form_shown` and `carrier_ended` (`CarrierKit.set_cue_tag`; the dash and every move's end) so those cues throw their cards' motes (S2). New cue keys (`CueBase`): `bare`, `if_cards`, `if_layer`, the `arc` effect (drawn along the cue's size times its `reach`), and the motes part's `below`.
- **The dash's path is motes:** every dash gives off `DASH_STREAKS` (2) streaks a moment as births of the look kind `SPARK_STREAK` (-1), from beside its body back along its way in its caster's colours (look 0), thrown as the mote kind `streak` (`MoteKind.dash_path`: a kind any dash gives off, belonging to no card; a 3-pixel line along its flight). A dash's trail is no longer drawn at all (both draw loops skip `TRAIL_DASH` rows; its two batches stay empty with a one-quad mesh), and the trail shader lost the streak code (`Streak`, `STREAK_*`, `TF_DASH`, the search by time). Sheet: `tests/screenshots/shot_dash_looks.gd` (it now steps Feel once a frame: its own `_process` stepped it a second time, so every Feel effect aged twice as fast in the sheet).
- **For the owner to play:** Settings, "Tails: Off", in the sandbox and a horde fight, enemy shots included: whether a shot's way still reads from its core and its motes, at 4 players. Then step 4: no tails, short tails, tails only on enemy or fast shots, or tails below a shot count.

## 2026-11-04: One particle system (spell spectacle 2, S3, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle 2: one particle system (S3)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle-2.md) after the owner's Q1 ("Particles off" may become a minimal ring: "Yes!"), and two later asks: a dash's particles are motes too ("Can the dash also just be motes? (Since a dash can also carry elemental effects)"), and a death need not look the same as motes, only good (keep the old one only if the motes look clearly worse).
- **No Off:** `MoteCap.STOPS` are Minimal 2,048, Low 8,192, Medium 16,384, High 32,768; a 0 (a saved Off) is Minimal (`set_mote_cap` clamps to the stops; the pause menu snaps a saved 0 to the nearest stop). The motes always exist, so every "motes off" fallback is gone: the chips effect (`KIND_CHIPS`, a wall's and the quake's and slam's stand-in) and the preset's `wall_chip_size`. `use_motes(null)` stays for the stress benchmark's "no particles".
- **Trails:** the trail shader draws no card particles (its LOOK TABLE of 25 rows, `spark`, `trail_kind`, the WINDOW table, `spark_stride`, `particle_share` and the quads `TRAIL_Q_SPARK_LIGHT`/`TRAIL_Q_SPARK` are gone); it keeps the tail, lightning's line and a dash's two speed streaks a moment (`streak`, the old dash streak row's numbers, pixel for pixel the same lines). The meshes have no particle slots at any count, so `spark_motes_below`, `sparks_as_motes`, the stride and `spark_slots_for` go. `SPARK_RATES` is the rates' only home now (the shader's TIP table is gone).
- **A dash's particles are births:** a dash following its caster gives off its cards' kinds as births like a shot's (`_give_off_dash_sparks`; one writer for both, `_add_birth`, so S2's tag goes in one place), at `DASH_SPARK_RATE` (1) of a shot's rate and its embers and flecks from its feet at `DASH_FEET_RATE` (0.5). The shader gave them off at twice and always; as motes, which glow and live longer than its dots, that read twice as heavy on the sheet (`dash_looks_4x`), so half. A dash of no cards gives off none (its streaks only).
- **Sprays and puffs:** the effect draws only their flash (the cross, the dotted puff, the light); their sparks are motes thrown with it from `show_effect` (`_flash_motes`: the preset's `spray_mote_kind` spark, 10 along the hit within 92 degrees out to 15 px a zoom step, a puff 14 all round out to 12 px a step), so every spray and puff (hits, kills, reactions, the cues `appear`, `dismiss`, `conjure`, `rainfall`) throws them. They are many a frame, so they leave the moments' rows alone (`throw_part(..., often)`). A staff's poof keeps its clouds and sparks (shapes).
- **Explosions:** the `debris` and `scorch` parts, their shader parts (`B_DEBRIS`, `B_SCORCH`, `chunk_at`, `M_PIECE`) and the blast floor batch are gone; `blast` and `fireball` throw 16 more surface motes each for the fragments, and their scorch is the mark they already stamped (cards: true). The scorch no longer glows with embers for its first second; the spell motes' embers do.
- **Deaths:** the white-hot copy that fell apart pixel by pixel (Feel's ghosts, `GHOST_SLOTS`, `death_anim_seconds`) is gone: a dead body breaks into motes of its own art (`Spectacle.break_body`, `Motes.crumble`: the preset's `death_mote_share` 0.3 of its art's pixels, `RoomBase.pixels_of_art`, read once per frame of art, times its scale's area, at most `death_mote_most` 160; each a `chip` in its art's colours shown where its pixel was at the body's rest scale, flung out from its middle at 8-46 px/s and up to 40 up, falling to its feet and settling into specks). Kept: on the sheet (`shot_deaths.gd`: a mite, an imp, a husk and a boss-sized body) it reads as the body breaking apart and its pieces lying, where the old one was a white flash gone in a quarter second. A death is a moment, so it may take the frame's last rows; four on one frame (the sheet's first try) cut most of them, so many deaths on one frame (horde) break into fewer motes each.
- **Measured** (llvmpipe on WSLg, `trail_gpu.gd --trail-motes` and `sandbox_storm.gd --motes=32768 --fixed-fps 60`, two runs each side, the C1 commit as before; the machine's load swung the "before" storm from 0.58 to 0.83 ms of motes, so ranges): vertices a frame 69,240 to 67,044 at 60 shots, 26,328 to 25,212 at 400, 175,152 to 175,056 at 3,000 (only the dashes' particle slots: a shot's were gone since P1d); GPU median 18.5-38.1 to 17.8-19.9 ms, 19.7-26.2 to 18.8-63.8 ms (one noisy run), 96-1,117 to 73-698 ms. Storm: the motes' CPU 0.58-0.83 to 0.62-0.65 ms a frame (the sprays' motes are 58 more calls a frame, most cut by the frame's rows: 156 motes written a frame either way), a physics step's scripts 9.7-14.3 to 11.0-11.6 ms. Lines: shot_trail.gdshader 760 to 583, shot_effect.gdshader 523 to 506, shot_blast.gdshader 368 to 259, feel.gd 2,423 to 2,328.
- **Not done here:** the flashes' own sparks (a burst's, a cast's, a rune's), runes' and orbits' card particles (`shot_rune.gdshader`, `particle_share`), a beam's card particles and the quake's rubble are still their shaders' (shapes and particles in one effect); they are the next candidates. A body squashed mid-hit breaks from its rest scale (as the ghost did).

## 2026-11-04: Spectacle by card (spell spectacle 2, S2, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle 2: spectacle by card (S2)" ([spell-spectacle-2.md](milestones/spell-spectacle-2.md)): the owner's Q2, "Figure this out in the best possible way!", for how a card gets motes and marks of its own when the shots' 21 look bits are all taken.
- **Looked up through the tag, nothing added to the shot.** `MoteKind.cards` and `MarkKind.cards` name cards by id; a card's kinds are those that name it, else its look layer's (the rule before S2), and a spell's are its form's, infusions' and modifiers' together (layer 0's motes when none has any). The spell's cards come from the shot's tag as plain ids (`Spells.cards_of`, new: nothing outside core/spells reads a spell's fields, `test_spell_field_reads`) and `Spells.profile` says which spell it is now; worked out once per spell in `gameplay/feel/card_kinds.gd` and kept by tag with the spell it was worked out for: a staff's generation is a ring of 8, so a tag can name a new spell after 8 rebuilds, and an entry is checked against `profile` once a frame (a rebuild is seen the next frame; only drawing waits). A per-shot effect id was the other way: an array in the manager, a write at every spawn and a field in the native kernel's layout, for what the spell table already says.
- **The tags reach Feel** as `Projectiles.impact_tag` (set with `impact_look` while a shot's impact or end is sent; 0 for a beam or a strike, also when nested in another's), `projectile_expired`'s own tag, a reaction's or a death's shot (`get_tag_of`), `Spells.cue_tag` (set while a blast's `cue_shown` is sent; the event keeps its arguments, so no listener changes) into `CueContext.tag`, and each trail birth's `SPARK_TAG`: two floats, its low 12 bits and the rest, because a spell's tag is at least 2^24 (`SpellTag.FLAG`) and a float holds integers whole only up to there. `SPARK_BIRTH_STRIDE` 10 -> 12.
- **What it costs.** Measured alone (a scratch microbenchmark, 20,000 calls, median of 7): `kinds_of_look` 0.34-0.35 us a call before S2; `motes_of` with the spell checked every call 1.8 us, which a storm's 200 sheds a frame would have made about 0.3 ms. So: (1) a shed asks its kinds only when the frame still has rows for it (`Motes.shed_closed`; in the storm most sheds are cut), (2) a spell is checked against `profile` once a frame, (3) each answer is kept for the frame by tag and look (`_seen`). Then 0.31-0.44 us a call, two spells by turns 0.37 each, the first hit of a spell in a frame about 2 us. A shot with no tag 0.54-0.56 us (one more call than before), and while no kind names a card nothing is looked up at all.
- **The storm** (`sandbox_storm.gd -- --motes=32768`, `--fixed-fps 60`, the dev staff's spells by tag, so the lookup runs: grit names a card; main and the branch by turns under the benchmark lock, four fair rounds): the motes' CPU 0.43-0.51 ms a frame on the branch, 0.55-0.56 on main; a physics step's scripts 9.4-10.8 against 9.4-9.6 ms; 156 motes written a frame on both. The branch's lead is mostly that a cut shed no longer goes through the timed pool's call (`shed_closed` is not timed; about 170 a frame), so call it even. (Main's first runs had no native module, their physics step twice as long; left out.)
- **Every card keeps its motes.** `tests/gameplay/feel/test_card_kinds.gd` plays every form, infusion and modifier of the content that no kind names, alone and with fire, through `motes_of` against `kinds_of_look`: the same kinds in the same order. A trace of a fixed list of events (S1's, plus hits, walls and cues of five spells of cards no kind names, by tag, with a spell runtime that knows them), hashing every mote row with its kind written as its id, every mark row and settle, and Feel's effect slots, matched main at all 7 checkpoints (with the fireball's new ash left out); with heavy in one of the spells the branch's hash changed and main's did not, so the trace sees the lookup.
- **No kind limit for motes.** The kinds' numbers are a texture, a row a kind (`kind_table`, RGBAF, 3 texels: motion, sway, shape), read with `texelFetch` in the vertex shader in place of three uniform arrays of 16; `MAX_KINDS` 256 and `KIND_CODE` 255, so a row's kind is one byte step (a renderer that keeps the row's colour as bytes reads it back whole). `mote_flight` takes the row's motion and sway instead of an index (the GDScript twin's lines are unchanged; the test pins the new shader lines). On llvmpipe (`trail_gpu.gd -- --motes`, three rounds by turns) the ring full at 32,768 motes is 58-68 ms a frame against main's 70-76, at 8,192 21-22 against 20-23: the same within the noise (a vertex reads three texels where it read three uniforms). Marks keep their arrays of 16: there are 9 and none is drawn per mote.
- **Named-only kinds and a room's kinds.** A kind that names no card, no layer and is not `surface` is valid and thrown only where named: a cue's `motes` part, or `RoomBase.surface_kinds` (empty: every surface kind, as before; a wooden room would name splinters). A `motes` part gains `colours` (`spell` or `surface`, `CueBase.MOTE_KEYS`): the fireball's new `ash` cools through the colours of what burned (the room's walls or the body's art), white-hot first so it reads on a dark scorch. It stays faint on the crypt's stone floor; its count is the knob.
- **Two kinds as examples:** `ash` (named only, the fireball cue's) and `grit` (`cards: [heavy]`: a heavy shot sheds grit where it shed layer 0's sparks, since heavy's layer 15 has no kind; sticky, on the same layer, still sheds sparks). Both are the owner's to keep, tune or drop: `tests/screenshots/shot_card_motes.gd` draws heavy with no spell, heavy's spell, fire and heavy, and the fireball without and with its ash.

## 2026-11-04: The spectacle node and cue parts by type (spell spectacle 2, S1, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle 2: the spectacle node (S1)" ([spell-spectacle-2.md](milestones/spell-spectacle-2.md)), after the owner's goal for part 2: "It should be easy to add new effects, and when adding new content, there should be minimal coupling to this code."
- **The spectacle** (`gameplay/feel/spectacle.gd`, a child of Feel): the motes, the floor marks, spell light, the ramp table they share, their four settings (each connected to its own event) and the room they are in. Feel's hit, end, wall, reaction and death handlers still decide *when* and call it (`shed_hit`, `shed_end`, `shed_on_wall`, `reaction_motes`, `body_source`) with what they already worked out, in the order they draw. The spectacle does not listen to `projectile_impact` and the rest itself: it would work out the caster's ramp a second time on every hit (hundreds a frame in a storm), and the motes' order against Feel's own cue parts would change, so the same fight would throw different motes. Feel keeps `motes()`, `marks()`, `light()`, `ramp_table()`, `use_motes`, the settings' `set_*` and their values as forwards: the tests, the benchmarks and the pause menu use them unchanged (two tests now find the pool and the light under the spectacle, and one screenshot sheet calls the spectacle's room handler). `feel.gd` went from 2,611 to 2,415 lines (`spectacle.gd` 382, `cue_context.gd` 39).
- **Cue parts by type:** `play_cue` plays each part by the player of its type from a table (`add_cue_part(type, Callable)`; none is `effect`): Feel's flashes and the spectacle's `motes` and `mark` (`Spectacle.cue_parts`). `CueBase.PART_TYPES` holds each type's keys and `problems()` checks keys and ways once for every type, then the type's own checks (`_type_problems`). So a new kind of part (P3b's light) is one player and one table row, and neither `play_cue` nor `problems()` grows. A part of a type nothing plays is skipped, counted (`unknown_cue_parts`) and warned of once per type: a warning, not an error, since the content test already names an unknown type, and an error line fails a test log.
- **`CueContext`** (`gameplay/feel/cue_context.gd`): what a cue is played with, handed to each part's player. Feel keeps one per depth of cues played inside a part, so a cue allocates nothing and a cue inside a part leaves the outer one's as it was. It replaces the `_cue_surface` side channel (a dying body's art colours now go in as `play_cue`'s `surface`) and the two copies of the `way` match (`way_of`).
- **Stepped settings:** `SettingStops.nearest` (core/settings) is the nearest-stop search `MoteCap`, `MoteDensity` and `ShotCap` each had a copy of. `EffectSlots` is a range with a step, not stops, so it stays as it is.
- **The same game, checked by a trace:** the screenshot sheets cannot show it, because two runs of main already differ pixel for pixel (their clocks are real time). Instead a script drove Feel through a fixed list of events (40 frames of cues, wall and body hits, damage, reactions, deaths and setting changes, then 120 frames of settling) and hashed every mote row, every mark row and settle, and Feel's effect slots, at seven points: main and this branch gave the same hashes at every point. Changing one mote count in the spectacle changed them, so the trace notices a change.
- **Not slower:** `sandbox_storm.gd -- --motes=32768`, `--fixed-fps 60`, three rounds alternating main and this branch on the same machine (another session's GPU benchmarks ran between them): the motes' CPU 0.551-0.556 ms a frame on main and 0.555-0.563 ms here, a physics step's scripts 9.41-9.45 against 9.42-9.59 ms, process scripts 2.46-2.48 against 2.48-2.49 ms. One run on each side was loaded (motes 0.80 and 0.82 ms, a step 13.7 and 14.2 ms) and is left out. A hit costs one more call (Feel's handler calls the spectacle, which calls the pool), lost in that noise.
- **Not done here:** S2 (spectacle by card), S3 (one particle system), S4 (a burst a row).

## 2026-11-04: Spell light: effects, beams, torches and a room's ambient (spell spectacle P3b, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: effects, beams, torches and room ambient (P3b)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle.md, P3), after the owner answered the plan's three questions: (1) "Yes the world can take light from spells!"; (2) a darker room: the owner followed the suggestion of a per-room ambient, 1.0 by default, and then set the **sandbox's to 0.85**; (3) enemy shots light too: "Yes". The owner then asked "why not a smooth gradient?", so the steps are not written into STYLE.md as a rule: they are a switch, and the owner picks from a sheet.
- **STYLE.md:** a line that the world may take light from spells, drawn by the game over the art (never baked into it), in the steps the light's `bands` sets.
- **The steps are a switch:** `light_apply.gdshader`'s `bands` (`SpellLight.set_bands`, `DEFAULT_BANDS` 3): 3 is P3a's three steps, pixel for pixel; 2 one step; more, more steps between `lit_from` and `bright_from`; 0 smooth, a line through the steps' corners (none to lit at `lit_from`, to bright at `bright_from`, then flat), so smooth and stepped carry the same light and the pair is a fair choice. The sheet `tests/screenshots/shot_spell_light_rooms.gd` draws a fight in the sandbox, its torches lit, at ambient 1.0, 0.85 and 0.7 in steps and at 0.85 smooth. 3 stays the default until the owner picks.
- **A room's ambient:** `RoomBase.ambient` (1.0 by default, the room as drawn), handed to the light on `room_entered` and kept when the setting is turned off and on; the sandbox's is 0.85 (the owner's), the arena's and the horde's stay 1.0.
- **Effects and beams light from the rows Feel already uploads:** at the end of `_step_effects` Feel hands the flares', the explosions' and the arcs' buffers to the spectacle, on to `SpellLight.light_effects`; each is drawn again into the light buffer as one soft light a row (`light.gdshader`, its rows read by `rows`: trail, rune, effect or spot). How much each effect kind lights at its start is data by Feel's KIND_* codes (`EFFECT_LIGHT`, `BLAST_LIGHT`: a burst, a ring, a rune's burst, a cast, a hit's spray or puff, an arc, a blast and a fireball; not chips, a dash, a status mark or a scorch), fading over its life; a test names every code. A live beam hands `light_beam` the path it just gave its own shader, drawn by `light_beam.gdshader` as a band 24 px each side, rounded past its ends.
- **Torches are data, not script:** rooms and decals carry no scripts (the room tests count them), so a light that is part of a room is any node in the group `light_spot` with its numbers as metadata (`light_reach`, `light_strength`, `light_flicker`, `light_art`, `light_tint`), read by `core/base/light_spot.gd`. The torch has a `Light` marker at its flame; its colour is its flame art's lightest common colour (`RoomBase.colours_of_art`: #ffa23c), not a number. The light finds spots through the tree's `node_added` and `node_removed` and writes their batch only when one comes or goes; they flicker in the shader. A new light source is a node in the group.
- **Enemy shots light by the same rule** as every shot (P3a already mirrored every projectile batch); a test checks an enemy shot's live head is in a light batch.
- **Measured** (`trail_gpu.gd -- --light`, llvmpipe on WSLg, windowed, main's light files against these in turn; the first pair of runs was noise on both sides, p95s of 0.5-2 s, and is left out): the light buffer's pass at 60, 400 and 3,000 shots 1.97, 2.39 and 11.10 ms before, 1.76, 3.29 and 12.27 ms after; the whole GPU frame 28.5, 39.9 and 93.3 ms before, 26.8, 65.5 and 102.3 ms after, within this machine's noise (P3a measured the same pass at 1.8, 3.0 and 10.4 ms). The benchmark draws shots only: the new lights are a few quads each (Feel's live effects, a beam's 79 quads, a room's torches: nine in the sandbox), not measured apart. Web and a real GPU are not measured.
- **Where it sits after S1** (the spectacle node, merged first, #538): the room's ambient, the light's making and the beam shader's warm-up are the spectacle's (`Spectacle.ambient`, `_make_light`, `_on_room_entered`); Feel owns the effect batches and the live beams, so it hands their rows and paths over at the end of `_step_effects` and in `_draw_live_beams`, through the spectacle (`Spectacle.light_effects`, `light_beams_begin`, `light_beam`), never to the light itself. No cue part type was added.

## 2026-11-04: Motes, trail motes and settled marks together (spell spectacle P1c + P1d + P2b, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner, on merging P1c (#535), P1d (#534) and P2b (#533): "We need to make sure that this code is good, and that it is a good addition to the project. It should be easy to add new effects, and when adding new content, there should be minimal coupling to this code." The three were built in parallel; a design review and a correctness review (fresh sessions) read the merged branch, and the fixes below are theirs.
- **Measured together first** (`sandbox_storm.gd -- --motes=32768`, `--fixed-fps 60`, floor marks on, runs alternated with P1c's branch on the same machine): the motes' CPU 1.0-1.2 ms a frame against P1c's 0.51-0.57 ms. Settling every chip (P2b) was most of it: about 2.3 us a settled mote, 1.1 in `burst` and 1.2 in the mark layer's step, and a storm settles almost every mote (chips).
- **One writer:** every row is written by `burst`. The trails' `give_off` (P1d) had its own loop, which had already drifted: it kept no chunk life (a chunk only trails wrote was hidden the next step), counted nothing, settled nothing (frost's flecks leave specks off a hit, not off a trail), and at Double "Particle amount" scaled the count past the births handed over and read stale ones. It is now a one-mote `burst` a birth (about 1 us more a birth than its own loop, measured by `tests/benchmark/settle_cost.gd`), so a trail mote is written, counted, kept drawn and settled as every other mote. **Owner to look at:** a frost bolt's trail now leaves a dotted line of specks where its flecks come to rest (one rule: a kind that settles leaves its mark however it was thrown); `fleck.settles` empty, or a frost trail kind of its own, would end it.
- **Settles are budgeted:** at most `Motes.SETTLE_ROWS` (24) a frame, what a quiet frame leaves kept up to `SETTLE_MOST` (128), and each settle takes a row of the frame's (it costs about as much), so `FRAME_ROWS` still bounds the motes' whole time. A storm leaves 24 specks a frame (1,440 a second) and skips the rest (`settles_skipped`); a blast in a quiet room settles all of its chips. A mark is drawn, never a rule, so this is a visual budget, not a limit on an interaction. The settles use `mote_land`'s own sum (`_land`, written out flat, one call a settle), no longer a fourth copy in `burst`.
- **No speck where nothing landed:** a settle carries its mote's serial (rows written since the pool was made), and each step the motes tell the layer which serials the ring has written over (`Marks.forget_settles_before`); their settles fade. On the web's 8,192 rows a storm wraps the ring in under half a second, before many chips rest.
- **The reserve holds:** a shed or a wall's throw that starts just above `MOMENT_ROWS` used to write its whole count into the reserve; it now stops at it (`_keep`).
- **`FRAME_ROWS` 256** (was 288; `MOMENT_ROWS` still 128): the trails' births come out of the rows left after the hits, so they add to what P1c measured. Measured, same machine, alternated (load 1-6, noisy): P1c alone 0.46-0.63 ms a frame; this branch without trail motes (`--no-trail-motes`) at 256 rows 0.50-0.52 (one run 0.66), the same as P1c; with the trails' 25 births a frame 0.65-0.77. So the hits, cues and settles cost what P1c's did; the trail motes add 0.1-0.2 ms in the storm, the CPU side of P1d's trade (its GPU side: 42 -> 16-26 ms at 400 shots on llvmpipe). A storm frame writes 156 motes (was 186), 25 of them the trails'.
- **Named offsets:** a row's floats are `Motes.ROW_*` and a birth's `ProjectileManager.SPARK_*`, used by the writers, the readers, the tests and the benchmarks (no slower: GDScript folds constants). `LOOK_LAYERS`, the look flags and `LIFE_MAX` come from core (`MoteKind.LOOK_LAYERS` from the manager's `LOOK_COUNT`, `MoteKind.LIFE_MAX`) instead of repeated literals; tests check `SPARK_MOMENTS` against the trail shader's `BIRTHS` and that a table source is past every spell's.
- **Drift guards:** a test holds `Motes.flight`, `_land` and the shader's `mote_flight` to the same lines (the shader cannot run headless); settle tests with a swaying kind and a drag-free one; the merged-branch bug (a chunk only trails wrote) has its regression test.
- **The stress benchmark's "no particles"** (`Feel.particles_enabled` off) no longer hands the trails their shader particles back: the trails keep no particle slots and none are thrown, so "no particles" means none.
- **The storm's report** counts births and settles where the motes take them (its second `update_draw` found no new moment and printed 0 births), and its timed pool draws at `Feel.MOTE_Z` (was 0, under the spell light).
- **Left for follow-ups** (the design review; owner's call where marked): moving the motes, marks and light out of `feel.gd` (2,600 lines) into a node of their own with cue parts dispatched from a table; a mote kind a cue may name alone; 32 kinds; one shader include for the source decode; whether a card should get motes of its own when the look layers are full (owner); folding the older particle effects (the trail shader's particles, the chips, spray and puff effects) into motes (owner: "Particles off" would be a small ring, not the old effects).

## 2026-11-04: Settled motes and the other floor marks (spell spectacle P2b, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: settled motes and the other marks (P2b)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle.md, P2).
- **Which kinds settle is data:** a `MoteKind` names the mark its motes leave where they come to rest (`settles`, a `MarkKind` id), after the owner's rule that kinds are data and easy to change (2026-11-03). Chips and frost's flecks settle as a `speck` (a new one-pixel form) in the shadow shade, the shade a mote has cooled to when it ends, so the speck takes its place with no change. Embers, sparks, wisps, murk and glimmers do not (they rise, drift or burn out); a kind that rises cannot settle (`problems()`).
- **When:** `Motes.burst` works out where and when each mote of a settling kind comes to rest as it writes it (`mote_land`'s sums in its order, read back from the row, so the very point and time it gives; calling `mote_land` itself cost 2.3 us a mote) and hands the point (the mote's pixel, as the shader floors it) and the time to `Marks.settle`. The marks keep it in a wheel of 128 slots of 1/30 s (4.3 s, past `Motes.LIFE_MAX`), at most 256 a slot, and stamp a slot once the clock is past it: never before the mote lies still, at most a slot and a frame later, under the still mote. Nothing is allocated: the wheel is one array made with the layer (1 MB). The plan's cap holds: at most `SETTLED_PER_FRAME` (512) are stamped a frame, and the rest just fade with their motes (`settles_faded`); a mark is drawn, never a rule, so this is not the deferred-never-dropped rule for interactions. The wheel learns Feel's clock even before a room, so a room's first frame settles too (a regression test).
- **One ramp table:** the motes' table of colours other than a spell's (a room's walls, a body's art) moved into `gameplay/feel/ramp_table.gd`, held by Feel and handed to both the motes and the marks, so a speck reads the very row its chip cooled through. A stamp's colour source may now be a table row, and a stamp carries a second one, its `surface`: a mark kind's part can come from the `surface` (a dying body's own art, else the room's walls). The body's art colours are read for the marks even with Particles off.
- **The other marks:** `burn_ring` (a new `ring` form) round a nova's caster; `rune_scar` (a new `rune` form: two circles and hooked strokes) where a rune goes off, from a new `rune_burst` cue named by the rune card's impact look and played on a body too (a rune lies on the floor; other carriers' impact cues still play only off a body); `ichor` (a ragged blot in the dying body's own colours, shadow with base specks; a dull sap, not blood) in `enemy_death` (6 px) and `boss_death` (14 px); `char` (a `crack` with seven jagged lines in the spell's shadow) as lightning's card mark, so a lightning blast or fireball leaves char where a plain one leaves a scorch. The plan's "a lightning strike" has no cue of its own; char comes through `cards`. Eight mark kinds of the shader's 16.
- **Owner to look at:** the sheet `tests/screenshots/shot_marks.gd` (second row: nova, rune, death, lightning blast, boss death). Char's lines are lightning's shadow, a strong gold on the crypt floor (the fire scorch's rim is as strong a red); the specks in shadow read faintly on the crypt floor, as the cracks do; ichor in the sheet takes the walls' colours (played with no body).
- **Cost:** (superseded by the integration below: settles are budgeted and take rows) measured headless on this machine, a burst of 384 chips (a whole frame's `Motes.FRAME_ROWS` when built) takes 215 us unlinked and 667 us settling (about 1.2 us more a mote: the landing sums and `Marks.settle`); a blast's 40 chips about 50 us more. Not measured: the GPU cost of a 512-speck frame (P1c's benchmarks), and moving the settling into the native module if P1c moves the mote writes there.

## 2026-11-04: Trail particles as motes (spell spectacle P1d, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: trails' shed particles as motes, an experiment (P1d)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle.md): drop the trail mesh's particle slots and throw those particles as Feel's motes, merged only if the GPU time saved is more than the CPU time added.
- **How:** while something takes them (`Projectiles.spark_motes`, set by Feel while it has motes and `particles_enabled`) and fewer than `spark_motes_below` shots are drawn, the shots' trail meshes have no particle slots (`trail_mesh(0, ...)`; a dash's keep theirs: its streaks and feet stay the shader's), and `update_draw` hands out the particles they would have given off as births (`spark_births`, `SPARK_BIRTH_STRIDE` floats: where, the way back along the shot, the look kind, ramp, look, core). Feel throws one mote a birth (`Motes.give_off`: a kind of the look kind's layer, else layer 0's, at `TRAIL_SPEED` 0.3 of its burst speed, in the shot's ramp) before its motes step. The shader's rule, rolled on the CPU: each moment (60 a second) a flying shot gives off one of its kinds, picked at random, at that kind's rate (`SPARK_RATES`, the shader's TIP.w; a test reads the shader's table) times the pool's share.
- **Rolled per birth, not per shot:** `_high` x the share tries a moment, each a random slot kept at its kind's rate. The pool's share still thins the births as it thinned the shader's particles, so a frame has about as many births as the pool's particles live (65-71 a frame at 400 and 3,000 shots, 28 at 60), whatever the shot count: the plan's worry (30 rows a second a shot, 180,000 a second at 6,000 shots) assumed no pool.
- **Measured** (`tests/benchmark/trail_gpu.gd -- --motes`, new; llvmpipe on WSLg, runs taken in turn, medians): the vertices a frame 117k -> 68k at 60 shots, 335k -> 25k at 400 (stride 1 at a share of 0.40 kept 61 particle slots and their lights a trail) and 317k -> 170k at 3,000, plus the motes' own (6 chunks of 1,024 quads at most). GPU time 22-37 -> 13-21 ms at 60, 42 -> 16-26 ms at 400, 71-114 -> 62-81 ms at 3,000 (4 more rounds: medians 85 -> 72). CPU added: the births 0.1 ms (60) and 0.3-0.6 ms (400, 3,000) in `update_draw`, throwing them 0.13-0.38 ms (0.22-0.29 once `give_off` wrote its rows itself instead of a one-mote `burst` each). The sandbox storm (`--no-trail-motes`, new; headless, so CPU only) shows no difference beyond its noise: at cap 380 a frame 6.91-6.92 vs 6.93-6.94 ms (its shots hold few trail rows, 2.5 births a frame); at 6,000, with motes at every count, 13 births a frame, `update_draw` 0.89 vs 0.81-0.83 ms, Feel's frame 0.49 vs 0.48-0.49 ms, a frame 8.7-8.9 vs 8.6-8.8 ms.
- **So it is on at every count:** `SPARK_MOTES_BELOW` is past every cap (1 << 20), a knob kept for a platform that measures otherwise. The web export and a real GPU are not measured: GDScript in WASM is slower (the births and throws could be 2-3 times the desktop's), and the vertex stage is what the Intel UHD paid most for (2026-11-02), so the web should gain more on the GPU side than it loses; the stress run on the owner's GPU (`wingpu.sh`) is the check.
- **The look is not the same:** the motes are the kinds of `content/motes` (embers, flecks, wisps, murk, sparks, glimmer), not the shader's 22 particle kinds: no particle light quads (a mote glows by its kind), nothing circles or is pulled to the shot after it is given off (void's pull, the orbiters), and a layer with no mote kind (7-21) gives off sparks, layer 0's, as does a plain shot's twinkle. Side by side: `tests/screenshots/shot_trails.gd` (motes) and `shot_trails_shader.gd` (the shader's, as before). The owner saw both sheets on 2026-11-04 and approved the motes' look ("it looks good"), so it merges on; `spark_motes_below = 0` turns it off.
- **Rows a frame:** the births take `Motes.FRAME_ROWS` like any burst (384 when built; 288 after P1c, 256 after the integration below).

## 2026-11-04: Motes measured and tuned (spell spectacle P1c, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: measure and tune motes (P1c)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle.md, P1, "Measurement"), with P2a's open GPU measurement of the mark layer.
- **How it is measured:** `sandbox_storm.gd -- --motes=N --mote-density=X` hands Feel a pool that times its own calls (`tests/benchmark/timed_motes.gd`, through `Feel.use_motes`) and prints the motes written, the bursts cut and the CPU time, a frame and a 60th of a second; run with `--fixed-fps 60` a frame is a 60th. `mote_write.gd` times each call alone (and the timer's own cost, 0.13 us a call), `trail_gpu.gd --motes` the GPU with the ring full of motes in flight, `trail_gpu.gd --marks` the mark layer with 300 stamps a frame and with none.
- **Where the time was:** a shot's hit or end sheds 2-6 motes, and the call costs more than the rows: alone, a shed of 3 motes is 4.7-5.4 us and a burst 0.46 us a mote; in the storm about 2.3-2.5 us a mote written, everything counted. The storm asks for far more than any frame writes (about 200 sheds a frame), so a frame's cost was its FRAME_ROWS (384) times that, 0.74-0.88 ms a 60th, over the plan's 0.5 ms; and a shed after the rows ran out still cost 2.5 us (its kinds worked out every time).
- **Tuned:** (1) a look's kinds are kept (`kinds_of_look`, by its layer bits) and a shed past the frame's rows returns at once (0.16 us); `_step255` and the chunk lookup are written out in `burst`. (2) `FRAME_ROWS` 288, of which `MOMENT_ROWS` 128 are kept for the moments: a shed or a wall's throw stops once only those are left, and a cue's or a reaction's `throw_part` (tens of motes in one burst, about 0.5 us each) can use them, so a blast in a storm still throws its motes. Swept at fixed 60 FPS, the CPU a frame (timer included, about 0.03 ms of it): 384 rows 0.79-0.88 ms, 256 0.57-0.70, 192 0.48-0.49, 128 0.39; with the reserve, 320/128 0.49-0.53, 288/128 0.46-0.53 (186 motes a frame), so the storm's motes cost about 0.43-0.50 ms a frame. The storm's frame with motes off 10.8-12.1 ms, on 11.9-13.5 ms (the machine's noise is about 1 ms). (3) The native module was not used: the rows are a third of the time, and the calls from Feel would stay.
- **GPU** (`trail_gpu.gd --motes`, the ring full of motes in flight, none drawn else): llvmpipe 2.4 ms with none, 16.7-23.4 ms at 8,192, 70.8-79.1 ms at 32,768 (40.2-43.8 ms with the glow off: the glow's pixels are about half of it); the laptop's Intel UHD (d3d12) 1.1-1.3 ms with none, 1.05 at 8,192 and 3.1-3.4 ms at 32,768; the NVIDIA's timer reads 0 through d3d12, so it is not measured. Tuned: (4) a mote with no glow draws a quad only as big as its shape (`Motes.reach_of`, the shader's `kind_sway.w`; was 5x5, or 7x7 turning), pixel for pixel the same sheet (`motes_kinds` compared); (5) a chunk is hidden once its bursts' longest life ends, not LIFE_MAX (4 s) after its last write, so a fight's few chunks stop drawing about twice as soon.
- **Defaults kept:** Particles High (32,768) on desktop and Low (8,192) on the web, Particle amount Full. At 186 motes a frame and lives up to 2.5 s (chips) the storm fills most of the desktop ring (30-32 of 32 chunks shown), and that full ring is about 2 ms on the Intel UHD; the web ring is about nothing there.
- **The mark layer (P2a):** `trail_gpu.gd --marks`, the arena's 704x448 layer (1.2 MB), runs of frames with 300 stamps (radius 4-24 px) and with none: the layer's pass 4.2-7.5 ms on llvmpipe and 1.2 ms on the Intel UHD on a frame with stamps; a frame with none renders the layer 0 times (72 frames) and its whole frame is within the noise of a stamping frame's (llvmpipe 4.6-5.8 ms against 4.9-6.5, Intel 1.1-1.2 against 1.1-1.3). Floor marks stay on by default on both platforms.
- **The web:** not measured well enough to set anything. The stress run in the exported Web Test build in headless Chrome here (software rendering, the native module on, motes at the web's 8,192) gave a physics step's scripts 34.4 ms avg with the renderer and 15.0 ms with the render loop off, and did not reach its third scenario within websmoke's 900 s; there is no motes-off run beside it (the stress runner has no switch for them), so the motes' share is not known. From the storm, the motes are about 0.45 ms of a desktop frame; a web build's GDScript is a few times slower. The web's defaults stay until a run on a real browser (wingpu) says otherwise.
- **Not done here:** the trails' particles as motes (P1d), settled motes (P2b).

## 2026-11-04: Bundled hit events and threads in the native module allowed (owner)

- **Asked:** the owner, on the plan for about 100,000 live shots ([shot-scale.md](milestones/shot-scale.md)): "Don't mind what has been decided earlier. Bundle hit events if needed and add threads!"
- **Reverses "no bundled events" (2026-11-02, below).** A step's hits may go out as one batch, resolved in phases (Combat, then Spells, then Feel), when the measurements show the hits are still the largest part of a step after each listener has been made cheaper (shot-scale.md, H3b). Within one step, an on-hit card or reaction then sees the state after all of the step's damage, not only after its own hit. Every changed recording is listed with its reason in the PR that changes it.
- **Threads:** the native module may use Godot's `WorkerThreadPool` on desktop (shot-scale.md, H5). Still no `std::thread` and no system calls. `ci/smbot/rules.py` and `native/build_profile.json` stay the owner's files: the PR that changes them has the owner's approval and the owner merges it. Results are merged in shot order, so the kernel stays byte-identical to its GDScript. On the web too, if the web build supports threads (owner: "If the web build supports thread, you can use them there, too!"; shot-scale.md, H5w), otherwise the web runs one chunk.
- **Damage is the requirement** (owner: "as long as the shots deal the correct damage it is fine doing whatever"): how hits are found, ordered, batched, deferred or shown may change. Each target's damage taken may not. A deferred shot no longer skips a target.
- **The target machine** (owner: "a desktop PC with medium specs"): about a 6-core desktop CPU from the last five years, 16 GB, a GTX 1660 or RTX 3050 class GPU. The desktop cap is what it holds at 60 FPS.

## 2026-11-04: Spell light: the light buffer and shot light (spell spectacle P3a, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: the light buffer and shot light (P3a)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle.md, P3). Effects, beams, torches and a room's ambient are P3b, after the owner's answers to the plan's P3 questions; STYLE.md is unchanged (question 1).
- **No multiply blend:** the plan's rect was `blend_mul` outputting `ambient + light`. Checked on the compatibility renderer (llvmpipe): a 0.4 grey under a `blend_mul` rect of 1.5 stays 0.4, because the factor is clamped to 1 in an 8-bit target (the web's too), so a multiply can only darken and ambient 1.0 would never show light. The rect instead reads the screen under it (`hint_screen_texture`, one copy of the 640 x 360 view) and draws it times `ambient + light` (`light_apply.gdshader`); where the light is below the first step and the ambient is 1.0 it draws nothing (`discard`), so the room is pixel for pixel as it was. Light added on top was the other way, and it washes a dark floor towards the light's colour instead of lighting the floor's own colours.
- **The steps:** by the light's brightest channel, below `lit_from` (0.16) nothing, from it `lit_gain` (0.22), from `bright_from` (0.45) `bright_gain` (0.45), each in the light's hue (the light over its brightest channel). The buffer is sampled linearly, so a band's edge is cut at the world's pixels, not the buffer's 4-pixel texels.
- **Mirrored rows, not a shared MultiMesh:** the plan had each light batch share its shot batch's `MultiMesh`. A MultiMesh holds its mesh, so the light would run the trails' mesh, hundreds of vertices a shot, drawn or folded (2026-11-02: that vertex count is the trails' cost): as many vertex stages again as the trails. Instead `ProjectileManager.mirror_batch(batch, multimesh)` gives a MultiMesh of the light's own (a quad) the batch's instance count, and each frame the very `PackedFloat32Array` the batch uploads: no row is written twice, four vertices a shot. Measured: `update_draw` 9.2-10.9 ms without and 9.4 ms with at 3,000 shots on this machine, within the noise.
- **Where:** `Feel.LIGHT_Z` = 15 (`MOTE_Z - 1`). The bodies and the room's art are at 6 or below (pillar tops 5, the dummy 6); the motes (16), floor flashes (17), beams and rings (18), flashes (20), ghosts (21), reaction names (22), entities (40), shots and effects (50) are over it. `test_light.gd` walks the scenes and Feel's and the manager's nodes. The floor mark layer (P2a) must sit under it to be lit.
- **Measured** (`tests/benchmark/trail_gpu.gd --light`, llvmpipe on WSLg, the compatibility renderer): the light buffer's own pass 1.8 ms at 60 shots, 3.0 ms at 400, 10.4 ms at 3,000 (about 3,000 soft quads of 16 x 16 texels, added); the main view's frame 1.2-1.9 ms dearer at 60 shots (the screen copy and the full-view pass), lost in the noise at 3,000 (85-99 ms either way); a whole frame at 60 shots 32.6-33.2 ms without, 35.8-36.6 ms with. On a real GPU these passes are a few hundred thousand pixels; the web export and a real GPU are not measured yet. Both platforms default to on (`SpellLightSetting`); the web's is the first to turn off if its stress run says so.
- **Not done here:** effects, beams, torches and the room's ambient (P3b); the ambient is fixed at 1.0 (`SpellLight.set_ambient` exists for the screenshot's dev look at 0.7).

## 2026-11-04: Motes: where they come from (spell spectacle P1b, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: mote sources (P1b)": the plan's events table, the `motes` cue part, motes in the existing cues, `mote_density`.
- **No new kinds:** the plan named `ash`, `splinter` and `shard` besides the first ones. With the owner's rule that kinds come from the cards' look layers (P1a), a cue's motes are its cards' kinds (`kind: spell`, so a fire blast throws embers and a frost one flecks) or the surface kinds (`kind: surface`, chips); a part may still name one kind by id. `shard` is P1a's `fleck` (frost); ash and splinters wait until a card or surface needs them.
- **What breaks sheds its own colours:** the plan's "splinters and embers" for a death became one rule with the walls: a body that dies is a surface that broke, so its death cue's chips take the colours of its own art (`RoomBase.colours_of_art`: the frame it shows, by the walls' rule, read once per texture and kept), and its spell motes are the killing shot's cards (`enemy_died`'s `shot`, read while the event is sent). A boss throws more (its cue's counts).
- **Sources:** a body hit sheds the shot's kinds back along its way (half their count, `FeelPreset.hit_mote_share`), a bolt or orbit whose life runs out puffs them (`expire_mote_share`), and a reaction throws a ring of the reacting shot's kinds in the reaction's exact colour (`reaction_mote_count` 16 and `reaction_mote_reach` 22 px, times its power). Blast, fireball, slam, quake, nova and both deaths have `motes` parts. P1c measures and tunes the numbers.
- **`reach` is how far they glide:** a part's `reach` times the cue's size sets the kinds' top speed so a mote of its drag glides that far (speed / drag), keeping the kind's slow-to-fast ratio, so a blast's motes land within its radius whatever kind it throws.
- **The chips effect stands in for motes,** in cues as on walls: with motes on, a cue's `chips` part is left out and its `motes` parts throw them; with "Particles" off the old effect shows.
- **The amount:** `MoteDensity` (None, Half, Full, Double; Full by default), the pause menu's "Particle amount" row, `mote_density_changed`. It scales every burst in `Motes.burst` (wall hits too); a share of a mote is thrown that often (`floor(count x density + random)`), so Half keeps the average and a one-mote burst still throws half the time.

## 2026-11-04: The trail texture holds 64 rows a line (owner session)

- **The bug:** with Max shots at 10,000 or more on the owner's web build, after the sandbox's LB storm every fire bolt's tail was short and at a wrong angle; below 10,000 never. The storm holds every trail row (`TRAIL_ROWS_PER_SLOT` x the cap = 20,000), and `trail_data` had one texture line per row (32 texels wide), so it grew to 32 x 32,768, past the 16,384 most GPUs and WebGL take, and the shader read the wrong samples from then on (the texture stays at its largest size). Not seen at the default caps (1,000 web, 6,000 desktop: 2,048 and 16,384 lines).
- **The fix:** `TRAIL_ROWS_PER_LINE` (64) rows side by side on a line: 2,048 texels wide (the smallest maximum WebGL guarantees) and rows / 64 lines high, 2,048 x 2,048 at the top cap (50,000). `trail_texel` finds row r's sample s at (r % 64 x 32 + s, r / 64). The texels are in the same order, so `_trail_data`, its writers and the native kernel are unchanged; only the image's size and the shader's address are new.
- **Not done:** the texture still never shrinks after a storm, and freed rows are reused last freed first; neither is wrong, only memory (a 2,048 x 2,048 RGBAF texture is 64 MB at the top cap).

## 2026-11-04: Floor marks: the layer, the stamp batch and three marks (spell spectacle P2a, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: the floor mark layer (P2a)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle.md).
- **Under every body means the floor moves down.** The plan put the layer's sprite at `FLOOR_Z - 1` (16), but wizards and enemies outside the horde draw at z 0, and Feel is an autoload drawn before the room, so a sprite of Feel's at z 0 would be under the room's floor. The rooms' `Floor` sprites are now at `RoomBase.FLOOR_Z` (-2) and the marks at -1 (`Feel.MARK_Z`): over the floor, under the bodies (0), the pillar bases and shade, the decals, the motes and Feel's floor flashes. A test checks every room's floor.
- **Marks are data, like motes** (the owner's rule for motes, 2026-11-03): a `MarkKind` file each in `content/marks/` (form, numbers, where each colour comes from), handed to `mark.gdshader` as uniform arrays. The shapes are computed (a ragged blot, forking cracks, a rime of specks and crosses) at any size, one pixel a line, never a scaled picture.
- **No hardcoded colour:** a part's colour is the floor's own (`RoomBase.floor_colors()`, sampled from the `Floor` sprite and multiplied by its modulate, since both crypt floors are tinted; `floor_mark_colors` overrides) or the spell's ramp, picked by the shot shaders' `look_of` as the motes do. The floor's shadow is 7 % of the crypt tile's pixels, under the walls' 10 % cut, so the floor sample keeps colours on 1 % or more (`FLOOR_MIN_SHARE`).
- **Frost comes from the cards, not a frost cue:** there is no frost cue. A mark part with `"cards": true` stamps the marks of the cue's cards (`MarkKind.look_layers`) instead of its own when they have one, so a blast with a frost card leaves frost and a fire or plain one its scorch. The blast and fireball cues leave a scorch (`cards`), slam and quake a crack.
- **Renders:** the viewport is `CLEAR_MODE_NEVER` and `UPDATE_DISABLED`; a frame with stamps sets `UPDATE_ONCE` and the next step sets `UPDATE_DISABLED` again (the node keeps the mode it was last given, so the step sets it back once the frame's render is done). The batch shows only its frame's rows (`visible_instance_count`), so the wipe of a new room (`CLEAR_MODE_ONCE`) or a later frame never draws old stamps twice.
- **A flood waits, it is not dropped:** at most 512 stamps are drawn a frame and the rest wait (up to 4,096, a ring; past that one is counted in `left_out`), after the owner's rule that interactions are deferred, never dropped (2026-10-29).
- **Memory:** one RGBA8 texel a world pixel over the projectile manager's bounds (the room plus its 32 px margin): the crypt arena and sandbox 704x448, about 1.2 MB; the horde 1344x784, about 4.2 MB. A side is at most 4,096 texels (`Marks.MAX_SIDE`); a room bigger than that (the owner's one huge map) would need a tiled layer.
- **The setting:** "Floor marks: On/Off" in the pause menu's Settings (`FloorMarks` in core/settings, saved as `floor_marks`, `floor_marks_changed`); on for the web and the desktop until P2's measurements say otherwise. Off frees the layer: no nodes.
- **Not done here:** settled motes and the other marks (P2b); the GPU cost of a 300-stamp frame on llvmpipe and the web export (measured with P1c's benchmarks).

## 2026-11-03: Motes: the pool, kinds as data, colours from spells and rooms (spell spectacle P1a, owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner-lane task "Spell spectacle: the mote pool (P1a)" (docs/milestones/spell-spectacle.md, approved 2026-11-03). After a first version with the kinds as tables in mote.gd mirrored in the shader and Stone chips, the owner: "It needs to be easy to change/add motes and different kinds should emerge from the spells", and colours "should also not be hardcoded, since the current tiles are just placeholders".
- **Kinds are data:** one `MoteKind` file each in `content/motes/` (core/base/mote_kind.gd), read from the registry when the pool is made; the shader is handed their numbers as uniform arrays (`kind_motion`, `kind_sway`; 16 kinds at most), so it has no table to keep in step.
- **Kinds come from the cards' look layers** (owner's pick: "look layer only", no new card field): a kind names the `look_layer`s it is the motes of; a shot sheds every kind its cards' layers name, its burst split between them; a shot whose layers have none sheds layer 0's. A card's particles in flight and its motes are the same thing, and a new card gets motes through the look it already has.
- **A spell's colours are the shot's:** the mote shader calls the shot shaders' own `look_of` (shot_look.gdshaderinc) with the core colour and the caster's ramp, so no palette is copied to GDScript.
- **A wall's colours come from the room's art** (owner's pick: sampled, with an override): `RoomBase.surface_colors()` takes the most common colours of the sprites on its wall bodies, leaving out the `Floor` sprite's most common colour (debris must show on the floor; the crypt's walls share the floor's Stone base), lightest first; `debris_colors` overrides it. Sampled once per room (the scene instanced off the tree), on `room_entered`. They reach the shader as a row of a small ramp table (a 4 x 64 texture).
