- **The row:** the numbers that need full precision (the birth time, the ground point, the velocities) travel in the instance transform's six used floats; the colour and custom data hold numbers between 0 and 1, the life and height in steps of 1/255 (the height in quarter pixels), because the compatibility renderer (the web's) may keep them as half floats or bytes, and the CPU's `flight` then reads what the shader does. Checked on llvmpipe in the compatibility renderer (`tests/screenshots/shot_motes.gd`).
- **Chunks made when first used:** a chunk's batch and its 64 KB of rows are made the first time a mote lands in it. A chunk's rows are an element of an Array of `PackedFloat32Array`, written in place (GDScript shares a packed array by reference, measured: no copy per write); `step` hands the whole chunk to `multimesh.buffer` once a frame when it was written.
- **A frame's rows:** writing a mote costs about 1.5 us in GDScript (21 us a wall hit), so at most `Motes.FRAME_ROWS` (384, about 0.6 ms) are written between two steps and a burst past that is cut short: a visual budget, nothing a rule reads. The plan's gate is 0.5 ms of spawns a frame in the storm; P1c measures and sets it (or moves the write to the native module).
- **One layer, not two:** the plan drew landed motes at `FLOOR_Z` and flying ones at `FORM_Z - 2`. A row cannot change batch when it lands, and two batches over the same rows would double the vertices, so every mote draws at `MOTE_Z` (`FORM_Z - 2`), under the floor marks, the beams, the flashes and every telegraph. P2b turns the landed ones into floor stamps anyway.
- **Wall hits replace the chips effect** while the setting is on; with it off the old `KIND_CHIPS` effect shows. The cue part `chips` (blast, slam) is unchanged until P1b.
- **The setting:** `MoteCap` in core/settings, Off, 8,192, 16,384, 32,768 (words Off, Low, Medium, High), 8,192 on the web and 32,768 on desktop until P1c measures them; `mote_cap_changed` (hud to feel) remakes the ring. `mote_density` waits for P1b.
- **Shapes are data** (owner, 2026-11-04: "do the shapes now"): a kind's `shape` is a multi-line text picture of up to 5x5 pixels (one row a line, frames apart by a blank line; `#` its shade, `o` the shade hotter, `.` none; owner: "make the shape frames more human readable", so not one line with `/` between rows), played over its life, looping or picked per mote, and turned with its flight (8 ways, from where it was 0.03 s before; at rest the way it was thrown). Mote.gd draws every kind's frames into a small atlas texture (5x5 squares, a kind's along its row); the fragment stage turns its pixel back by the mote's way and reads the atlas, so a new look is a new string, never a shader edit. A pixel stamp was picked over a fixed set of shapes in the shader for that reason. `big_share` went: mixed sizes are a `pick` among `#` and `##/##`.
- **Not done here:** the cue part `motes`, the other sources (P1b) and the measurements (P1c).

## 2026-11-02: Spells' hit and Feel's impacts; no bundled events (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner: how much bundling the hit events per frame would save and whether it is an "ugly" optimization; then "look into" Spells' `_on_hit` and Feel's spray.
- **Bundling, not done:** a signal and its listener cost 0.35 us against 0.02 us an item in one call a frame with arrays; a hit makes about 10 deliveries, a shot's end 2, so about 0.3 ms of a 12-15 ms storm frame. The rest of a listener's work only shrinks where it can be shared across a frame's hits, and those hits depend on each other in order (a kill, a status a reaction reads, an on-hit card's shots), so a bundle would keep the per-hit loop. Every hit event in `core/events`, its listeners and dozens of tests would change for it. What we do instead: one event per hit, and a listener that has costly per-hit work keeps it and does it once a frame (the training dummy's numbers).
- **Where the time is** (timed part by part in the storm, about 1,000 frames): Spells' hit 16.8 us (its relay 2.3, the heading and what a chain carries 4.8, its siphon, possess, pull and blast checks and the carrier's own part 5.5, its payloads 1.7); Feel's impact 13.4 us (the form's look 2.5, the manager's facts read by name 3, the burst 2.75); Feel's shot end 4.9 us; Spells' shot end 4.4 us (its blast check and the wall and expire triggers, already lean).
- **Spells' hit:** the heading goes only to a payload on hit, so it is worked out only for a spell that `fires_on(HIT)`; what a chain carries only when it has jumps left (still read before the hit's effects and payloads, which may spawn into the slot). `CompiledSpell.plain_hit()` (worked out on first use: only the compiler writes a compiled spell, before it is fired) is true with no siphon, possess, pull or blast, and `_on_hit_effects` then runs only the carrier's own part. `_relay` takes the spell `_on_hit` looked up. 11.6 -> 7.8 us a call.
- **Feel:** `projectiles` was a plain `Node`, read by name with `in` checks (`impact_form`, `impact_core`, `impact_body`, `expired_on_wall`, `tint_of`) for a manager that might not have them; every caller, test or game, gives it the projectile manager, so it is typed as one. Its shot end 3.6 -> 3.2 us; its impact within the noise. The spray itself (`show_effect`, a write of 13 arrays) was left.

## 2026-11-02: The hit's listeners: Feel and the actors (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner: "Look at both" (Feel's `damage_dealt` and the enemies' `damaged`, the largest parts of a hit after Combat's own).
- **Feel:** timed part by part in the sandbox storm (96% of its hits are repeats, which skip the flash, hold and sound): the repeat check 2 us, the hit's colours 4 (most of it the actor table), the kind's look 3, the spray, shake and sound 6. `_actor_table` found the `Actors` autoload with `get_node_or_null` on every call (the hat ramp and the hold ask for it on every hit); it keeps it while it is valid and in the tree, and looks again otherwise, as Combat's `_table` does. 14 -> 13 us a hit. Left: the kind's look read from the preset's dictionary (a cache would save about 1.5 us and go stale if a preset were edited in place); the per-hit camera refresh (the frame's shake does not look for a new camera).
- **An enemy's `damaged`:** its AI's grudge (one team check for a wizard's hit), its look's pose (changes the sprite only when the pose does) and its face's mood: 1.6 us a hit together for every stray kind. Left.
- **The training dummy's numbers:** a hit set a label's text, position, fade and visibility at once; in a storm a dummy takes several hits a frame on 8 labels, so most of it was overwritten before a draw. A hit keeps its number and queues one deferred write a frame (deferred calls run after the physics step and before drawing), which writes each new label's text once and places it by its age with the step's own formula (`_place_number`): at the hit's age that is where the hit put it, and a step between the hit and the write already moved it there. So every drawn frame is as before. A hit 2.9 -> 1.4 us at the listener; with the frame's work about 9 us less a hit.

## 2026-11-02: A cheaper elemental hit in Combat (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner: "Let's look at combat then", after the trail work found Combat's `_on_projectile_hit` at about 68 us a hit in the sandbox storm.
- **Where the time is:** timed part by part in the storm (40,000 hits, the timers themselves about 0.4 us each): the row and the spell's profile 4 us; before the hit lands 13 us, 9 of it the reaction lookup; the hit 45 us, of which Feel's `damage_dealt` 17, the actor's own `damaged` 10 (the training dummy's number label), the health 3, `hit_landed` and `actor_hurt` 5; the statuses 8 (every hit puts its status on: a frost bolt on a frozen dummy finds it frozen). So Combat's own work is about a third of a hit, its listeners the rest.
- **The reaction table indexed:** `ReactionTable.find` walked all 16 rows reading two properties of each, for every reacting status on the target, on every elemental hit. It keeps its rows by element, then by status (`for_element`; the first row of a pair wins, as the walk found it), rebuilt when the number of rows or the array changes (tests add rows to a table after it is made); a row is not edited after the table has been searched (no code does). `_reaction_for` asks only for the statuses the element reacts with, in REACTING_STATUSES' order, so the same row is found.
- **The status by its row:** a spell's hit knows its row; `_apply_status_row` is `apply_status` without the lookup of the actor (an id or a node, through a Variant), with the same checks (a body still there, not down). `_apply_spell` reads the body's position once.
- **Measured:** `tests/benchmark/combat_hit.gd` (new; Combat alone, nothing else listening; fastest of 15 passes, three runs each in turn): frost on frozen 19.8 -> 16.9 us, fire on burning 20.0 -> 17.4 us; plain 10.4 and storm on hexed 19.5 us unchanged (neither walked the table: plain has no element, and storm's statuses already reacted early in the walk).
- **Left:** the rest of Combat's hit is many small reads (the team twice, the body's validity and health checks, three events sent even with no listener) of about 1 us each. The larger costs are the listeners in other areas: Feel's `damage_dealt` and an actor's `damaged` (an enemy's AI, look and face each hear it).

## 2026-11-02: Cheaper trail vertices (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner: "optimize performance more for shots/tails".
- **Where the time is:** `tests/benchmark/trail_gpu.gd` on llvmpipe, 400 shots: the vertex stage is about 58 of 70 ms a frame (every vertex folded at once: 12 ms); a fragment stage that draws nothing changed nothing. On the CPU the sandbox storm's manager step is mostly the hits' listeners (Combat's 68 us a hit), not the shots' own work; the manager's `update_draw` is 1.3 ms at 4,000 shots.
- **The search only where it can matter:** every vertex ran the path search, though the head, the core and the pieces' lights never read it and most particle slots have no particle (not given off at this moment, or outside the trail's window). The particle's kind is now picked before the search (`spark` takes it), and only a particle that was given off, a tail pixel while the trail has a tail or a step of lightning's line searches. Still one call site of `samples_before` (Direct3D, 2026-10-29). Pixel-identical. 400 shots 76 -> 51 ms, 60 shots 33 -> 28.5 ms.
- **One triangle a quad:** a quad was a square of 4 vertices; it is a triangle of 3 (corners at the box's low corner and twice the box along x and y), and the fragment stage keeps a pixel only when its centre is in the box (left and top edges in, right and bottom out, as a square's two triangles take them; where the GL renderer's edge rule would differ, a pixel exactly on a box's edge is at the end of a light's falloff or beside a pixel quad's or line's, so it is 0 either way). Pixel-identical. Vertices a frame -25%; 400 shots 51 -> 47 ms (the triangles cover twice the area, so part of it goes back to fill).
- **Measured and left:** the trail's window (a 21-step loop over the look bits per particle slot) from the CPU in the free `INSTANCE_CUSTOM.a`: no measurable gain, and it would change the native kernel too. 3,000 shots are not faster on llvmpipe (the pool has already thinned their meshes to a few slots; what is left is per instance). Points (one vertex a quad) were not tried: point sizes are capped on some GPUs, and a light's quad is many pixels wide on a large screen.

## 2026-11-02: Sandbox storm, a launch's shots made together, repeat hits (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner: a benchmark of many shots that hit, with the sandbox's dev staff (splits and novas) at dummies, "for you to use"; then the batched spawn "if it will give more performance".
- **The benchmark:** `tests/benchmark/sandbox_storm.gd` holds the sandbox's Y build (Flow.SANDBOX_START_CARDS) among the training dummies and prints the shots, the hits on each dummy, the frame and (`--profile`, `--profile-signals`) each script's and listener's time. What it found (headless, ~12,600 shots made a second, ~60 hits a step): making the paced on-expire shots was ~19 us a shot (7.5 in `spawn`, 11.5 in `Spells._land_shot`), 5.6 ms a step; a hit on a dummy ~68 us in Combat's handler, 23 us of it Feel's.
- **A launch's shots made together:** the atom carrier (atom, bolt, nova; the only carrier with the plain `shoot`) spawns a launch with room for all its shots in one `ProjectileManager.spawn_batch` (the caster's team, ramp and budget read once) and sets them up in one `Spells._land_shots` (the spell's behaviours, looks, echo, caster shot and trigger rows read once; `set_looks`). The same shots, headings (the spread's draws come first, in order), slots, records and order of setup; `test_spells_land_shots` compares every array of the manager and the runtime, byte for byte, with the batch and one shot at a time, and fails when one setter is left out. With no room for all (at the cap a spawn drops a shot, which could be one of the launch not yet set up) it goes one by one as before. Storm: Spells' step 5.6 -> 2.3-2.4 ms, the frame 33 -> 14-17 ms (catch-up steps 1.7 -> 0.9 a frame). The row writing stays GDScript; a native batch would cut the rest of the 7.5 us.
- **Every carrier (owner: "Make sure it works for all carriers"):** the batch is the base carrier's (`Carrier.launch`): a carrier whose shot is one pool row set up and nothing more (`rows_only`: the atom, the rune's still shots, the orbit's circling ones) makes a launch's rows first, in order (`spawn_row`, the spread drawn per shot as before), then sets them up in one `_land_shots`; the atom also spawns its rows in one `spawn_batch`. The others do more per shot (a beam's ray, a swing's sweep, a slam, a body of its own) or launch their own way (moves, wall, rain) and go one at a time, untouched. `test_spells_land_shots` casts every carrier card, from a staff and as a payload of a shot, with the batch and one shot at a time, at the desktop cap and at a small one, and compares every array; setting up one shot too few in the base carrier's batch fails it in both sets.
- **One implementation (owner asked whether it made the code worse; it duplicated the setup):** `_travel_shots` and `_register_shots` are the only setup; `_apply_travel`, `_register` and `_land_shot` are their one-slot forms (`_one_slot`), so a new behaviour is added once. A carrier's batch is one path, `Carrier.launch`, with `spawn_rows` as its hook (one `spawn_row` per heading by default; the atom's makes them in one `spawn_batch`, worth 3.3 -> 2.4 ms of Spells' step in the storm, so it stays). `test_spells_land_shots` casts every card in the registry (three sets of 16 staffs) and fails for a card no staff casts; it gets one shot at a time from a manager of its own whose `has_room_for` says no (the path a launch takes at the cap), so no switch is left in the game (`native_constants` now reads the manager's constants through a script that extends it, so such a manager keeps the native kernel); setting the records up in reverse, or the atom's rows in reverse, fails it in every set.
- **Repeat hits:** a hit that repeats the last one Feel flashed, held and heard (same actor, body, attacker and kind, no physics frame or Feel step between) skips those three; its spray and shake stay (`test_feel_repeat_hits`). Feel's hit 23 -> 15 us. The training dummy hears `Actor.damaged`, not every hit on the bus.
- **Next, measured:** Combat's own hit path (~45 us a hit: four events and the element logic), Feel's impact and expiry effects (~2.6 ms a frame, past the 256 effect slots).

## 2026-11-02: Trail meshes by shape: lightning and dashes in batches of their own (owner)

- **Asked:** the owner: "Can the shot trail be made cheaper without losing its effect?", then to do it (one trail mesh per kind of trail), and the per-trail work on the CPU and several tail pixels per quad "if it is worth it".
- **What costs:** every quad of a trail's mesh runs the vertex stage, drawn or folded (2026-11-02, "More shots"), and every trail had the one mesh made for the costliest: lightning's 16 jagged-line quads, a dash's 32 streak slots on the tail and 32 piece lights whatever the tails' share. With the pool thinned (hundreds of shots and up) a plain shot's mesh was 84 quads of which it could show about 14.
- **Done:** the trail batches are split by what their trails can draw (`trail_mesh_slots`): a shot's (no jagged line; with no share for the tails no tail slots and only the newest TRAIL_PIECES_NO_TAIL pieces' lights), lightning's (`BATCH_BOLT_JAG`, `BATCH_ORBIT_JAG`, `BATCH_ENEMY_JAG`: a shot or cooling trail with LOOK_SPARKS) and a dash's (`BATCH_DASH`, `BATCH_ENEMY_DASH`: its particles and streaks, no core). Ten batches, kept in arrays (`_nodes`, `_rooms`, `_buffers`); the native kernel's counting and writing mirror it (`loose_batch`), compared byte for byte for every batch in `test_native_kernel.gd`.
- **The same look:** `tests/benchmark/trail_gpu.gd` renders a fixed scene stepped by hand. Without lightning and dashes (`--plain`) every frame of 60, 400 and 3,000 shots is pixel-identical to main's, with full tails and with none; the run is deterministic. With them, a lightning or dash trail now draws after (over) the other trails of its layer where they cross, which moves a few pixels where trails overlap; no trail loses a quad it could show.
- **Measured (llvmpipe under xvfb, so the vertex stage runs on the CPU; a real GPU's numbers are the owner's to take with the same script):** vertices a frame 168,000 -> 157,700 at 60 shots (full tails), 504,600 -> 250,900 at 400, 2,259,000 -> 434,000 at 3,000; GPU time a frame (median) 32.9 -> 32.5-38 ms (noise), 108 -> 70 ms, 403 -> 131 ms.
- **Not done, measured not worth it now:** the per-trail work on the CPU (which samples are drawable, the path's ends, the tail's spacing): removing those reads from the shader altogether saved about 8% at 60 shots on llvmpipe, and the last pass found a folded quad costs about as much as a drawn one, so it is the vertex count that counts. Several tail pixels per quad: the tail is drawn only while the pool has room (tens of shots), and cutting the tail's quads to a quarter outright saved 20% there (32.5 -> 25.7 ms) before the extra work each quad would take; it would also grow the fragment stage the Windows compilers choke on (2026-10-29). Worth another look if a real GPU shows the tails costly at low counts (integrated GPUs: 2026-11-02, "More shots").

## 2026-11-02: Projectile caps doubled: 1,000 web, 6,000 desktop (owner)

- **Asked:** the owner: "After the new native code it should be possible to raise the projectile counts." `ProjectileManager.DEFAULT_CAP_WEB` 500 -> 1,000, `DEFAULT_CAP_DESKTOP` 3,000 -> 6,000.
- **The manager is no longer the limit** (`test_projectile_timing`, headless on a shared 4-core box, native): at 6,000 shots a step is 0.33 ms with no targets, 0.9 ms with fresh hits in the crowd, 1.1 ms steady, 1.3 ms all orbiting; `update_draw` 0.77 ms. Its GDScript at 3,000 was 2.8, 3.2, 3.8, 5.1 and 5.8 ms. At 10,000 the native step is still 0.6 to 1.4 ms and `update_draw` 1.5 ms.
- **What more shots cost now is their hits:** each hit runs Combat's, Spells' and Feel's handlers in GDScript. The stress run (headless, game logic only): desktop scenario (200 enemies) 3,000 -> 6,000 shots, avg 80 -> 86 ms a frame, a physics step's scripts 24.4 -> 26.0 ms (the frame is the 200 actors, as before). Web scenario (100 enemies), a physics step's scripts: 10.3 to 10.6 ms at 500, 12.4 to 13.4 ms at 1,000, 13.1 ms at 1,500 (frame times swung up to 50% run to run on this box, so the step's scripts are the figure to read). WASM scripts run 2 to 3x slower, so 1,500 would put the web step near its budget; 1,000 is the estimate until the next web stress run reads it, as with 500 (2026-10-23).
- **Not measured: the GPU.** The trail batches cost about 6 ms a frame at 3,000 shots with cards on the RTX A3000 (2026-11-02, "More shots") and grow with the shots, so 6,000 may cost about 12 ms there. If a real GPU cannot hold it, lower the desktop cap or thin the trails further; the effect-quality setting is the place for a per-machine cap.
- **The horde scenarios keep 500 shots** (`stress_runner.gd`'s `HORDE_SHOTS`), so their curve (docs/milestones/horde.md, horde-swarm.md) stays comparable: the enemies are what they measure.

## 2026-11-02: No automatic reverts (owner)

- **Rule (owner):** "Remove the rule about it rolling back merges if the daily build fails." A daily build that fails is not released, and the Daily log says so; nothing is reverted automatically. The owner rolls a merge back with `/revert` when that is the fix.
- **Why:** the bisect reverted the day's merges newest first and kept every revert up to the first passing build, so a breaking merge took the innocent merges after it with it; with the native module's Windows build compiled for the first time on a daily run, one failure there would have rolled back a whole day of work.
- **Gone:** the daily workflow's `revert-bisect` and `revert-apply` jobs, its `retry` input (the re-run after reverts) and `ci/smbot/revert.py`. `/revert` (`smbot.commands`) stays.

## 2026-11-02: The decoy, reviewed: its own timer, its own hurt, its sight, no telegraph (owner)

- **Asked:** the owner had the decoy change (#514) reviewed for fit, then: "Fix it all!"
- **Each decoy keeps its own timer:** an ended entity took the last timer entry of its tag with it, not its own, so with two decoys of one staff the standing one went quiet (and the ended one's entry ticked on, from an actor id the next actor might reopen). Entities now names the entity's key (`Spells.entity_key`) while it sends `entity_ended`, and only that key's entries leave (`_lc_leave_key`).
- **A decoy placed again** starts with an empty intent: with no foe about it aims the way it was placed, not where its row's last decoy aimed.
- **A body that went down** (its aim cleared) keeps the way it had for its timer's payload, not an infinite one.
- **`on hurt` on a decoy** fires when the decoy is hurt (owner), not its wizard: a carrier that is a body of its own (`LC_ACTOR`) is hurt when that body is.
- **No telegraph on a decoy** (owner: only the casts the AI presses itself are telegraphed, "exactly how it should be"): a decoy casts nothing of its own; what its triggers fire comes on the card's clock, an enemy's decoy too, as a familiar's sprinkler or a timer on an enemy's shot or rune does. A windup on the decoy was built and taken out again in this PR as a special case. The card rules test now also tries `on hurt` with an enemy shooter, so a body of the carrier's own is hurt.
- **It aims at what it can see:** the nearest foe behind a wall is passed over for the nearest of the nearer ones with a clear line (at most 8 line checks per ring of the AI's look); with none in view it keeps the nearest.
- **It turns:** a copy of a living staff shows the head sheet's column for its aim, as the staff it copies does.
- **Its card text** (owner): "Distract your enemies with a convincing illusion", no longer a still copy that enemies go for first.

## 2026-11-02: A decoy is an actor that aims itself (owner)

- **Found:** a decoy's timer fired only outward from its wizard through the decoy (a point, `LC_POINT`), so `timer · decoy · bolt` shot where the wizard faced when placing it and never at the foes round it. The owner wanted it to aim itself without a rule of its own.
- **Rule:** a payload fired from an actor's body goes the way that actor aims now (`Spells._lc_fire`, `LC_ACTOR`). A decoy is an actor (`Entities`; done from the backlog's "decoys and familiars as actors", for the decoy only): Health, Hurtbox, Mover, a Caster with no cards (it tells the systems where it is and aims), a `DecoyLook` and a `DecoyController` that aims at the nearest standing actor of another team (`AIBrain.nearest_foe`, the AI's own look). Its trigger's entry follows its body (`Spells.entity_body`, named by Entities while the spawn is asked for). The familiar stays a point and keeps the sprinkler rule (R4 F8).
- **The dash too** (owner: fine as long as a plain dash still goes the way the wizard walks, which is untouched): a timer on a dash fires along the caster's aim now, not the aim at the cast.
- **No lure** (owner): enemies and swarmers chase a decoy when it is the nearest actor of another team, as any; `Entities.lure` and `DECOY_LURE` are gone.
- **Homing on a decoy** (owner): the decoy now `STEERS`; it creeps toward its target within its homing reach at `SpellLimits.HOMING_DRIFT` (24 px/s), the homing rune's rule, so one can always walk away from it. Without homing it stands.
- **What follows from being an actor** (not asked, kept because they are the actor rules): it belongs to its caster (`Actor.belongs_to`: its hurt circle is a projectile target owned by its caster, so the caster's shots pass it, as before, and a payload it fires does not hit it); everything else hurts it, area hits now too; hits push it (its Mover); it has health, `DECOY_HEALTH` 15 (three bolts), instead of breaking after any three hits (a beam's ticks or a nova used to count as hits); its break is no kill. The payload stays its caster's (the tag): kills, mana and `on hit` are the wizard's, as before.
- **Pool:** each Entities row makes its decoy actor the first time it holds one and keeps it; an ended decoy leaves the tree and is placed again under a fresh id. It is `downable`, so Combat never frees it: Entities ends it when its health is gone.

## 2026-11-02: The native module: the projectile manager's hot loops in C++ (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner: "implement the native code performance optimization options. The rule about not using eg. gdextension does not count anymore." The 2026-11-02 pass below had found GDScript's per-shot floor; beyond it was native code or visual-only shots.
- **Rule (owner):** hard rule 4's ban on native code no longer holds for one module, `native/`. Claude kept it Claude's and the owner's only (a bot path; `.cpp`, `.h` and `SConstruct` only there; `ci/smbot/rules.py`): contributor C++ compiled into every player's build is the one thing the GDScript API bans cannot check. Binaries stay banned: CI builds the module from source (`ci/build/native.sh`) and nothing built is committed, not even the `.gdextension` that names the libraries.
- **A GDExtension (godot-cpp), not an engine module:** it loads in the stock, hash-checked templates; an engine module would mean building Godot's export templates ourselves. The web uses Godot's extension-capable template (`web_dlink_nothreads`, `variant/extensions_support=true`), about 1 MB more download (10.1 → 11.1 MB compressed, budget 40), and the module is built with Emscripten 4.0.20, the version Godot 4.7's own web templates were built with. godot-cpp builds only the engine classes the module uses (`build_profile.json`): seconds, not minutes.
- **The GDScript stays, as the reference and the fallback.** Each kernel does what its GDScript does to the bit, mirroring float types (a GDScript `float` is a double, `Vector2` single). A checkout or platform without the module runs the GDScript; `Projectiles.native = false` switches it off. `test_native_kernel.gd` steps a busy scene (bolts, runes, orbits, both ramp kinds, card looks, bounce, homing, growth, linger, ghost, spiral, hit handlers that spawn and remove targets, a dash) with both and compares every packed array byte for byte, the draw buffers and the threat answers. Changing one rounding, branch or charge in the C++ fails it. `test_projectiles_step_exact.gd` holds both to its recording.
- **How the kernel reaches the data:** a `ShotKernel` method takes the manager, reads its packed arrays with `get` and writes through their shared buffers for the length of the call, holding no reference after it. A held reference would make every GDScript write copy the array. The draw buffers are the kernel's own (`ptrw`, so a buffer the renderer still holds is copied, not overwritten). Anything that sends an event stays in GDScript: the native hit pass walks the plain shots and hands back each shot with a hit, a wall, an end or a pass to catch up, in turn, so events keep their order. GDScript then takes the run of handed-back shots after it itself; one kernel call per waiting homing shot made the all-homing step slower than the GDScript.
- **In the kernel:** the common pass, the hit pass (bounds, walls, the hit test with its memory and work budget), trail sampling, `_count_batches` and `update_draw`'s writing (not with a solid shot), and `threat` (its scan, the grid's filling, an ask of the grid).
- **Measured, native against GDScript at 3,000 shots.** In WASM (exported Web Test build, headless Chromium, `test_projectile_timing`): step with no targets 8.4 → 0.66 ms; long wall paths 24.6 → 0.34 ms; straight shots near the crowd 21.6 → 0.6 ms; fresh hits in the hostile crowd 9.0 → 1.8 ms; orbit 14.9 → 6.9 ms; `update_draw` 20.7 → 1.0 ms; homing 22.8 → 24.5 ms (its pass is still GDScript). Desktop, headless, this WSL box: step 5.6 → 1.0 ms; `update_draw` 3.9 → 0.34 ms; a trail sample 1.16 → 0.03 ms; a threat ask 318 → 13 µs. The stress run (headless, so CPU only): desktop scenario avg 116 → 69 ms a frame, p95 179 → 93 ms; web scenario avg 19.6 → 8.4 ms, p95 46 → 21 ms.
- **Reviewed (a fresh session, then fixed):** the web preview would have failed (its Linux import found an extension with no Linux library: `--web` now builds Linux too); `check` covered 35 of the kernel's constants (it now walks one C++ table of all of them against the manager's whole constant map, `native_constants`); a renamed member read as empty (each kernel now declares the members it reads with their types once, and `check_fields` refuses a mismatch); an error in the native hit pass skipped the rest of the pass (the manager now finishes it); `SwarmKernel` was split from `SwarmLookKernel`; the GDScript references were split out under the kernels' names (`_sample_shots`, `_draw_shots`, `_pass_hits_native`); the tests gained frozen, sticky, solid (the drawing switching mid-run), homing-and-spiral and orbit-round-a-shot shots; the C++ is scanned (`check_native_source`: allowed includes only, no system, file, socket, dynamic-load or by-name engine calls) and its build script and profile are owner-only; the exported timing run times the kernels only (its web timeout). **The GDScript stays** (the reviewer and Claude agreed): as the reference the side-by-side tests compare with, the fallback for a checkout without a compiler, and the readable spec contributors can edit; an exported game without the module logs an error.
- **More kernels, chosen by profile (owner: "focus on turning more of the gd code into native modules, if they really give that big a performance improvement").** The stress run, profiled with the module on: in the desktop scenario (3,000 shots, 200 enemies) a physics step was the actors' step 13.6 ms and the projectile manager 4.5 ms, the manager's part almost all hit handlers (Combat, Spells, effects) with filler shots that are plain; in the horde scene (1,000 swarmers) the manager 4.1 ms (its target refresh 0.6 ms of it), the swarm 3.4 ms, its look 1.2 ms. The actors' step (timed per component: looks 5.1 ms, the AI's think 4.5 ms, staffs 2.6 ms, `move_and_slide` 1.1 ms) is node work and gameplay code calling gameplay code: from C++ the same engine calls cost about the same, so it was not ported. Ported, each bit-identical and mutation-checked:
  - **Homing:** the pass, and the search a homing shot waits for, run by the native hit pass itself (Godot's own `hash` for the pick, `object_get_instance_from_id` for `is_instance_valid`). 3,000 homing shots: desktop 6.3 → 0.8 ms a step, WASM 17.9 → 1.0 ms (500 shots: 19.2 → 1.2 ms).
  - **Orbit, return, spiral, growth, linger:** orbit at 3,000 desktop 7.1 → 0.6 ms, WASM 11.8 → 1.9 ms. A shot that circles or returns to another shot still waits for GDScript.
  - **The swarm** (`SwarmKernel`): spacing and the move onto the floor, the look's step and batch writing (its random habits through the look's own `RandomNumberGenerator`). 1,000 swarmers: the swarm's step 3.1 → 1.5 ms, its look 1.0 → 0.15 ms, the look's drawing 0.45 → 0.07 ms a frame. The chase loop (it calls the AI's look for a target and Combat) stays GDScript.
  - The swarm's tests force a branch today's content never takes on both sides (every kind's hurt flag, a face style that glances), so the kernel's copy of it is checked too.
- **Every build is native (owner: "Windows should built with native on github! Not without. So ideally all builds from now on are with the native code").** Windows is cross-built with MinGW-w64 (Ubuntu's `g++-mingw-w64-x86-64-posix`, which the daily build and the bisect install with apt; godot-cpp links the C++ runtime in). A full build makes Linux, the web and Windows and fails when one doesn't build, when an export lacks its library, or when an exported test run (Linux, web) did not run the module; CI refuses `NATIVE=0`. The Windows build was not run on this machine (no MinGW, and the owner asked for no downloads): the first daily run is its test. The GDScript stays as the kernels' reference, compared in the tests.
- **CI** keeps the tools in one cache (`native-*`, Emscripten included: 1.7 GB unpacked) that only the daily build saves (it builds every platform); every other job only restores it, so a quick job can neither save a partial cache nor write what a release build uses. `native.sh` checks the pins again after a restore and builds godot-cpp from a cleaned tree; the toolchain emsdk installed is trusted as downloaded, not hashed.
- **Not done:** the move, stuck and shield passes, `_refresh_targets` and the rays are still GDScript. Past the manager, the desktop stress frame is now mostly the actors' step (the AI) and the rest of the game.

## 2026-11-02: More shots: the dodge check, trail meshes by the pool, three catch-up steps (owner session)

- **Asked:** the owner: "We need to handle way more projectiles and effects. Can this be optimized?", then "Do all of them if they really help."
- **Measured on main (60826bb9), this WSL box:** the headless stress run's desktop scenario (3,000 shots, 200 enemies) sat at 8 physics steps a frame, 343 ms a frame, a step 40 ms: the actors 29 ms of it (15 ms with few shots, `ai_step.gd`), the projectile manager 9 ms, its `update_draw` 8 ms a frame. One `Projectiles.threat` walked every shot (1.4 ms an ask at 3,000; an AI asks on its reaction timer, about 8 asks a step for 200). On a real GPU (windowed, vsync off, `GALLIUM_DRIVER=d3d12`; `MESA_D3D12_DEFAULT_ADAPTER_NAME` picks the adapter, llvmpipe without it) the trail batches cost about 19 ms a frame at 3,000 shots on the RTX A3000 and the frame fell apart from about 100 shots on the Intel UHD (100 plain shots 76 ms, 250 shots with cards 72 ms). Effects (Feel, 34 quads each, 256 slots) are not where it goes.
- **The dodge check:** `threat` keeps its one pass for few shots and few asks; from 256 shots, once a step (or the one before) has 3 asks, the first builds a grid of every shot's path for the horizon (one pass, per-cell lists) that every ask of the step shares, with the same exact test (lowest slot on a tie, as the pass), so the answer is the same; tested against the pass on 400 random asks. Asks per step at 3,000 shots: 1 ask 1.45 -> 1.51 ms, 4 asks 5.8 -> 4.5 ms, 8 asks 11.3 -> 5.4 ms, 16 asks 23.1 -> 6.8 ms.
- **Trail meshes by the pool:** every quad of a trail's 300-quad mesh runs the vertex stage whether drawn or folded, and cutting the mesh 5x cut the trails' GPU time 2.4x (A3000) to 3.3x (Intel). Under the pool's cap the shares draw little (beyond about 130 shots with cards the tails get no share, at 3,000 shots 4% of the particles), so the batches draw a mesh with only the slots the shares can fill: thinned particles come every `spark_stride`-th moment (each trail at its own phase, at stride times the chance: as many born from stride times fewer slots), and with no tail share the tail keeps only a dash's 32 streak slots. With everything shared it is the full mesh. A deterministic render (manager stepped by hand, drawn at fraction 1) is pixel-identical to the old shader with the pool full and with both shares 0; between, which moments give off a particle differs, not how many. A3000, 3,000 shots with cards: trails about 19 -> 6 ms. Intel at low counts (shares near 1) is unchanged.
- **Three catch-up steps** (`physics/common/max_physics_steps_per_frame` 3, was Godot's 8): this supersedes the 2026-10-23 rule "make the step cheaper, do not lower `max_physics_steps_per_frame`", by the owner's go. It is a trade, not a free fix: the game keeps real time down to 20 FPS, and below that plays slower instead of piling up catch-up steps, each as slow as the last. Desktop stress after this pass: 3 steps, 93 ms a frame (was 8 and 343 ms), simulation about 32 steps a second (was about 23), the step 26 ms (was 40: the actors 18 ms, was 29).
- **Tried and dropped (they did not help):** deciding in the trail shader whether a quad can show before its path search (pixel-identical, but no GPU gain on either GPU: folded vertices still cost the same); one pass in `update_draw` instead of counting the batches first (2%).
- **Test fixed:** `test_projectiles_targets`' crowd waited one real frame with its manager in the tree, so the engine stepped it there as many times as it caught up (8 at the old limit) and the fingerprint counted them. The manager is stepped by hand only now; the fingerprint was recorded again on main's manager, and this branch's gives the same.
- **Open:** Intel UHD costs about 0.6 us a trail vertex against little on the A3000 (stubbing the particle routine halves it); worth a look for integrated GPUs and the WebGL path (the look table's constant arrays indexed per particle are a suspect, not a finding). The GDScript per-shot floor (about 1.6 us a shot a step, 1.2 us a frame to draw) caps what more passes can win; beyond that is a native module (hard rule 4) or visual-only shots.

## 2026-11-01: The enemy AI's cost (enemy AI L1)

- **Measured:** the base before the rework (`a36d5ae`) against main, three runs each, interleaved, on cores pinned to the run (WSL, 24 threads; other sessions' tests ran on the other cores, so frame times are noisy). A new benchmark, `tests/benchmark/ai_step.gd`: 200 staffed strays of every kind on their own profiles round four wizards that walk and cast; the median of one `Actor.step` pass over the crowd.
- **Found:** the AI step was 29% slower on main: median 12.4 ms (base) against 16.0 ms. Timing the brain's parts: a decision's `line_clear` 4 µs, `Projectiles.threat` 12 µs, reading the staffs 20 µs, weighing the behaviours 23 µs; the target lookup 175 µs a call, two thirds of the brain's time, because `nearest_target` with the room-wide `SIGHT` reads every cell of the target grid. The base paid the same lookup; the decisions are the new cost (about 1.3 ms a step for 200).
- **Rule:** the brain looks for its target in rings, nearest first (`AIBrain.LOOKS`: 160 px, 480 px, then the room), and keeps a find only when its centre is inside the ring, so the answer is the nearest of all, as before. The AI step is now 12.2 ms, as fast as the base. The plan's other fixes (skip `threat` far from a fight, a longer `reaction` in a crowd, caching `line_clear`) were not made: they cost little.
- **The stress and horde runs** (`stress.gd`, `stress.gd -- --horde`; the enemy step part, median of three): web 100 enemies 1.82 ms base, 1.82 main, 1.85 with the rings; desktop 200: 3.19, 3.43, 3.04; horde 600: 8.1, 12.0, 8.4 (the horde run passed 1, 2 and 3 of 3 runs). The swarm (`swarm_step.gd`) does not use the brain and is unchanged (about 4.6 ms for 1,000).
- **After L2-L5** (heal ally, spreading, the call, pressing on; the benchmark now steps the fixture's team board), on a quiet machine, interleaved, median of three: base 12.0 ms, the rings alone 12.4 ms, all of L1-L5 12.6 ms. So the AI with every part of the rework costs about 5% more than the AI before it, against 29% before the rings. The next saving, if one is wanted: a decision reads every staff's `cast_ready` and weighs every behaviour (about 45 µs together).

## 2026-11-01: Enemies telegraph, allies don't (owner)

- **Rule:** a telegraph is a warning for the players, so an AI driving an actor of the players' team winds up nothing; any other team telegraphs as before. It follows the side (teams follow the driver), not a profile: bots, minions and a player's possessed body cast at once, and an enemy's AI possessing a wizard still telegraphs. Planned as the first step of L5 ([M2-enemy-ai.md](milestones/M2-enemy-ai.md)); replaces "a bot profile with `windup_scale` 0" from the first plan.

## 2026-11-01: The AI reads what it casts next; behaviours are files (owner)

- **Why:** the owner wants one AI that adapts to whatever staffs an enemy holds, so an enemy or a boss is designed by its staffs and a few numbers, not code ([M2-enemy-ai.md](milestones/M2-enemy-ai.md)). The earlier rule below ("No AI knows what it casts next") was there to stop hard-coding like `if dash.is_next(): do_dash()`; the owner is fine with reversing it as long as that stays impossible.
- **Rule:** the AI reads a summary of each staff's next cast (`CastSense`: reach, damage, blast, moves the caster, blocks, spawns, heals, sustained...), never a card or carrier id. A test fails on any card or carrier id under `gameplay/ai/` or `tests/bots/`. Replaces "No AI knows what it casts next" (same day, below); the `BLOCKS` trait comes back for this.
- **Behaviours are files:** each AI behaviour is one stateless file with `want()` and `act()`, shared by every enemy; the highest want wins. Replaces "State machine: an enum and a `match`" for the AI's decisions (below); the executor that carries a plan out keeps a small enum of steps. Why: coop behaviours (heal an ally, shield an ally) are to be added one by one, and a growing `match` works against that. Nothing is allocated per step: behaviours hold no state.
- **Bosses:** no `.gd` per boss. Staffs make the behaviour; a profile of numbers, more staffs and phases (staffs, profile, look) make a boss. A mechanic the parts cannot express goes into the shared AI.
- **Bots** are to decide with the same brain and still press virtual buttons. First try held back (same day): brain-driven bots failed the 4-bot smoke run about half the time, so the old bot stays until a bot profile presses on like a person (docs/NEXT.md, AI9). Second try (L5, same day) merged: with no windup for allies, no rest and `press_on` 1, ten smoke runs all passed (mean 8.7 s, the old bot 9.0 s); the bot's state machine and its "RB is the dash" rule are gone.
- **Found while building:** guarding answers a shot coming, not a touch (a Wall staff stood at every touch boxed its caster in); a target's telegraph aimed at the AI counts as a threat, as a person reads it; the range band narrows with aggression at both ends, and an aimed cast beyond the band is little wanted, because a slow shot from the edge of its reach is easy to dodge.

## 2026-11-01: AIs handle walls; the bot is a state machine (owner)

- **Why:** a bot holding a wall staff with its bolt stood its wall in front of its own bolts every cast and stalled the 4-bot run; the bots had to leave `wall` out of their builds. The owner asked for AIs that handle walls, useful for enemies too, and suggested the AI be a state machine.
- **Sight:** `AISight` (gameplay/ai/sight.gd) is how every AI sees walls: the projectile manager's wall grid, which holds the room's walls and every live Wall card, the same grid shots stop on. Chosen over physics rays (what the bot used), so the enemies and the bots agree and nothing needs a physics query.
- **Enemies:** a new state, REPOSITION: an aimed cast is never wound up into a wall; the enemy walks round it first. A cast that is not aimed (nova, orbit) ignores walls as before.
- **No AI knows what it casts next (owner):** an AI reads the world, not its own staffs: it walks round whatever stands in its line of fire and seeks cover behind whatever wall is near (`AISight.cover`), whoever made it. A first try had the bot ask whether a staff's next cast was a wall (a `BLOCKS` carrier trait and `next_cast_traits`) and keep it for cover; the owner turned it down, and it is gone. Which staff an AI should fire, and when, needs its own design.
- **Dashing emerges (owner):** the bot does not dash as part of a dodge. A state says where to go and whether it is urgent; the one move step walks there and, when urgent with the dash staff ready, dashes once (a dash goes the way the body walks). Dashing into cover and dashing off a shot's line are the same code.
- **Minions (an idea, not built):** the owner asked that a future minion card need only an AI controller. It does: an actor's team is its driver's, so an `AIController` of the players' team handed an enemy (`Actor.drive`) makes it fight the other enemies with its own staff, walls included; `release` makes it an enemy again (`tests/gameplay/ai/test_ai_staff.gd`). Turning an object (a shot, a wall) into one would first need an actor body for it, as decoys and familiars get.
- **State machine:** the bot's flags and counters became one `State` (APPROACH, ATTACK, DETOUR, UNSTICK, DODGE), an enum and a `match` like the enemy AI's, not a framework of state objects: nothing is allocated per step, and a new state is a new enum value and a branch.

## 2026-11-01: Every staff has its own mana pool (owner)

- **Rule:** mana is a property of the staff (`StaffBase.max_mana`, `mana_regen`), not of the wizard. Each staff a body holds has its own pool, full when the body is set up (`Caster.mana_of(at)`, `max_mana_of(at)`); a cast pays from its own staff's pool only, a `hold` reserves from that pool only (`Spells.reserved_mana(actor, staff)`), siphon refunds the staff whose shot hit (`mana_refund_requested(caster, staff, amount)`), and `mana_reserved` names the staff. Replaces "one mana pool per player, shared by its staffs" (DESIGN.md).
- **Why:** the owner asked for the sandbox's "practically infinite" mana on the dev staff alone, without a branch in Flow: with mana on the staff it is two numbers on `content/wizard/staffs/dev/dev.tres` (a million each).
- **One staff file per setup, used as it is (owner, same day):** no numbers are copied between staffs, and the build channel no longer picks staffs. Play and the horde hold the four basic staffs (curl, star, moon, crystal: one per button, each its own head); the sandbox holds the `dev` staff on all four buttons. Replaces the dev and preview builds' starter heads with the dev staff's numbers copied in (`DEV_CHANNELS`, `DEV_FIELDS`, `staffs_for_channel`: gone), so Play in a test build is now the same as in a release. Chosen over a staff that only sizes one shared pool (the owner picked separate pools).
- **Enemies:** their pool moved from `EnemyBase.max_mana`/`mana_regen` onto their staff sub-resource; the numbers are the same.
- **HUD:** the corner panel's mana bar shows the pool of the staff on show (`player_stats_changed` carries it, and is also sent when another staff goes on show); `staff_mana_changed(player, staff, mana, max_mana)` reports every staff's pool, and the builder's MP bar and mana shares are the viewed staff's.
- **Balance, open:** each starter head has the old wizard's numbers (100, 10 a second), so a wizard has four times the mana it had while using all four staffs. Left for a balance pass.

## 2026-10-31: The builder's tree view; copy/paste and cast names removed (owner)

Design: [M2-builder-tree.md](milestones/M2-builder-tree.md), worked out over mockups rendered in the game at true scale.

- **A tree of the compiled staff on Y,** in the ring's place: a row per carrier and per trigger, made of the slots' own 16 px tiles, the trigger card's name and nothing else on a trigger row. With each trigger taking its own payload, the slot grid alone no longer says what a staff does.
- **Rejected on the way:** brackets with words under the slots (they assumed a slot row that does not exist: the slots are a 5 × 4 grid of 16 px tiles in a 112 px column); a mark on every tile of every payload (it covers the icons and clashes where payloads nest); a card popup (it covered rows of the tree).
- **The card under the slot marker is described in the column,** where the corner panel and the staff's numbers were: both are hidden while the builder is open, and thin HP and MP bars framed in the player's colour sit on top of the column instead, so health stays in view during a wave.
- **Every quarter is laid out as player 1's:** the column on the left, the ring or tree on the right.
- **The hub keeps the staff's numbers** when no card is pointed at (owner: good to see them).
- **LT held clears the staff** (the press still takes out at once); Y opens the tree.
- **Removed: staff codes (staffs D2) and cast names (staffs E1).** Copy and paste go from both builders, with `StaffCode`; the generated names go, with `SpellNamer` and every card's `name_words`. Where a cast was called by its name, its plain description is used.

## 2026-10-31: Each trigger takes its own payload (owner)

- **Rule:** the triggers before a carrier are all kept, and after the carrier each reads its own payload, in slot order: the first trigger the next group, the second the group after it. A payload's own payloads are read inside it first (depth-first). A trigger whose payload finds no card, or whose event never happens on the carrier, is wasted as before; the others keep theirs. Before, a second trigger before one carrier was wasted.
- **Why:** it makes a staff a tree, not a chain: one carrier can do something different on each event (`on hit · on expire · bolt · nova · frost · bolt`), and payloads branch at every level, which gives the most combinations (pillar 1). Every extra trigger costs the cards and mana of its own payload, so it never multiplies a payload for free. It extends Noita's reading (a trigger's payload is what is read after it) in the same spirit. Chosen over "every trigger fires one shared payload", which made extra triggers free repeats of the same effect, and over stacking only timers, which was a special case (a timer-only rule in the compiler; tried on 2026-10-31 and dropped the same day).
- **How:** `CompiledSpell.triggers` (each a `CompiledSpell.Trigger`: event, interval, payload, slot) replaces `trigger_event`, `trigger_interval` and the single `payload` (now the first trigger's, read-only). The runtime fires every trigger whose event happens (`Spells._fire_payloads`, `CarrierKit.fire_payloads`); a lasting carrier is listed once per timer or on hurt trigger (`_lc_trigger`), each with its own clock. A shot that ends on a wall fires both its `on wall` and its `on expire` payloads.
- **Fire rate:** two timers are two payloads on the same clock, not a faster one. A faster totem comes from what its payload holds (`burst`, `triple`) or from a card that changes a timer's interval, if one is ever wanted.

## 2026-10-30: A carrier knows the runtime only through its kit; the runtime names no carrier (owner)

- **The kit** (owner, in the review of the carriers PR: the carriers reached into about 34 of the runtime's own members, so a new carrier had to read `spells.gd`): a carrier, `CasterMoves` and `StaffPoses` ask the runtime only through `CarrierKit` (`core/spells/carrier_kit.gd`), one short file of named, documented calls (the cast under way, the aim, the shot kit, the hit path, payloads, live carriers, moves, entities). Each forwards to the runtime's one rule for it; nothing moved or changed in what it does. Something a carrier needs that the kit lacks is added to the kit, never reached around it.
- **No carrier named in `spells.gd`:** the beam, swing and slam fields and the `beams()`, `swings()`, `slams()`, `dashes()`, `decoys()` getters are gone for one `Spells.carrier(id)`; whoever reads a carrier's own getters asks its class (`CarrierBeam.of(spells)`). A live carrier on a row is `LC_ROW`, placed by its carrier's `tip_of` (was `LC_BEAM`, `LC_SWING`); a live beam's magnet pull runs in the beam's own `step` (so a beam that another part of the same step starts pulls from the next step, not that one); a block's form is the blocking carrier's `id()` (a dash or teleport only deflects, so theirs is never seen). A ray the runtime sends itself (a jump, a zap) still looks like a beam (`RAY_FORM`). `tests/core/test_carriers.gd` reads the scripts and fails on a way around the kit or a carrier named in `spells.gd`.

## 2026-10-30: An explosion hits as `burst` (owner)

- **A blast's strikes are `burst` again** (owner, after the merge of R4 F2 with the carriers): what a blast strikes is still its carrier's hit (cards, triggers, chain, relay: rules R4 F2), but with kind `burst`, so it has the explosion's hit-stop, shake and sound and a wizard caught in their own reads "downed by your own Blast", not "Shot" or "Beam". The cards that land on a point strike as `burst` too, as the area hit did before F2. The compiler makes the copy once (`CompiledSpell.blast_hit`, `echo_blast_hit`; `StaffCompiler.blast_hit`); a hit that relay changed is copied per blast. The kind is a feel-and-text label only: no rule reads it.

## 2026-10-30: A wasted card has no text; works is yes or no (owner)

- **No reasons** (owner: "I don't need a string explaining why! Just a works = yes/no ... No text should be shown. The visuals are enough"): a card that does nothing is marked in the builder (the "!" on its slot, greyed in the staff rows and the radial builder) and nothing says why. The needs are flags only (`ModifierBase.NEEDS`: `{"any": FLIES}`), a carrier answers `works(behaviour)` and `fires(event)` with a bool, `CompiledCast.wasted` and `CompiledStaff.wasted` are slot -> true, and `Spells.card_works(card_id, carrier)` replaces `card_waste`. The carriers lost the words that only built sentences (`noun`, `what`, `hits_nothing`). This reverses rule 1's "with a reason a player can read" (M2-rules.md R2a, R2c); the rule itself, works or wasted and never silent, stands, and `test_card_rules` still checks it.

## 2026-10-30: A card's needs live with the card; one question for the builder (owner)

- **Needs moved to the cards** (owner, after "what if the cards had defined traits?"): what each behaviour needs from a carrier is `ModifierBase.NEEDS`, and each trigger's is `TriggerBase.NEEDS`, next to their enums, per kind of card rather than per card file (two cards with one behaviour cannot disagree, and a card file cannot claim more than its code does). The flags they are written in are `CarrierTraits`, on their own because a constant cannot reach across `Carrier` and `ModifierBase` both ways.
- **One question for everyone:** `Spells.card_waste(card_id, carrier)` answers, before a card is placed, whether it does anything on a carrier on its own and why not, with the compiler's own rules and words (the compiler's trigger check is the shared `StaffCompiler.trigger_waste`); a test asks it for every card on every carrier and compares with a real compile. What it opens (builder marks, reward hints and offers, bots, category tags, granted traits) is in docs/BACKLOG.md, by milestone.

## 2026-10-30: Which cards do nothing on a carrier is worked out (owner)

- **No lists per carrier** (owner: "I don't like that I have to run a test to find out if I should add something to some lists on a new carrier. This sounds like something that can be computed"): a carrier answers yes/no questions about itself (`traits()`, four new: `MOVES`, `LINE`, `MEETS_SHOTS`, `MOVES_CASTER`), and each card says once, for every carrier, what it needs from one (`Carrier.NEEDS`, `EVENT_NEEDS`: "any" of some flags, "not" others, and its "why_not"). The compiler works out from the two, every time a staff is built, whether a card does anything on a carrier and the sentence the builder shows ("<why_not>, and <what the carrier is>"). Every one of the 14 x 32 answers is the same as the lists gave (checked cell by cell); 98 sentences read a little differently, as they are now put together from the card's reason and the carrier's description.
- `test_card_rules.gd` stays the check that the yes/no answers are true, in CI; nobody has to run it to know what to write.

## 2026-10-30: A carrier lists only the cards that do nothing on it (owner)

- **The table is cut down** (owner: "I would not be able to fill it out on my own... why is this table even needed?"): the per-carrier table that answered, for every card, whether it works, whose code runs it and how it looks (`Works`, `Shows`, a row per card) is gone. Only the part the game uses stays: `DOES_NOTHING` (modifier behaviour -> the sentence the builder shows) and `NEVER_FIRES` (trigger -> sentence). Every card not listed does something.
- **Why that is enough:** the compiler and the builder only ever needed the sentences; whether a card works is already found by `tests/core/test_card_rules.gd`, which plays every card on every carrier and fails on a card that does nothing but is not listed, or the other way round, so nobody has to predict the list. "Whose code runs it" was documentation, and the look column was only read by a test.
- **What became plain code:** on a carrier that hits nothing, an infusion and heavy count only with a blast (`takes_infusion`, `BLAST_CARRIES`). The three carriers that show no card visuals (teleport, decoy, familiar) are a backlog note, not a declaration.

## 2026-10-30: The shot travels with its events; carriers own their mechanics (owner)

- **No "current shot" on the manager** (owner, 2026-10-30): `Projectiles.event_slot` was a public global the spell runtime, combat's listeners and Feel read to know which shot an event was about. Every event of a shot now names it as its last argument, `shot` (`projectile_hit`, `projectile_expired`, `shot_blocked`, `shot_deflected`, the manager's `wall_met`; -1 for a ray), combat keeps it beside its tag and names it in what a hit sets off (`hit_landed`, `kill_landed`, `reaction_landed`, `reaction_triggered`, `enemy_died`), and spells names it in a blast's `area_hit_requested`, so an `on kill` from a spent shot's blast still leaves along that shot. `Spells.hit_profile(tag, shot)` takes it from combat. The manager keeps `_event_slot` for itself only. Each emit passes what the global held at that moment, so nothing plays differently.
- **Carriers own their mechanics:** dash and teleport share `CarrierMove`; every move of a caster goes through `CasterMoves` (dash, teleport, hook, swap, recoil); decoy and familiar share `CarrierEntity`; the rain's telegraph and landing and the wall's line are in their carriers. Two carrier hooks, `reset_actor` and `switch_off`, replace hand-picked calls in the runtime.
- **Bug fixed on the way:** `Spells.clear` and `close_caster` ended beams and swings but not slams, so a slam winding up still struck afterwards (regression tests in `test_slam.gd`).

## 2026-10-30: Carriers are named; the table's words read plainly (owner)

- **A carrier is known by its name** (owner, 2026-10-30): a form card says `carrier = &"beam"`, not `motion = 3`, and `FormBase.Motion` is gone. The enum's numbers were stored in every form card, so a removed carrier had to leave a placeholder (`_UNUSED_10`, the fissure's) and the order could never change; now adding or removing a carrier is its script and its line in `Carriers.make_all`, and a card that names a carrier that is gone is refused by the validator (`unknown-carrier`). `CompiledSpell.carrier` is the name too.
- **The table's words** (owner: "really hard to understand"): `Use` is now `Works` (`YES`, `OWN_WAY`, `ONLY_WITH_BLAST`, `NO`) and `Look` is now `Shows` (`DRAWN`, `AS_EFFECT`, `NOTHING`), so a row reads as a sentence: `B.RETURN: [Works.NO, "it does not fly out"]`.
- **Rows name their answers** (owner, later the same day: "a json like structure would help"): a row is a dictionary whose keys say which question each value answers, `{"works": Works.NO, "why_not": "it does not fly out"}`, `{"fires": false, "why_not": "a decoy hits nothing"}`; `tests/core/test_carriers.gd` checks every row has exactly the keys its answer needs.
- **Two more rules moved into the carriers:** the fields a form must set (`required_fields`, read by the validator) and how long the staff keeps working for its animation (`HOLDS_STAFF`).

## 2026-10-30: Carriers decide every card (owner direction)

- **The carrier is the important part** (owner, 2026-10-30): each carrier (one per `FormBase.Motion`, `core/spells/carriers/`) decides for itself what it does with every modifier behaviour, trigger and infusion, both in whether each works on it and how it shows (then `Use` and `Look`; renamed `Works` and `Shows` the same day). There is no default: `tests/core/test_carriers.gd` fails until a new carrier, behaviour or trigger is decided on every carrier, and casts every carrier to check that what it says it draws reaches its renderer. The design is docs/CARRIERS.md.
- **Why:** the answers were spread over the compiler's waste table (one `match` per behaviour, carriers listed inside), the runtime's motion checks and the projectile manager's refusals, and the waste table's default was "runs", so a new behaviour was assumed to work everywhere until `test_card_rules` caught a silent cell. Grouping by carrier puts the decision where a designer thinks about it ("what does bounce mean on a beam?"); the shared implementations (homing, bounce, the hit path) stay written once.
- **One mechanism:** the compiler reads the carriers' tables and traits (its static helpers are thin wrappers now); the runtime hands every launch to its carrier (`Carrier.launch`, `shoot`, `on_hit`, `on_move_end`, `step`...) and never asks which motion a spell has. The beam, swing and slam carriers keep their own rows (they were the `SpellBeams`, `SpellSwings`, `SpellSlams` kits split out of `spells.gd` earlier the same day); a hit with no shot slot goes back through the runtime as one `RayHit`.
- **No card reads differently:** the tables were generated from what the compiler answered before. The retired motion slot (`_UNUSED_10`) now runs nothing. The look column states three gaps the tests confirm: a teleport, a decoy and a familiar draw none of their cards' looks.

## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4 F8, walls let out what they cast; the familiar casts from its orb (owner)

- **What a wall casts comes out of it, beams too:** one rule for every payload of a wall: the wall's row does not count for what it cast. A flying shot's step and a beam's ray walk ask the same thing (`_solid_of`; a beam names its caster shot with `Projectiles.ray_cast_by`). A beam cast by a still carrier (a rune, a wall) keeps it as its caster and starts where it is.
- **The familiar casts from its orb** (replaces F3's "from its wizard's body"): Entities reports each orb's place every step (`Spells.familiar_at`, keyed by `Spells.entity_key`), and the payload flies outward from what the familiar circles through the orb (the existing live-carrier rule: outward from the caster's body through the carrier), so `timer · familiar · bolt` is a rotating sprinkler a player can predict from where the orb is. An enemy's familiar sprays the same way, so it no longer casts at the wizard.

## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4 F1: every move adds up; Hook becomes Pull (owner)

- **Every move adds up** (docs/milestones/M2-rules.md "R4 F1"): the mover keeps one list of moves, each with its own way, distance and time, as shots' moves do (R1e) and as recoil did. Nothing replaces a move in progress any more, so "a later dash, teleport, hook or swap replaces an earlier one" (staffs B4, "Simultaneous moves" below) is gone; two dashes stack, two pulls add, recoil is one more move (its own arrays and the teleport's recoil folding of R4c went). Every move answers `caster_move_finished`.
- **A request carries its way:** `caster_move_requested` sends the move itself (way × distance) instead of a point measured from the staff tip or the feet, so a dash cast by a point goes to the point with no special case.
- **A dash the wizard casts itself goes the way it walks** (owner, option (b) on #492: staffs A6a kept), else along the aim. The caster passes its walk with the cast (`cast_staff`'s `walk`, from the actor's intent: no new event), and `_move_caster` turns the wizard's own dash by the walk's angle off the aim (one branch). A dash cast by a point, and a payload dash moving a shot, go along their aim. Walking stops while a dash runs (the actor's own legs); a pull, swap or recoil adds to the walk.
- **Mirror has no exceptions** (owner, 2026-10-30): the carrier fired again with its way reversed, whatever that way is. A wizard's `mirror · dash` dashes the walk's way and back: both run, both strike along their way and show their look, and they add up to nothing (give or take walking). `mirror · teleport` blinks out and back, both cues. The skip of a move's mirrored firing in `_fire_spell` and the waste row "a move and its mirror cancel" (R4g, and its merge with R4b) are gone.
- **Hook is the Pull modifier:** whatever its carrier hits, its caster is pulled to; several hits, several pulls. A blast's hits move nothing (F2's guard against a pull at every arrival, kept for Pull until the owner decides: `_blasting` marks a blast's strike, so a swing's, beam's or dash's own hits still pull), and the waste table says so. Checked where hits land (as possess and siphon), wasted through `hits()`. R4a's "a hook's on expire happens once" is now Pull's: a shot its hit spent ends where the caster arrives. The Rime Crook casts `frost · pull · bolt`. `Motion.HOOK`'s number stays as an unused slot so stored motions keep their meaning.
## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4 F3, placing (owner)

- **What you place appears a step ahead along the aim** (twice the card's size; with a wall in the way, where it is cast, never in the wall: `Spells._placed_at`): a rune, a decoy, a familiar. One rule, so a mirror's reversed aim puts its copy behind you instead of on the same spot; the rune's `count == 1` case is gone. Decoy and familiar sizes are now their bodies' (8 and 3 px), so the step is visible. Spread (sniper) now counts on all three: their spot follows the aim.
- **The familiar has no payload of its own** (replaces R4g's hand-on): it is a body that lasts, and casts what its triggers give it, as any lasting carrier (`timer · familiar · bolt` fires a bolt every tick). Gone: `familiar_cast_requested`, Entities' once-a-second foe search, `StaffCompiler.hands_on` and its helpers; `carries_payload` is rain's alone (owner: rain keeps its payload). Its timer fires from its wizard's body along the aim at the cast, not from the orb (the orb's place is Entities'; see the PR for the question).
- **A rain's timer ends with its landing:** an edit that drops the landing also takes the rain off the live carriers (`_drop_pending`), so a rebuilt staff's rain stops ticking at once.
## 2026-10-30: Fissure removed (owner)

- **The fissure carrier is gone** (owner, 2026-10-30): the card, its icon and lore, its runtime cases (the fissure launch, the held fissure as a beam, `PIERCE_ALL` and `_ray_pierce`: a ray's pierce is now just its card's), its compiler cases (`StaffCompiler.rays` was only beam and fissure: inlined as `RAY`) and its tests. A beam does what it did.
- **Its two combos keep their idea on a beam:** `beam_fire_line` (pierce · fire · beam burns both imps on its line) and `beam_hit_nova` (on hit · beam · frost · nova).
- **`FormBase.Motion` keeps slot 10** as `_UNUSED_10` so the numbers stored in every other carrier's `.tres` keep their meaning; a card that uses it fails `range_problems`.

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4b, a wasted card is checked too (owner)

- **A W cell is played with the card forced on** (owner item 22): the card rules test failed only on a card that does nothing unlisted, not on a card listed as wasted that would work (slam's was found by hand). Now every W cell also runs with the card kept, and a different trace fails the test. Still auto-discovering, no new job.
- **One seam:** `StaffCompiler.keep_wasted`, a test-only static switch that keeps every card the waste table would drop; the runtime never reads the table, so it alone decides. Costs (mana, a slower cast, less damage) are no effect and are paid by both casts.
- **Findings:** none on main today; every W cell changes nothing when forced. Item 23 (rule 2 checks) stays in docs/BACKLOG.md.

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4e: hold (owner)

- **One rule: hold keeps the carrier going until the staff is pressed again.** A carrier that lasts lives on (as before); one that is over at once is cast again every wind-up + lifetime (`Spells._repeats`): a held rain falls again every 0.8 s, a held swing sweeps back and forth, along the wizard's aim now. Its hold (and mana reservation) ends only when switched off, not between two repeats.
- **A held fissure is a beam** with the fissure's pierce-all (`_ray_pierce`, the one place a ray's pierce lives): it stays open and hits everything on its line every beam tick. No second ray walk.
- **Still wasted:** slam, dashes and teleport ("it is over at once"; a slam's repeat was not asked for).
- **Echo on hold** is no longer skipped silently: the echo is a second held copy, N seconds later at half damage, switched off with the hold, reserving no extra mana (its firings share the hold's tag). A repeat does not echo again.

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4g, bodies and mirror (owner)

- **A rain strikes where it lands** with its own hit and cards (a small splash, `RAIN_SPLASH`), then drops its payload: its infusions, heavy, chain, siphon, possess and on hit/kill/reaction count. One strike for it, in the beam context the slam's ring uses (`_strike_round`).
- **A familiar is a caster carrying cards:** its infusions and hit cards ride on every carrier it casts, as if placed on each (costs included). Done once, in the compiler (`hands_on`), not at each cast; a handed card nothing it casts takes on is wasted.
- **A decoy guards itself** with a shield (stops the other teams' shots at it, one charge each) or deflect (turns them), each a block for `on block`: the swing's guard (`_guard`), asked by Entities every step.
- **Mirror is one rule:** the carrier fired again with its aim reversed, on every carrier (a wall behind, a rain through the caster, an orbit from the other side, hooks and swaps backwards). Dash and teleport stay wasted: a move and its mirror cancel (until R4 F1: now both fire and add up). The per-carrier allow-list is gone.
- ~~**A move's mirror is not fired (merging R4g with R4b, 2026-10-30):**~~ Replaced on 2026-10-30 (R4 F1: mirror has no exceptions). with the five-motion allow-list gone, a forced mirror on a dash or teleport fired the move again backwards (the mover replaces a move in progress), and R4b's forced check found the cell listed as wasted while it changed the cast. `_fire_spell` skips the mirrored firing of a dash or teleport, as `_recoil` skips them, so "a move and its mirror cancel" stays true.

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4c: moves (owner)

- **A dash hits what it passes, a teleport where it lands** (docs/milestones/M2-rules.md R4c): every hit card was wasted on them; now they hit once each body with the carrier's damage and cards, through the swing's hit path (no second pipeline). Pierce on a dash is wasted with a reason: a dash already hits everything on its way.
- **Recoil adds to a move:** a dash's push runs with it; a teleport's is taken off its distance, and a recoil longer than the teleport sends it backwards.
- **One shield rule for every move:** dash, teleport, hook and swap guard what they move (the wizard by a barrier, a moved shot by shield charges).
- **A point cannot move, so the wizard does:** a teleport cast by a point blinks the wizard to it; a hook or swap cast by one moves the wizard. A dash cast by a point is left for the owner (the mover picks a dash's way from the walk; done in R4 F1: it dashes to the point).

## 2026-10-29: Staff animations are carrier data (owner)

- **Every carrier says how the staff moves** (`FormBase.staff_pose`), as it says its cast flash and impact (`cast_look`, `impact_look`): the owner asked for staff animations for slam and saw that every carrier could use the same thing for variety. One player of that data (`Spells.staff_pose` and the rest), drawn the same way by every look (`ActorLook`), so the strays play the same animations as the wizards.
- **Channels** (owner's pick): turn, lift, push, glow (the owner: "would glow fit?": yes, it shares the timeline; the infusion's colour, else the player's), stretch, shake and the face's mood. Keys are dictionaries (readable, like `cast_look`), any channel left out at rest; packed into numbers once per card.
- **Timing in the carrier's own time:** wind-up, strike (its working time: a swing's sweep, a slam's ring, a beam's, rain's or dash's life, ended by its last shot), a hold that loops while the strike lasts (a held beam straining), after. A new cast starts from where the staff is (no jumps).
- **Aim:** a swing or slam keeps the aim of its cast (their aim is locked); every other animation turns with the live aim (a held beam follows it). For carriers that are no swing a negative turn lifts the head, whichever way the body faces (a slam raises, never dips, facing left).
- **Not card order or special cases:** a shield and deflect question earlier the same day settled that rules, not order, decide; the same here: the strike is defined per motion in one table (`_pose_strike_of`).
- **Cleanup:** Feel's thin-and-tall squash on every cast is gone (each card's `stretch` replaces it; the two together doubled up), and `SWING_HOLD`/`SWING_SETTLE` became the swing card's keys. The dash look (stretch, lean, ghosts) stays: it is a body being moved (a dash, a hook's or swap's pull), not a cast.

## 2026-10-29: Deflect's look and price (melee I5)

- **Look: zig (18), shared with bounce and arc.** Every kind is taken (22 to 24 are flags), so the card shares one. Zig is streaks thrown back at an angle, which is what a deflect does to a shot: it is a bounce off the carrier instead of a wall, so it sits with bounce in the "turning off something" group. `test_original_looks.gd` lists it.
- **Price: mana ×1.25, delay ×1.2, complexity 2.** Shield (one block, a charge per card) has no price of its own; deflect turns every shot that touches the carrier and hands it to the caster 1.7× faster, so it costs a little more, about what return and bounce cost. On a swing (no mana) the price is the delay.

## 2026-10-29: Freezes in co-op (melee I7)

- **The hold lives in `core/`, Feel only asks for it.** Areas only talk through `core/`, so the per-actor hold is in the actor table (`Actors.hold`, read by `Actor.step` with `spend_hold`) and the per-shot hold in the projectile manager (`freeze_shot`, state `S_FROZEN`); Feel decides who is held and for how long. A step longer than the hold runs for the rest, so a hold never eats a long step.
- **Which bodies:** the attacker and the target of a hit, plus the shot whose `projectile_hit` is being sent (`event_slot`; a ray has none). A spent shot is already gone, so only a piercing, lingering or orbiting shot is held. A kill holds the killer (the dead body is freed at once). A reaction, which used to stop the whole game, holds its caster: it is a hit, not a rare moment.
- **The rest is per actor** (`hit_stop_rest` after a hold), so one body is not frozen for ever by a stream of hits, while two players' hits never gate each other. Kills and reactions ignore the rest, as deaths did.
- **Game-wide stop:** only `player_downed` and the death of an enemy whose `EnemyBase.boss` is true (a new flag; no boss exists yet). The parry's local stop is added when I5 lands (done: the parry cue's `hold` on the parrying actor, `content/cues/parry.tres`).
- **Held bodies stop in Feel's own time:** the hold counts down in the simulation's time, not real time; with `Engine.time_scale` at 1 they are the same.

## 2026-10-29: Screen shake setting (melee I6)

- **The switch lives on the Settings page and talks to feel by an event.** Areas only talk through `core/` events, so the pause menu sends `screen_shake_changed(enabled)` and feel sets `shake_enabled`; the menu never touches `Feel`. The saved choice is read when the menu is bound (the Hud loads after Feel) and sent only when it is off, since on is feel's default. Settings go in a `settings` section of the same `SaveFile`; only flags make a save "not new", so the intro is unaffected.

## 2026-10-29: Staff codes in the radial builder

- **X copies, A pastes.** The shoulders are the staffs and the paging, RT/LT/Y/B are taken, and the d-pad's sides flip the kind; X (`alternate`) and A (`accept`) were free in this builder. A paste replaces the whole staff, so it is the plain "confirm" button; it is also refused with a result line when the code is bad, and the player still refuses cards it does not own.
- **The result replaces the two buttons' row** for 3.5 s instead of using the 3-line note box, which is 108 px wide and would cut a refusal's reason; the row is as wide as the ring's side, and longer text is cut with "..".
- **The cast names are one line** (joined with " / ") above that row, cut to fit, because the column under the slots is full.
