The fragment stage only colours a pixel of its own quad in one of four small modes (a round light, a piece's tail light, pixels with an optional cross and line, a one-pixel line): 69 lines with what it calls, no loops, no look kinds, nothing read from the path (a test checks the text). The look kinds are data: one row each in a LOOK TABLE in the shader (life, start, throw, drag, curl, float, circle, line, blink, colours, light, rate; a dash's streaks and feet are three more rows), read by one routine, `spark`; the trail's window (how far back its particles are drawn, the longest of its kinds' and its tail's) is a small table too.
- **Compile times on the real GPU** (the owner's Windows Chrome, ANGLE on Direct3D 11; each program's link timed in a page with the exact GLSL Godot sends, captured from the build; a program with a stub other stage times one stage; runs vary by about ±30%): the trail's fragment stage **68 ms** (it was ~90 s and a dead GPU process), its vertex stage 1.0-1.9 s (the particle routine about 1 s of it; the table reads, the hash and the searches' loop bounds measured as not the cost, it is plain code volume), the whole trail program about 2 s. The others: `shot_effect` 1.8 s (fragment 1.95 s), `shot_beam` 2.2 s (fragment 2.0 s), `shot_orbit` 0.67 s, `shot_rune` 0.54 s, `shot_status` 0.22 s, `shot_arc` 0.05 s, `shot_sprite` 0.04 s; Godot's own canvas shader 1.5 s. Godot compiles each in two variants. In the four-bot run the first frames in the room wait 12-14 s for all of them (one long frame; the game then runs at frame p95 ~19 ms with 20 shots). `shot_effect` and `shot_beam` are the heaviest fragment stages left (per-pixel loops over their particles with `particle()`'s branch per kind): within the limit, a candidate for the same treatment.
- **Real-GPU tests:** CI and headless Chrome cannot catch this class of bug (SwiftShader compiles anything at once), so the fix was proven on the owner's Windows desktop with a script that serves a web export from WSL, opens it in a separate Windows Chrome (fresh temp profile), waits, closes it and reads the page console from Chrome's log: FAIL on a lost GPU process, a lost context, or a shader that fails to compile or link. Results: the Web export stayed up 150 s on the menu (PASS; #400 died at ~90 s), and the Web Test export's four-bot run passed with "SMOKE OK" (the bots cast spells, so every shot shader compiled and drew), twice. The same runs with a WebGL wrapper in the page logged every program that blocked over 100 ms, which named the heavy ones above.
- **Look differences** (screenshots before (two runs, identical) and after, compared by eye and by pixel, at 1x and 3-4x): the same tails, cores, lights and particle kinds, motions and colours. Differences: each particle's random numbers are new (one seed range per table row), so a given frame's particles are placed differently, with the same spread; a particle is no longer clipped to its trail's margin round where it was born (a far-flying ember or gust is drawn whole); inside a trail every particle pixel is under every tail pixel, lightning's line over both and the core on top (before, the youngest pixel won on its piece and newer pieces were on top); a tail longer than 128 px loses its oldest, sparsest end (a shot faster than ~430 px/s, or ~280 px/s for frost's longer-lived tail: a Faster frost bolt at 330 px/s loses its last ~20 px, where few tail pixels still live); lightning's jagged line is a straight step between its 4-px bends, so at a bounce it cuts the corner inside one step; with homing and void together the dark heart is centred on the woven core (it was 1 px off).
- **Cost:** a trail is 1,200 vertices (was 264) of straight-line work, and much less fragment work: each quad covers only its pixel, line or light.

## 2026-10-29: A loading screen before the first room; loading as a core service (owner)

- **The owner:** the shader compiles should not happen on the main menu but "in a loading screen before entering a level", built so it is "easy to use in the future, when starting playing". This replaces the menu warm-up below.
- **Design:** loading is a core service, `core/loading` (autoload `Loading`): `run(tasks, done)` does a list of LoadTasks (`load_task.gd`: units, begin, step, is_done, end) one unit a frame after the screen is drawn, sends `loading_started` / `loading_progressed(done, total)` / `loading_finished`, then calls `done`; `cancel()` drops it. The HUD's screen (`hud/panels/loading/`) is drawn from the events alone, so it serves any load. Flow, on Play and Sandbox, runs `Flow.play_tasks()` (state LOADING) and then starts the intro or the run; today that list is the shader warm-up (now a LoadTask) while the session's shaders are not compiled, so later Plays start at once. Anything else to prepare before playing (a room's art, a generated level, M3's room sequence) is one more LoadTask in that list. Test scripts turn it off (`loading_enabled`); the automated runs load like a player (the owner: their windows lagged while the shaders compiled mid-run): the four-bot and stress runs wait for `room_entered`, so the stress run measures with every shader already compiled.
- **Found on the way:** the autoload first went before Projectiles, and every test file then logged leaked shaders and a mesh at exit (a static of a script freed in a different order); last in the list, nothing leaks. Flow finds it on use, as it starts after Flow.
- **Measured on the owner's GPU** (four-bot web run): every shader compiles after Play, behind the screen, none on the menu and none in the room; the room starts ~2 s of game time later than before in that run.

## 2026-10-29: Shader warm-up: every shader compiles on the menu, not in the first room (owner)

- **Why:** Godot compiles a shader the first time something draws with it; on the owner's GPU (Windows Chrome, ANGLE on Direct3D) the shot shaders took 10+ s together, all in the first frames of the first room. Making each faster (above) helps; moving the wait off the game is the rest.
- **How:** `core/projectiles/shader_warmup.gd`, added by the projectile manager at start, waits 3 frames (the menu is drawn first), then draws one shader a frame the way the game draws it (a one-instance batch, a mesh, a sprite) with nothing on screen (a quad of no size never culled, data that folds every quad away, a sprite far below a pixel), plus Godot's own canvas shader by a batch and a mesh, then frees itself. The program Godot compiles is per shader and variant, not per material, so the game's own batches reuse them.
- **Every future shader too (the owner):** the shot shaders are listed in the warm-up by how they are drawn; an area's shader is handed over with `ShaderWarmup.want(shader, how)` (Feel's hit flash, Entities' telegraph, the HUD's vignette), since core must not load an area's files. `tests/core/test_shader_warmup.gd` walks the project and fails for any `.gdshader` not warmed, naming the fix. AGENTS.md says so.
- **Measured on the owner's GPU** (four-bot web run): every shot shader now compiles between "main menu ready" and the room (the menu hitches for a few seconds in all, a shader a frame), none after the room starts. The beam, timed for the first time as geometry: 0.8 s a variant (was ~2.2 s). Godot's canvas shader still compiles at boot (1.7-2.6 s a variant), before the menu shows. Screenshots (menu, sheets) unchanged.

## 2026-10-29: Effects and beams as geometry; the trail searches its path once (owner)

- **Why:** after the trail fix, the first room still waited 12-14 s on the owner's GPU (Windows Chrome, ANGLE on Direct3D) while every shot shader compiled. Timed there today (each program's link in a page with the exact GLSL Godot sends; a stub other stage times one stage; minimum of several runs, single runs vary 2-3x): `shot_effect`'s fragment stage 1.6-1.8 s, the trail's vertex stage 1.0-1.7 s, Godot's own canvas shader 1.7 s, `shot_orbit`/`shot_rune` fragments 0.4-0.9 s.
- **Effects are geometry** (as the trail): an effect is an instance of `Feel.effect_mesh()`, 34 small quads (its light, 12 card particles, 16 sparks, a poof's 4 clouds, its flash: cross, dotted disc, fraying ring); `vertex()` places each, the fragment colours its own quad in four small modes with no loops. `shot_particle.gdshaderinc` now has `place_particle` (where a card's particle is and what it looks like, once) and `particle_pixel`; `particle` is the two together, so runes, familiars and statuses draw the same particles from one routine. Result: the effect's fragment stage **40-50 ms** (was 1.6-1.8 s), its instanced vertex stage **~70 ms**. Screenshots: every effect sheet is pixel-identical to main except a handful of pixels where a value lands on a threshold (a fleck's star, a poof cloud's rim on a diagonal staff; the hash rounds a hair differently in a vertex stage).
- **Beams are geometry:** a beam (live or short) is a `MeshInstance2D` of `Feel.beam_mesh()` (a light and a pixel quad per piece of its path, its splash's sparks and lights, 12 card particles), its path a uniform array (`beam_path`: x, y, how far along). It replaces the `Line2D` and the `Sprite2D` (and the shader's two modes). A bent beam's pieces each keep to their side of the bend's middle, so the glow is not doubled there; a particle's origin across the beam follows the beam's middle where the particle is (it followed the pixel's). Looks the same in the beam screenshots. Not yet timed on the GPU: the four-bot run casts no beam and the web stress run fails to start (on main too, see below).
- **The trail searches its path once:** its four inlined binary searches (a particle's moment, a tail pixel, lightning's two step ends) are one call of `samples_before`, which searches two keys at once: the compiler builds a function's loop again for every call site. In game 2.8 s / 1.7 s for its two variants (was 3.5 / 2.4). The rest is the particle routine (`spark`) itself: without it the vertex stage is ~0.3 s. Turning the LOOK TABLE into uniforms or a cheaper hash did not help on its own; it is code volume. Splitting `spark` is the next step there.
- **Found on the way:** the Web Test's `--stress` run prints "STRESS FAILED: the run did not start" in Chrome (headless and real GPU, main and this branch); it passes on Linux.

## 2026-10-29: Teleport numbers (staffs C)

- **Teleport blinks 96 px for 4 mana, 2.5 s cooldown, complexity 2.** The three dashes move 48, 80 and 128 px, all free every 2 s. A blink is at once (a dash takes time to travel and can be hit on the way), so it costs mana and a longer cooldown; 96 px is only a little past the medium dash and short of the long one, so the 4 mana and 2.5 s pay for the instant arrival, not for range. `speed` (0) and `lifetime` (0.1) are placeholders: the compiler takes the distance from `ray_length` alone. Its combos do not test an end point (blast, on expire): the combo arena does not move its caster, as for dash.

## 2026-10-29: Hook numbers (staffs C)

- **Hook flies 300 px/s for 0.5 s (150 px reach), 3 mana, 2 s cooldown, 1 damage, complexity 2.** It reaches farther than the long dash (128 px) but not across a room, and the pull takes `HOOK_SECONDS`, so it is a gap-closer that can be hit on the way; it costs a little less than the instant teleport (4 mana, 2.5 s). A miss pulls nobody. Its combos do not test an end point (blast, on expire): the combo arena does not move its caster, as for dash and teleport.

## 2026-10-29: Swap numbers (staffs C)

- **Swap flies 300 px/s for 0.5 s (150 px reach), 3 mana, 2.5 s cooldown, 1 damage, complexity 2.** Same flight and mana as the hook; the cooldown is 0.5 s longer because a swap also moves the target (an escape through an enemy; it can leave a chilled foe where you stood). A miss trades nobody. Its combos do not test an end point: the combo arena does not move its caster, as for dash, teleport and hook.

## 2026-10-29: Decoy numbers (staffs C)

- **Decoy lasts 4 s, 5 mana, 4 s cooldown, complexity 2, base damage 1 (the range's minimum; a decoy deals none).** The cooldown is the lifetime because it starts at the cast, so a decoy can be recast the moment the last one ends; 5 mana is what limits how often. Its two combos check that the cast asks for a decoy (the new combo check `spawned`, which fails without the card) and that the wizard's other staff works beside it; they are not showcases. The combo arena has no `ai`, so the lure and the end point (`blast · decoy`, `on expire · decoy`) are tested in `test_entities.gd` and `test_spells_payload_carriers.gd`.

## 2026-10-29: Familiar numbers (staffs C)

- **Familiar lasts 8 s, 6 mana, 8 s cooldown, complexity 2, base damage 1 (it deals none itself; its payload does).** It casts every second, so 8 s is about eight payload casts for one cast of mana; the cooldown equals the lifetime so there is one familiar per card at a time, and the payload's own cost limits the rest. Its combos check that the cast asks for a familiar; the casting is tested in `test_entities.gd` and `test_spells_payload_carriers.gd` (the combo arena has no `ai`).

## 2026-10-29: Beams and fissures bounce (owner)

- **`bounce · beam` and `bounce · fissure` turn at walls** (owner: "do 2"; before, bounce did nothing on a beam and the compiler did not call it wasted). The ray reflects off the wall face it meets by the same rule a bouncing shot uses (`ProjectileManager._bounced`, shared now: one face flips one axis, a corner sends it straight back), steps 0.5 px out of the wall as a shot does, and goes on straight for the length it has left. One turn per bounce (the card's 3, counts add as always). No cap (owner): only the beam's length ends its turns. The path keeps room for `RAY_TURN_ROOM` (128) turns; a leg between turns is about 7 px at the least (8 px wall cells, 0.5 px out), and the tightest gap a test sweeps turns 51 times in 360 px. A homing beam's curve was dropped after its first turn (it went straight); since the same day it homes again from every turn, see "A homing beam homes after every turn".
- **What a turned beam does:** it hits the nearest `pierce + 1` targets along its whole walked path (each target once per ray, at its nearest point), is spent on its last target as before, sends a wall impact (a spark) at every turn and one where it ends on a wall. Its `blast` and `on expire` happen at its real end, past the turns, heading the way the last leg went.
- **`on wall` fires at every wall, not only the last** (owner: "I feel like it should, also just in general"; this replaces B2a's "bounces do not send `on wall`"). The manager sends its own `wall_met` at every bounce of a shot (with `event_slot` set to the shot, so its record's `max_fires` counts it) and, for a ray sent with `hit`, at every turn and at the wall it ends on (counted against the beam's fires). A shot's last wall still goes through `projectile_expired`. The point is 0.5 px out of the wall; the payload goes the way the shot or beam goes on (off the wall after a bounce). Before, a beam never fired `on wall` at all. Every wall counts against the trigger's `max_fires`.
- **How it is drawn:** the manager leaves the walked path in `ray_path`; a live beam copies it into its own points (`BEAM_POINTS` grew by `RAY_TURN_ROOM`, and Feel's `LIVE_BEAM_POINTS` to 200), so the existing beam line follows it; a fissure sends one `form_shown` beam cue per straight leg. A ray without bounces is unchanged, to the bit.

## 2026-10-29: Triggers have no fire count (owner)

- **`max_fires` is gone** (owner: "Remove the max_fires for everything. Unless there is something that really should not have it removed?"). Every trigger fires every time its event happens while its carrier lives: `on hit` at every hit of a piercing shot or chain jump, a live beam's at every tick on every target, `timer` every interval for the shot's whole life (a `hold` carrier's for as long as it is on), `on hurt` at every hurt, `on block` at every block, `on kill` and `on reaction` at every one, `on wall` at every wall. The field is removed from `TriggerBase`, `CompiledSpell` and the nine cards, and the per-shot, per-beam and per-ray counts from `Spells`.
- **Nothing needed it for safety:** every payload still goes through `_take_fire`, the per-step fire cap (`SpellLimits.MAX_FIRES_PER_STEP`, 32 a budget a step: one budget per seat, one shared by every actor no seat drives), and the projectile cap, the chain cap and the ray depth bound the rest. Payloads nest only as deep as the cards go.
- **What to watch:** with no count, the fire cap is reached far more often (a split nova with `on hit`, a bouncing beam in a tight gap that turns many times a tick). What is over it is dropped, first come first served, so one busy carrier can take a seat's whole step; the enemies' shared budget is one seat's size. `fires_dropped` counts it but nothing reports it yet.

## 2026-10-29: What the fire cap costs (measured, native)

- **How:** `tests/benchmark/fire_cap.gd`, headless native on the owner's WSL machine (timings vary up to about 2x run to run there). The cap is now a variable, `Spells.max_fires_per_step` (default still `MAX_FIRES_PER_STEP`, 32), and `Spells.fires_made` counts fires.
- **Real builds never reach it:** four seats with the heaviest trigger staffs of today's cards (split nova `on hit` split bolt, a bouncing beam `on wall` nova, an orbit `timer` split nova, a double pierce bolt with two `on hit` levels) and eight enemy casters, cast as fast as their cooldowns allow at a crowd that never dies: at most 12 fires in a step, none dropped, at every cap from 32 to none. Cooldowns, not the cap, hold the fire rate.
- **A burst can:** a nova split three times, cast inside a 100-target crowd with `on hit`, makes up to 95 fires in one step. What a fire costs is mostly the shots it makes: about 35 us native per fire of a bolt in the step it fires (60 us over its life), about 300 us for a nova (24 shots). The worst step was 5.5-8.7 ms with bolt payloads and 33 ms with nova payloads (desktop shot cap), against 2-5 ms without the trigger. So the cost follows shots spawned per step, not fires; a fire cap of N allows N x (payload shots) spawns in a step.
- **Not measured yet:** web (WASM runs the same GDScript several times slower; the stress run already fills the web frame with game logic).

## 2026-10-29: No limits on interactions (owner)

- **The rule** (owner: "we dont limit the interactions, they follow the rules. Then player can then experiment and always get expected results"). Nothing caps what a card or a trigger does; engine load is spread over steps or made room for, never dropped.
- **Fires wait instead of being dropped.** `MAX_FIRES_PER_STEP` (32 fires, the rest dropped) is replaced by `FIRE_SHOTS_PER_STEP` (256): the shots one budget's trigger fires may make in a step. A fire past it waits in the pending queue (`_fire`, `_run_waiting_fires`) and fires as the next step opens, oldest first, cast by its shot if that still flies; one fire always goes in a fresh step. The window runs from one `Spells.step` to the next, so one frame can hold up to twice it. Measured (`fire_cap.gd`, native): a nova-payload burst's worst step is about 12 ms paced against 18-33 ms unpaced; bolt payloads are hardly touched. Web is not measured.
- **Removed caps:** the chain jumps per step (16; a beam's jump ring now grows), the waiting launches (128, and the shared budget's 32; the queue grows), the live beams (64, 16 shared; a beam no longer ends the oldest, the rows grow, and Feel's beam lines with them), a homing beam's 8 legs (it bends on as often as its pierce gives), a fissure's 16 targets (it hits everything on its line), a ray's 64 hits, 360 px length, 4 nesting levels and 80 path points (the scratch of each level grows; a ray walks every wall cell it crosses), the growth card's 4x (a shot grows until it is as big as the room), the instant homing bend's 90 degrees (it bends up to all the way round, 180), the arc's and the timer's 0.1 s floors (a shot zaps and a timer fires at most once a physics step), and the 64 shot moves (the table grows; past it a move used to end at once).
- **Kept, as pacing that drops nothing:** the arc zaps (32 a step, the rest a step later), the magnet pulls (16 a step, taking turns) and the projectile manager's hit-work budget. **Still open with the owner:** the projectile cap (500 web, 3,000 desktop; it drops the oldest shot).
- **Piercing hits: hit again after leaving** (owner's pick, 2026-10-29). A piercing shot hits every target its path passes in one step (it was 3 a step) and a target once while it touches it; once its path no longer touches the target it may hit it again, so an orbit ring hits a target each time it comes round, however big the crowd (it remembered its last 3 targets). The memory is per shot and grows; one scan per step finds every new target, with the remembered ones marked first, so the cost is the targets touched plus those remembered (a first version that scanned again after each hit was quadratic in a crowd). `test_projectiles_step_exact`'s recording was made again: its first change is a slow shot that pushes a target ahead of it and hits it again when it catches up, then the scene's handlers cascade.

## 2026-10-29: Long beams are thinned by their turns (owner)

- **What:** a live beam whose path has more points than `shot_beam.gdshader` holds (80) used to be drawn from evenly spaced points, which could cut across a bounce's corner. `Feel._thin_beam` now keeps its two ends and the 78 points where it turns most (a wall bounce turns it sharply, a target it bends at less, a curve point hardly) and drops the gentlest curve points. Only how it looks changes; its hits and turns are the runtime's, whole.
- **Cost (native, `_draw_live_beams`):** a normal beam is unchanged (16 us a frame); a 200-point beam costs 123 us a frame while it is on (it reads its points once, ranks the turns and sorts them). Only builds with many pierce or bounce cards get there.
- **Why not more points:** a bigger uniform array risks WebGL's limits and the owner's GPU (#400). The full fix (drawing a long beam as several meshes) is in BACKLOG.md, with the fissure's beam cue slots.

## 2026-10-29: A homing beam homes after every turn (owner)

- **The owner:** "A homing beam, should also home after bouncing on a wall! [...] This should be the general case for projectiles aswell". A turn at a wall takes none of a beam's cards away: from the turn, along its way off the wall, it homes again as it did from the staff, with the legs (one per pierce, plus one) it had not reached before the wall, then goes on straight for the length left. The projectile manager walks the walls and asks the spell runtime at each turn (`ray_points(..., steer)`, `Spells._beam_steer`), so the rule is the runtime's one homing rule, not a second copy. Shots already did this (a bounce only turns their velocity; they stay homing); a test now holds it.
- **The general-rules pass:** the owner asked for a pass that finds every card that breaks the general rules (special cases, silent no-ops, effects dropped by another event, caps). An audit of the compiler, the runtime and the manager is the first step; a permanent test of every card against every carrier is the proposed follow-up.

## 2026-10-29: Echo, zaps, relay and jumps keep the whole hit (phase R, R1a)

- **Rule 2 (nothing takes a card away), docs/milestones/M2-rules.md findings 1, 2, 14:** an echo hit, an arc zap and a relay's merge are the carrier's whole `HitProfile` (`HitProfile.scaled(share, knock_share)`, `copy()`): heal, slam, every element and status, the kind. Only the strength changes: an echo at ECHO_DAMAGE (knockback whole, as before), a zap at ARC_DAMAGE (knockback too; a heavy shot's zap now slams for its share). An echoed mend bolt heals teammates; a relay mend bolt that takes on frost still heals.
- **Relay adds each element with its status** (as the donor's hit had it), after the shot's own; before, it took only the first status, and only when the shot had none.
- **A hit carries on:** a chain jump hits with what the hit it jumped from hit with (an echo's half, a relay's merge), on every tick of a live beam or swing, not only the first; a zap's jumps hit as the zap's shot does. A relay shot's zaps take on the merge too, and a zap is a hit of its shot for relay. No allocation per hit except a relay merge (two profiles, as before one).

## 2026-10-29: A chain jump keeps every card (phase R, R1b)

- **Rules 2 and 6, docs/milestones/M2-rules.md finding 3:** a flying chain jump is its carrier fired again from the hit: homing, bounce, ghost, spiral, linger, growth, sticky, return, shield and pierce ride on (`Spells._apply_travel`, which now also sets homing, is the one place for a shot from the staff and a jump), as arc, magnet and its trigger already did, with one jump fewer. Before, each jump was a plain bolt with pierce 0.
- **Only the aim, the source exclusion and the speed differ:** a jump flies at least `CHAIN_MIN_SPEED` (a rune or a slow orb has no speed of its own to jump at) and as far as its carrier would fly (`lifetime × speed`, at least far enough to reach its target); before, it lived just long enough to reach its target.
- **Not changed (R4):** a beam's or fissure's jump is still a ray that pierces nothing.

## 2026-10-29: Heavy and growth cards multiply (phase R, R1c)

- **Rule 3, docs/milestones/M2-rules.md finding 16:** behaviour strengths were summed for every card, but heavy's strength is a knockback factor and growth's a growth rate: two heavy cards gave ×8, not ×16. `StaffCompiler.stacked` now multiplies the behaviours in `MULTIPLIED` (heavy, growth) and adds the rest (counts, seconds, pixels). Homing keeps its own stacking (`HOMING_STACK`). Two growth cards are ×4 either way; three are ×8 now (×6 before).

## 2026-10-29: Every carrier blasts at its end point (phase R, R1d)

- **Rule 1, docs/milestones/M2-rules.md findings 10 and 11:** a hook that misses now blasts where it ends, as every expired shot does (before, its blast waited for an arrival that never came). A hook that hits still blasts where its caster arrives. A rain blasts where it lands, before its payload falls (before, `blast · rain` did nothing).

## 2026-10-29: Hold in a payload is a hold (phase R, R1f)

- **Finding 13** (docs/milestones/M2-rules.md): a `hold` carrier in a payload (`on hit · bolt · hold · orbit`) got the hold's long life but was not listed as the staff's hold. It never switched off, reserved no mana, and lived until the staff was rebuilt.
- **One path for every firing (rule 6).** `_fire_spell` switches a hold on whenever a hold carrier made shots: the cast's own, a payload's, a burst's later firing. The cast no longer lists its holds afterwards. A held payload sets `is_holding`, so the next press switches it off and casts nothing, as for the cast's own hold.
- **Many firings, one hold.** Every firing of one carrier has the same tag, so a payload that fires ten times is listed once, and the next press ends all its shots. The staff reserves the mana of the cast the carrier belongs to (`CompiledCast.mana`, payloads included), once, as before.
- **It may switch on again.** A shot of the cast still in the air after the hold was switched off can fire the payload again; that switches the hold on again and reserves the mana again. It follows the rule: a held carrier is on while it lives.
- **An old build holds nothing.** An edit (or a closed caster) switches the staff's holds off. A shot of the old build that fires its held payload later makes a carrier that lives its own lifetime, not `HOLD_LIFETIME`, because no press could end it.

## 2026-10-29: Moves on a shot add up (phase R, R1e)

- **The bug** (finding 12, rules 3 and 6): a shot kept one move at a time, so `move_shot` replaced the move under way. A payload's `mirror · recoil` pushed its shot forward (the second push replaced the first, where on the wizard the two add up and cancel), and a hook or dash on a shot dropped the rest of a recoil.
- **Now:** a shot keeps a list of the moves under way (one growing pool, as the hit memory; no cap), and each runs on its own time, added on top of the shot's motion. Two opposite pushes cancel; a hook after a recoil gets both. A blink (a teleport or swap) moves the shot at once and keeps the moves under way. `move_shot` is the one place this lives; `Spells` did not change.
- **What a player sees:** a payload with `mirror · recoil` leaves its shot on its line, and a shot's recoil is not lost when it hooks or dashes.
- `test_projectiles_step_exact`'s recording was made again: the shots do the same; only its state line shows the moves under way, added up, where it showed a finished move's stale numbers.

The rules phase (R2, R3) had no tasks in `docs/NEXT.md`, so they are added to the bot's Queue, with the clip recorder leftover. The owner-lane melee tasks I2, I3, I4, I5 and I8 have merged (#457, #460, #463, #464) and are ticked off. Still open in the owner lane: B5, B6a, B8, and R4.

## 2026-10-29: The compiler lists triggers that cannot fire (phase R, R2b)

- **Rules 1 and 4, docs/milestones/M2-rules.md findings 7 and 8:** a trigger whose event never happens on its carrier is listed as wasted, with the reason, instead of doing nothing silently. `StaffCompiler.trigger_runs(event, motion)` and `trigger_why_not` are the table, read from what the runtime does today (the table is in M2-rules.md). The missing triggers are not built here; R4 decides them.
- **The cards an event needs count too:** on block needs a shield or deflect on its carrier, on reaction an infusion or relay (a hit with no element sets off no reaction), and on wall is wasted on a ghost shot. On kill and on reaction on a carrier that hits nothing itself (dash, teleport, rain, decoy, familiar) need a blast: the blast's area hit carries the carrier's tag, so its kills and reactions fire them.
- **The payload is still read, then wasted.** The cards after a wasted trigger are read as its payload, as when it fires, so the reading order and the casts do not change when R4 makes the trigger work. They are not attached, each is listed as wasted ("Nothing casts it"), and they cost no mana or time.
- **Tests changed:** the trigger clips put a shield on `on_block`'s bolt and fire on `on_reaction`'s (`tests/clips/scenarios.gd`), and `test_on_kill_and_on_reaction_fire_from_the_hit` gives its bolt fire: a plain bolt never blocks or reacts at runtime.

## 2026-10-29: The compiler lists every card that does nothing; a test plays them all (phase R, R2a, R3)

- **One table (rule 1):** `StaffCompiler.behaviour_waste(behaviour, motion, blasts)` says, for every modifier behaviour on every carrier, that it runs or why it is wasted, in words a player reads ("it only changes a shot in flight, and a rune stays where it is"). `behaviour_runs` asks it. It replaces the three-case rule and `_why_not`. A wasted card changes nothing else (its numbers too).
- **What it lists, from the runtime as it is:** travel cards (spiral, linger, growth, sticky, ghost) off flying shots; bounce off shots and rays; chain, siphon and possess on carriers that hit nothing themselves (dash, teleport, rain, decoy, familiar); heavy there too unless a blast carries it; magnet and relay off live shots; shield on rain, decoy, familiar; mirror off straight shots, beams, fissures and swings; hold on dash, teleport, rain, fissure, swing; recoil on dash and teleport.
- **Infusions and numbers:** an infusion on a carrier that hits nothing is wasted unless a blast carries it. A numbers-only card is wasted where none of its effects count (damage where nothing hits, speed and lifetime on a fissure or teleport, shots on a move, spread on a carrier with no aim, pierce on a fissure or swing); its costs (mana, a slower cast, less damage) are no effect.
- **Too short to tick:** arc and timer on a carrier that ends before the first zap or tick (a hook, swap or nova at 0.4 to 0.5 s against a 0.5 s interval) are wasted with the times. R4 may decide the first tick should come sooner instead.
- **R3, the permanent check:** `tests/core/test_card_rules.gd` finds every card and carrier through the registry and every behaviour and event from the enums (no list), plays each card on each carrier in a small world with and without it, and fails on a card that changes nothing and is not listed as wasted, or on a behaviour or event with no probe. Rule 2 checks (an effect survives a bounce, jump, echo, payload) are not in it yet.
- **R2c:** the builder, the radial builder and the staff row already show every wasted slot with its reason; nothing changed there.
- **For R4:** every W here is a cell the owner may turn into ✓ (M2-rules.md R4).

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4a: fixes (owner decisions, phase R)

- **Every recoil runs.** The actor's mover kept 8 pushes and dropped a ninth (rule 5); its list of running pushes now grows when full (no allocation in the steady state).
- **A shield always blocks.** Shield and deflect checks no longer spend the per-step hit-work budget, so a crowded step cannot let an enemy shot through. Measured first: the plain full scan was 4–6× slower in a 1,500-shot scene with 250 shields, so a shield now looks only at the shots on the target grid's cells its path could touch (the shots are put on the cells once a step, the first time a shield needs them); within about 1.2–1.5× of the budgeted scan (which skipped blocks), and the step recording only changes its work counter.
- **Switch-off and edit drop only what they end.** A hold switch-off drops the waiting firings of its hold carriers only. An edit drops the cast's own waiting launches (the wizard casts them: burst repeats, echoes, a rain, a waiting `on cast`); a waiting payload of a shot or a point keeps its old build registered, as a shot in the air does, and fires. A caster that closes still drops all of its own.
- **A hook ends once, where its shot ends:** at the caster's arrival when the hit spent the hook shot, at the end of its flight when it had pierce left. Its `on expire` and `blast` go off there only (replaces "both accepted for now").
- **A returning shot keeps homing.** On the way back the shot heads for its caster, and homing bends that heading toward its target at the homing card's turn rate (back toward the straight line with no target), never more than 60° off, so it always closes in and cannot circle. It still ends at its caster, or when its life runs out, as before.
- **A zap is not its shot meeting a wall:** an arc zap that ends on a wall sets off no `on wall`. The dead tenth `WHEN` phrase ("through a friend", the removed on ally trigger) is gone.

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4h: travel cards on orbit, rune and wall (owner decisions, item 19)

- **The owner's decision:** growth grows a rune (its trigger circle), an orbit's shots and a wall's shots each second. On an orbit, linger stops the circling for its seconds (the shot keeps to its caster), spiral weaves its radius by its width, and sticky takes an orbit shot that hits off its circle onto the target. Still wasted: bounce, return and homing on all three ("it does not fly"), ghost on an orbit ("an orbit already passes walls"), linger, spiral and sticky on a rune and a wall ("it does not move").
- **One mechanism:** `Spells._apply_travel` runs on every shot a carrier makes (bolt, rune, orbit, a wall's shots, a chain jump), and each `ProjectileManager` setter takes what the shot can physically do: growth any shot, linger, spiral and sticky a shot that moves (`_moves`), bounce, ghost, return and homing a straight shot. The wall's own copy of shield and deflect is gone (it calls `_apply_travel`). The existing passes do the rest: linger holds the orbit (the orbit pass keeps a held shot on its caster without turning), the stuck pass takes a sticky orbit shot, and the orbit pass adds the spiral's sine to its radius (an orbit joins no spiral pass).
- **The waste table lists fewer cells:** `behaviour_waste` lists growth off shots that stay in the world, and linger, spiral, sticky off a rune and a wall only.

## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4 F2: one strike for every area; a blast's hits are hits (owner decisions)

- **The owner's decision ("Ok lets try it"):** a blast's hits are its carrier's hits. A blast goes off at the end point and strikes everything round it, its caster's own body included and through walls as before (its centre may be in the wall a shot ended on), with what the carrier hits with now (an echo's half, a relay's merge) and the chain jumps it has left. So on hit, chain, siphon, possess and relay work on what it catches, and every hit card works on a decoy or familiar with a blast. A mend blast still heals the caster's team (Combat's one heal rule). Each caught body is a hit like any other: it wears a decoy down and Feel shows an impact on it.
- **One strike:** swing, slam, dash, teleport, rain and blast (and the cards that land on a point) call `Spells._strike`, which hits each body the sweep found once, outward from where it is struck, in the beam hit context, and returns what it hit with; a swing, slam and dash keep that for their next strike, so relay on them lasts the whole sweep, ring or dash as on a live beam. A whole-circle `Projectiles.sweep` strikes from its centre and heads outward. An echoed slam now strikes at half (it read `_echo_spell`, which no hit reads).
- **No second blast, no cap:** a blast's hits blast no more (only a spent shot's own hit does), and a hook's or swap's blast moves nothing (only its shot's hit pulls or swaps: else every arrival would pull again). `area_hit_requested`, Combat's area hit and `AREA_DEPTH` are gone; blasts set off by a blast's kills are payload fires, paced by `FIRE_SHOTS_PER_STEP`. The hit kind of a blast's hit is its carrier's (`shot`, `ray`, ...), no longer `burst`; a relic that guards against explosions would need it back.

## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4 F5: one Dash and a Reach card (owner decisions, 2026-10-30)

- **One Dash:** Medium Dash and Long Dash are gone (folders, icons, lore, combos); the kept Dash has the medium's numbers (80 px at 300 px/s) and the starter keeps it. A longer dash is `reach · dash` (128 px).
- **Reach** ("goes farther"): `ModifierBase.reach_mult` (1.6) multiplies the carrier's range in the compiler's one multiplier loop: a flying shot's `lifetime` (bolt, nova, hook, swap), every other carrier's `ray_length` (beam, dash, teleport, rain, wall, swing, slam). Several multiply (two: x2.56). Wasted on a rune, an orbit, a decoy and a familiar (`StaffCompiler.reaches`, through `_numbers_waste`: no new row). Look: glitter (12), shared with sniper (both say "long shot").
- **A wall's distance is its card's `ray_length`** (28 px, `WALL_REACH` only without one), so reach moves it farther; the compiler's ray hit kind is now by motion (a beam, `motion == RAY`, and rain), not "has a `ray_length`".
- **Ring** (a multicast that fires the carrier's shots in a full circle, like nova) is in docs/BACKLOG.md as an idea.

## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4i: homing on placed carriers (owner decisions, 2026-10-30)

- **The owner's decision:** homing was wasted on a rune, a wall and a rain. A homing rune drifts: it creeps toward its target at `SpellLimits.HOMING_DRIFT` (24 px/s: below the slowest walking enemy, 30, so it is a trap that follows, not a chaser; over a rune's 8 s about the card's 160 px reach), with a bolt's pick and reach; with nothing in reach it stays put. A rain's and a wall's aim point moves onto a target near it, as homing bends a beam's or a teleport's aim. An orbit stays wasted: it circles its caster, nothing to aim.
- **One mechanism each:** `set_homing` takes any shot but an orbit, and `_pass_homing` moves a shot with no speed at HOMING_DRIFT (one branch, no rune case elsewhere). `Spells._homed_place` asks the one homing search (`homing_point`, any way round, the card's reach round the point) for a rain and a wall. A wall's shots then keep still (its homing went into where it stands; a wall's chain jump, a flying shot, still homes).
- **The waste table lists fewer cells:** `homing_steers` adds rune, wall and rain; homing is wasted on an orbit, a fissure, a swing, a slam, a decoy and a familiar ("it has nothing to aim, and ...").

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4d: every lasting carrier keeps a clock (owner decisions)

- **Items 7 and 10 of the R4 list** (owner, 2026-10-29). Timer and on hurt now work on every carrier that lasts: a shot, a beam while it is on, a rain while it falls, a decoy and a familiar while they live, a swing while it sweeps, a dash while the caster moves. The payload fires from where the carrier is now: the shot, the beam's tip, the rain's spot, the decoy, what a familiar circles (its wizard, shot or point), the caster during a dash (on hurt, as before, from the hurt actor). Fissure, slam and teleport stay wasted: "it is over at once" (`StaffCompiler.lasts`).
- **The first tick:** a timer's first tick and an arc's first zap come after the interval or half the carrier's life, whichever is shorter (`Spells.first_tick`), then every interval. A hook or swap (0.5 s) ticks at 0.25 s, a nova (0.4 s) at 0.2 s; a bolt, orbit, rune and wall are unchanged. The compiler's "ends before its first tick / zap" wastes are gone.
- **One mechanism:** the per-slot timer list and hurt list are replaced by one list of live carriers (`_lc_*` in `spells.gd`): every lasting carrier with a timer or on hurt joins it as it starts, with where it is (a slot, a beam row, a swing row, an actor's body, a fixed point), and leaves when that row ends, its time is up, or its entity ends. `_lc_where` is the one "where is it now".

## 2026-10-29: Card rules R4f, cards on beams and fissures (phase R, owner decisions)

- **Item 13, a ray's chain jump pierces as its carrier does:** a beam's jump keeps the beam's pierce, a fissure's pierces everything on its line (`Spells._ray_pierce`, one rule for `_launch` and the jump queue; the jump's own `pierce` argument is gone). A jump of a swing's, slam's or zap's hit follows the same rule (their carrier's pierce).
- **Item 15, relay on a beam and a fissure:** a ray with relay takes on the infusions of the actors its line hits (the same `_relay` merge as a shot, into `_ray_hit`), for that hit and every one after; a live beam keeps it for as long as it is on (`_bm_hit`, which replaces the echo flag: an echo's half and a relay's merge are one per-beam hit). Every actor it passes adds what it has, as for a piercing shot. Swings and slams still reset it each step: relay stays wasted on them.
- **Item 20:** ghost takes a beam or a fissure (and their jumps) through walls for their whole length (`ray`'s `ghost`; `on wall` is then wasted on them, as on a ghost shot). Magnet on a beam pulls toward its line every step it is on: `pull_requested` gains the line's other end (`to`; a shot's is its own point) and Combat pulls each actor toward its nearest point on the line, one pull per piece of the beam's path (on a curve or a wave the last piece in reach wins). Growth widens a beam's hits (and its guard) by its factor every second it is on, up to the room, as a shot grows; Feel draws it as wide (`beam_growth`). Spiral waves a beam's path sideways by half its strength, a sine every `BEAM_WAVE` (112 px, about a spiral bolt's) along it, from the staff and again from every turn at a wall (the walk's `steer`, as homing); the wave is sampled 8 points a wave, so a 240 px beam is 19 points, inside the shader's 80.
- **Wasted still, with the reason:** linger, sticky and return on both; magnet, growth and spiral on a fissure (it is over at once; spiral is not free there: a fissure walks one straight `ray`). A beam's chain jump flies straight at its target (no wave).
- **Simpler:** the waste table lost the beam and fissure cells for ghost, relay, magnet, growth and spiral; `StaffCompiler.rays` names the line carriers once; `on wall` with ghost asks the table instead of "a flying shot".
- **Relay on a swing and a slam (merging R4f with R4b, 2026-10-30):** a swing and a slam strike through the same ray hit (`_ray_hit`), so with R4f their relay takes on what an earlier body of the same sweep or strike was infused with, and R4b's forced check found them listed as wasted while they changed the cast. Relay now runs on every carrier that hits (`StaffCompiler.hits`), one row instead of "a shot or a line".

## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4 F4: a boomerang always comes home; a closed caster's payloads fire on (owner decisions)

- **A returning shot always comes home:** its way back takes no life (`_pass_return` gives back the step's life while it heads home), so a caster who walks away is still reached; it ends with its expiry there, and vanishes only when its caster's anchor is gone. Homing's 60° bend (R4a) keeps it closing in. Nothing else read the life on the way home (linger fires before the turn; growth, trails, the live-carrier clock and arcs go by the step or the shot's row). A stick or a deflect sets the life anew, as before.
- **A closed caster's payloads fire on:** `close_caster` drops only the caster's own waiting launches (a cast's later firings, a rain's landing), the same predicate as an edit (R4a); its shots in the air already flew on, and now their waiting payloads (an echo, a fire waiting for room) fire too, keeping their build as after an edit.
## 2026-10-30: Card rules R4 F6: the wall is solid (owner decisions, 2026-10-30)

- **The owner's decision:** a wall is a real wall, a short-lived piece of terrain that bodies cannot walk through and that stops shots, like the room's walls. It was five still stones that each hurt the first thing touching them and crumbled; enemies walked through the gaps.
- **One body:** `Spells._launch_wall` makes one still shot that pierces everything (its hits never spend it) and `Projectiles.set_solid` makes it a wall while it lives: its row of stones lies in the wall grid (a count per cell on top of the room's mark, so it goes cleanly when it ends) and is a `StaticBody2D` on the walls' physics layer, which the actors already collide with. Everything that asks the grid (shots, beams, `on wall`, ghost, dashes, teleports, knockback into walls) and every body's `move_and_slide` sees it with no change of their own. The enemies walk straight at their target (no pathfinding), so they slide along it.
- **Its hits:** it hits along its row, each body once while it stays against it, with all its cards. Pierce is wasted on it ("a wall stands still through every hit").
- **Homing:** it creeps toward a target as one piece, as a rune does, and stops at a room wall; the R4i guard and the wall's `_homed_place` call are gone (a rain keeps it).
- **Shield and deflect:** decided by the general rule, a shield or deflect acts on the shots that touch its carrier, so it guards the whole row: another team's shot that meets the wall is turned (deflect) or stopped with a block (shield, `on block`, its own payloads stay quiet) while charges last; without one it ends on the wall as on any wall. It keeps the mirror-wall and shielded-wall combos working with one function shared with the shot-against-shot guard (`_guard_meets`), where wasting both would have added table rows.
- **Its own payloads leave it:** a shot cast by a live solid shot does not end on that wall (its row is lifted while that shot looks); every other shot born inside it ends on it at once.
## 2026-10-30: The atom: damage below a point, specks, fast homing (owner decisions, 2026-10-30)

- **The atom is the base carrier:** the bolt carrier is renamed `atom` (`CarrierAtom`); Bolt and Nova are cards on it, a bolt being a bigger, slower atom. The Atom card is a speck: 0.3 damage, 1100 px/s for 0.3 s, hit radius 1.5, cooldown 0.02 (about 14 shots a second held). A form's cooldown may be 0.
- **Damage below a point, and decimal health:** card, compiled and hit damage are decimals and are no longer rounded (this replaces "rounded, at least 1" in the number rules above). Health is decimal too (owner, 2026-10-30; it was whole points everywhere only because it began so): `Health`, Combat's rows, `EnemyBase.max_health`, `damage_dealt`, `hit_landed`, `actor_hurt`, `heal_dealt` and `player_stats_changed` carry floats, so a 0.3 speck takes 0.3 health and is told as 0.3. A first try kept health whole with a leftover per target; it is gone. Health under 0.0001 after a hit is 0. Feel shakes for a hit of part of a point by its share; the training dummy's numbers and the HUD show a tenth (the HUD's health number rounded up, so a wizard with 0.2 left reads 1, not 0). A shock's jump and a static discharge are their exact share of the hit, no longer at least a point. An echo of a 5 bolt is 2.5, a piercing bolt 4.5. A mend still takes at least a point from an enemy. The builder shows a cast's damage to a tenth.
- **Specks and fast tails:** a shot of hit radius below `SPECK_RADIUS` (2 px) draws a one-pixel core. A shot too fast to leave a tail pixel every pixel for its tail's life leaves one every 2 to 4 pixels, so its tail reaches as far with the same 128 slots (no new quads; slower shots are unchanged).
- **Homing is a curve, at any speed:** the Homing card's strength is the radius of the tightest curve it turns on (73 px, what 3 radians a second turned at a bolt's 220 px/s), not a rate. Whatever moves turns its speed / that radius radians a second (a shot out and on a return's way home, a dash, a moved shot), and keeps the curve when its speed changes later, so a 1100 px/s atom bends onto what a bolt would and a slow nova shot turns as gently as its curve says. Stacking divides the curve by `HOMING_STACK`. A beam or teleport bends as far as the curve turns over `INSTANT_HOMING_REACH` (55 px; 43 degrees for one card, as before). This replaces the first try, a rate that only grew above a bolt's pace.
- **Guards along the step:** a swing's, beam's, slam's or decoy's guard tests a shot at points along its last step (one per shot width, at most `GUARD_PATH_SAMPLES`), not only where it is, so a small fast shot no longer slips past a beam's line between two steps.
- **A swing's guard is a parry:** every step of its sweep its whole arc meets shots, and a step of each shot's way ahead (`shots_in_wedge`'s `ahead`). Before, it met only the slice the blade turned through that step, and the blade leans past its arc at both ends, so it guarded about one step of its 0.06 s sweep: a bolt sits in its reach for several steps and was always met, an atom crosses in one or two and was met about half the time, or went from outside the reach into the wizard in one step. Now an atom that reaches the wizard during the sweep is always met; one that comes during the wind-up still hits. Its strikes on bodies stay with the blade.
- **Arc is ready at once and reaches beside its shot:** a zap is ready when its shot is cast and waits its interval after each zap; before, its first zap waited the interval or half the shot's life (an atom zapped only from 165 px out, a bolt from 110 px, a rune sat half a second). A zap never takes the body its shot hits next (`ProjectileManager.first_on_path` along its way ahead, as far as the zap reaches or the shot flies; for a still shot, what touches it), so arc does not double the hit on its target or set `on hit` off twice there: it hits what is beside the shot's way or what it missed. Timers keep their first tick (`first_tick`).
- **Arc crackles at random and looks like lightning (owner, 2026-10-30: "much more unpredictable", "waaaay cooler"):** ready-at-once made a stream of atoms zap from the same spot each time. Now a shot's first zap comes at a random moment of its first interval and each next one 0.5 to 1.5 intervals after the last (the card's rate on average), at a body in reach picked at random with the near ones likelier (`ProjectileManager.pick_target`), still never the one its shot hits next. A zap at the edge of its reach misses 60% of the time (none point-blank) and strikes the air beside the body. With nothing near, a shot throws harmless sparks into the air about three times a second. The rolls are a hash of the shot's sequence number and a count (`Spells._arc_roll`), so runs and tests repeat. A zap is drawn as lightning (`shot_arc.gdshader`, shared with the static discharge): a jagged bolt that wanders widest in its middle, a white-hot core with coloured edges, up to three forks, a star and sparks where it lands, strobing and burning out from its ends, in its shot's colours. The card-rule probe gives arc a second scene that keeps casting (`SIDE_AGAIN`). Screenshots: `shot_arc_looks.gd`, `shot_live_arcs.gd`.
- **Arc is now Zap, a chance a body (owner, 2026-10-30):** the card is renamed Zap (id `zap`, `ModifierBase.Behaviour.ZAP`, `zap_*` fields; old saved staffs and staff codes with `arc` are not carried over, owner's call). The timer is gone: each body that comes within a zap shot's reach rolls the card's chance once per shot (50% a card, whatever its distance; more cards combine as chances, two are 75%), and on a win is zapped at once, the body the shot is about to hit too. Zap shots look every other step (`ZAP_EVERY`, half of them each step), which is soon enough at any speed and halves the cost. With nothing near, a shot still sparks into the air. The random-moment clock, the miss at the edge, `pick_target` and `first_on_path` of the entry above are gone. The combos that show it cast several times, each cast a roll.
- **A zap's moment is random too (owner, 2026-10-30):** a won zap no longer fires as its body comes in reach (a shot passing a body always zapped it from the same spot). It waits a random part of the time the body stays in reach as the shot flies on (`Spells._zap_window`: until the shot hits it, if it is on its way, else until the shot has passed through its reach; at most `ZAP_WAIT_MOST`, 0.6 s, which is also a still shot's), less a look's step, so a body ahead is zapped before the shot hits it. A body that leaves the reach first is zapped as it leaves, so a won roll is never lost; a shot keeps up to 8 waiting zaps (more are made at once).
- **Zap works on a beam (owner, 2026-10-31):** zap needed a carrier that stays in the air as a shot (`STAYS`), so on a beam it was wasted. It now also runs on a line (`ModifierBase.NEEDS`: `STAYS | LINE`). A live beam keeps a zap shot's state per row (`CarrierBeam._step_zaps`): each body of another team within the card's reach of its line, as far as it got (`Projectiles.targets_near` with a segment), rolls the chance once a beam, and on a win is zapped from the nearest point of the line at a random moment of at most `ZAP_WAIT_MOST` and the beam's life left; a body that leaves the reach first is zapped as it leaves. With nothing near, it sparks off a random point of its line. The zap is one shared ray (`Spells._zap_ray`, `CarrierKit.zap`), marked a zap (`RayHit.zapping`) so one ending on a wall sets off no `on wall` though a beam's has no slot; it takes on what the beam's relay took on. The zap cap's window now opens at the start of `Spells.step`, before the carriers step.
- **A zap shot rolls for a body again after a second (owner, 2026-10-31: "it should be allowed to zap the same target again after 1s (useful on a hold zap orbit)"):** a body no longer rolls once a shot. A zap shot or live beam remembers when it zapped each body, or when the body lost its roll, and rolls for it again `SpellLimits.ZAP_AGAIN` (1 s) after that moment (`_rec_zapped_at`, `_bm_zapped_at`). A won zap's moment counts from when it is due to be made, so two zaps of one body by one shot are at least a second apart. A held orbit, rune or beam beside an imp keeps zapping it; a bolt passing by is in reach too briefly to roll twice.
- **No zap from a carrier that is going (owner, 2026-10-31: "a zap should not trigger, even after a positive roll, if the carrier it triggers from is gone. E.g. a beam"):** a waiting zap was already dropped when its shot died or its beam ended. But a beam dissolves over the last quarter of its life and a zap's lightning stays 0.25 s, so a zap made near the end showed after the beam was gone. Now a beam zaps only until it starts to dissolve (`SpellLimits.BEAM_FADE`, 0.25 of its life left, which Feel's fade reads too): a won zap waits at most until then, a zap still waiting then is dropped, and a dissolving beam rolls for nothing and throws no sparks. A held beam ends when switched off, with no fade, so it zaps until then. A shot does not dissolve (its core goes at its end), so its zaps are as before: within its life, dropped when it dies.
- **A carrier's end is one hook (owner, 2026-10-31: "make this a general thing to hook into ... it should be easy to time things in the future"):** `Carrier.fade()` is the share of its life over which a carrier dissolves at its end (0 by default; a beam's `SpellLimits.BEAM_FADE`; none for a held one). `Carrier.active_left(life_left, span)` is how long it may still act, and `Carrier.fade_left(life_left, span)` how whole it looks, so the logic and the looks read one moment. A beam's zaps wait within `active_left` and Feel draws its fade from `CarrierBeam.beam_fade`; a shot's zaps do the same through `Spells._active_left` (with `Projectiles.get_span_of`), so a shot carrier that gets a fade later stops zapping as it fades with no other change. docs/CARRIERS.md, "Its end: when a carrier is going", says how to time something new on it.

## 2026-11-05: The sandbox in every build, no demo flag, a setup per mode (owner decisions)

- **Now:** the main menu's Sandbox button shows in every build channel (`nightly`, `stable` and `manual` too, not only `dev` and `preview`). It keeps the focus only in `dev` and `preview`, so a release build still opens on Play. The pause menu's Sandbox page follows the run as before (shown while in the sandbox).
- **Why:** the owner asked for the sandbox in all builds.
- **The demo flag is gone (owner, same day: "The sandbox should just show every card available. I think the demo = true should be removed"):** no `demo` on `SpellComponent`, `EnemyBase` or `RoomBase`, no `Registry.demo_only` (nor `FEATURE_DEMO`, `EVERY_CARD_CHANNELS`, `demo_only_for`), no `demo` feature on the Web presets, and every `demo = ...` line left the content. Every build, the web releases too, sees every card, enemy and room, so the sandbox's showcase staffs (SANDBOX_START_CARDS) and its enemy arena are whole everywhere. What a player may build with is set by the run mode instead (below). This replaces the curated web subset of docs/PLAN.md ("Web demo and full build") and the 2026-10-27 rule that only `dev` and `preview` web builds showed every card; if the web build ever outgrows its budgets, the Web preset's `exclude_filter` can leave content out, by the owner's decision.
- **Each mode has its own setup (owner, same day: "Instead we should have an initialization for each game mode"):** a new wizard gets its mode's staffs, start cards and bag, the bag handed over by flow with `PlayerController.seat_wizard` (`Flow.bag_for_mode()`). Play: start cards A `dash`, X `bolt` (no longer fire bolt), bag `dash` and `bolt` (`PLAY_BAG`). The horde: as before, A `dash`, X `swing`, bag `dash` and `swing` (`HORDE_BAG`, which the horde director's `START_BAG` matches; its level-up picks add to it). The sandbox: its showcase start cards and every spell card in the bag. A wizard seated with no bag (tests) still gets every card.

## 2026-11-06: Cards with no carrier land on a fallback carrier, by the same rules (owner decision)

- **Now:** the cards a group reads with no carrier after them land on one of two carriers no form card fires, `caster` (the cast's own group) and `point` (a payload's origin), in `core/spells/carriers/` (docs/CARRIERS.md, "The fallback carriers"). Which cards do something there follows from their yes/no answers and each card's needs, as on every carrier; the runtime launches them like any other. The fallback's own list of what means something there (`ModifierBase.RULES` "travel_only", a case for shield, a numbers check) is gone.
- **Why:** the owner asked whether an explosion with no carrier should be a kind of card of its own; the answer was that it already exists (a lone `blast` explodes on you, `on hit · bolt · blast` where the bolt hits), but as a special case beside the carriers. The owner chose to make it a carrier ("This sounds like a very clean design. Do it!"): one rule, and the card rules test now plays every card on it.
- **What changed in play:** chain, pull, possess, relay, penta and mirror now work there (they need a hit, which the strike is), and deflect on the caster (it turns the shots round you as you cast); echo and recoil, which were kept there but did nothing, now strike again and push. Split, hold and a shorter cooldown (short delay, faster) are wasted there: no shots, nothing lasts, no cooldown of its own.

## 2026-11-06: A multicast that adds no carrier is wasted (owner decision)

- **Now:** a multicast card whose extra draws find no carrier (`double · bolt` with one bolt, `double · fire` alone) is listed as wasted and costs nothing (`StaffCompiler._take_multicasts`). One that finds fewer than it asks for (`triple · bolt · bolt`) still works. The draws found go to the multicast cards in slot order, so in `double · triple · bolt · bolt` the triple is the wasted one. The fallback carrier counts as a carrier drawn: `triple · bolt · fire` pulls the fire onto the caster in the same cast, so the triple works. A burst works whenever its group fires anything, a scatter whenever it has a carrier to turn.
- **Why:** rule 1 of docs/milestones/M2-rules.md (nothing does nothing silently); the owner, asked whether a partly used multicast is wasted: "Mark it wasted when it adds zero carriers". `tests/core/test_card_rules.gd` plays every multicast with one carrier too.

## 2026-11-06: A player's own spells hurt them, once they have left them (owner decision)

- **Now:** no spell spares its caster. A spell spares only the body it is cast from: the caster's own (`Spells._launch` gives every launch that body when no payload source took its place), a payload's source as before. A shot spares it until a hit test finds the shot clear of it (`ProjectileManager.exclude`), then hits it as any other; a carrier that never leaves it (a swing, a slam, a beam, a dash's pass) always spares it. Nothing else is spared: decoys, teammates and everything else are hit.
- **Gone:** the manager's skip of every target its shot's actor owns (shots, rays, sweeps, and the C++ `ShotKernel::scan` that mirrors the hit test), the unused self-hit flag (`F_SELF`, `F_SELF_LIVE`, `set_self_hit`, `is_self_live`), and every choice of `_strike_round` (`everyone`): a strike (a blast, a rain, a teleport's landing, a fallback carrier's) hits every body round it, its caster's too, and no wall hides one (owner, same day: "we'll live with rain hitting through walls"). The Teleport card deals no damage (`base_damage` 0; a form's range now starts at 0), so its landing strikes the body that lands harmlessly.
- **What changed in play:** a Return shot hits its caster on the way back, a bounced shot on its way back, a rain or a zap where its caster stands, a decoy by its own wizard's shots. Aim is unchanged: homing, chain and zap still pick other teams.
- **Why:** the owner: "A players own spells should hurt the player if hit by them. So for example return becomes dangerous. The rule from yesterday, where a spell can only hit a caster after it has left its body should still hold though." A team-fire setting was left for later ("If I need it, we will implement it later").
